# Ollin — the shipped-capability inventory

This is the per-feature inventory of everything Ollin ships today, split out of
`CLAUDE.md` so the always-loaded guidance stays small. It is the authoritative
answer to *"does Ollin already do X, and what must not be broken about it?"* —
one bullet per capability, each a terse statement of the load-bearing invariants
plus pointers to its example, snapshot, tests, and `Docs/` page.

Where each kind of fact lives:

- **Here** — what exists, and the regression-preventing invariants of each
  shipped capability (terse; a rule whose violation is a bug).
- [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) — vision, scope, and the cross-cutting policy that
  spans features (the architecture rule, conventions, sourcing & attribution,
  the shader/Metal and rendering-performance rules, the ship checklist).
- [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) — engineering internals: the mechanism,
  the history, and the measurements behind a shipped system.
- [`Docs/`](Docs/) — the user-facing API reference.
- [`ROADMAP.md`](ROADMAP.md) / [`DESIGN-NOTES.md`](DESIGN-NOTES.md) — what is
  *ahead*, and why it is shaped that way. Never a changelog.
- [`Guide/`](Guide/) — the narrative book that teaches the capability.

**When work ships it lands here**, as step (1) of the ship checklist in
`CLAUDE.md` § *Roadmap & design notes*. Keep bullets terse and route depth to
its home: explanation and history to `ARCHITECTURE.md`, user-facing API to
`Docs/`, forward plans to `ROADMAP.md` / `DESIGN-NOTES.md`.

A few load-bearing rules recur across the satellites; they are stated once in
`CLAUDE.md` § *Current state* rather than repeated per bullet (non-isolated
render-thread closures, no defaulted `resource:in:` bundle, never replacing a
GPU texture in place, satellites not depending on each other, and the
`@unchecked Sendable` producer + `OSAllocatedUnfairLock` handoff shape).

## Today (the shipped surface)

- **Lifecycle & input.** `Sketch` base class; temporal state (`frameCount` / `time` / `deltaTime` / `frameRate`); mouse (`mouseX` / `mouseY`, `mouseIsPressed`, `mousePressed()` / `mouseReleased()`) and keyboard (`key` / `keyCode` / `keyIsPressed`, `keyPressed()` / `keyReleased()`, `isKeyDown(_:)`, typed `KeyCode`). The held-flag + release-hook pair makes drag-to-paint a `draw()` poll. `Docs/Core/Sketch.md`, `Docs/Helpers/Input.md`.

- **Rendering.** `Drawer` + Metal renderer over several pipelines recorded into **call-ordered batches** (so SDF / triangle / fringe / image / glyph / 3D geometry composite front-to-back as drawn — never collapse into unordered passes): a tessellated-triangle path (libtess2 fills + 8×/4× MSAA), an instanced-SDF path, a fringe-stroke path (the high-quality stroke renderer), a textured-quad image path, and a glyph-atlas path. Gamma-correct **linear-light** compositing in a `rgba16Float` intermediate that a final present pass tone-maps + dithers to the sRGB drawable. **`blendMode(_:)`** (`.normal` / `.add` / `.subtract` / `.multiply` / `.screen` / `.lightest` / `.darkest`) is stack state carried as a `Pipeline` cache-key parameter, not a renderer-per-mode. **HDR / tone-map** — `toneMap(_:exposure:)` (`.clamp` default / `.reinhard` / `.aces`), a frame-wide mode over the float pipeline (`Docs/Drawing/HDR.md`). **Accumulation surface** — `noClear()` / `clearEachFrame()`, a persistent float canvas drawing piles onto; `background(_:)` is the in-mode wipe (`Docs/Drawing/Accumulation.md`). The **SDF path** covers a broad analytic-shape catalog (`drawPoint` / `drawCircle` / `drawEllipse` / `drawRect` / `drawOrientedBox` / circular `drawArc` / `drawTriangle` / `drawNgon` / `drawStar` / `drawRing` / `drawHeart` / `drawMoon` / … + `hollow(_:)` band mode, `strokeAlign(_:)`, area-conserving disk + outline coverage); the **fringe path** covers every stroke (`drawLine` / `drawBezier` / `drawPolyline` + polygon/shape/elliptical-arc outlines, honoring `strokeJoin` / `strokeCap` / `strokeProfile`, translucent + gradient paint); the **triangle path** covers fills (`drawShape` for concave/holed, curved via the `Path` builder + `drawCurve`; **`drawPolygon` is a fan and fills convex only** — hand it a concave outline and every dip is bridged straight across, so an area chart or any dipping profile goes through `drawShape`) and elliptical/full-arc fills. Collection sugar: `drawCircles` / `drawPoints` / `drawRects`. **Kaleidoscope symmetry** — `symmetry(_:mirrored:)` / `noSymmetry()`, stack state: every 2D draw call replicates through canvas-space fold matrices (each local rotation/reflection conjugated by the CTM at the `symmetry` call — fold 0 stays the exact identity, so the primary copy is byte-identical). **Load-bearing:** replication is per emission funnel — SDF instances and combinator groups left-multiply their per-instance `transform` (groups share one node program), the triangle/fringe/image/glyph paths range-copy canvas-space vertices via `Drawer.replicated` whose body must stay within **one batch kind** (wrap a fill section and its stroke separately; `isReplicating` passes a nested funnel through, and folds are rigid so screen-space AA/fringe widths stay exact), and `svgRecord` replicates commands so `--export-svg` folds too; 3D geometry, GPU particles, and depth scenes are untouched by design. Example `Patterns/Kaleidoscope` (declares `loopDuration`); snapshot `symmetry`; `SymmetryTests`. **Clipping** — `withClip(shape/rect/circle) { }`, the standard stencil clip (increment/decrement): the region triangulates through the fill path into a stencil-only `.clipPush` batch (CTM baked at the push), content batches carry a `clipLevel` and stencil-test `equal` to it, and the block's end appends a fullscreen-decrement `.clipPop`; nesting intersects. **Load-bearing:** a `stencil8` memoryless MSAA attachment joins a pass **only when that surface clips** (`Drawer.usesClipStencil` / `RenderTarget.needsStencil` — an unclipped frame is byte-identical), and in a stencil-carrying pass **every** pipeline key must declare `stencilFormat` (skybox and raymarch-upsample included) or Metal validation rejects the draw; never pass `setDepthStencilState(nil)` (validation rejects nil — use `noDepthState`); clipping is **per surface** (a layer opened inside a clip block starts unclipped); `background(_:)` inside a clip block re-emits the open pushes after the wipe (`reemitClipPushes`); clip edges AA at MSAA resolution (the clip shape rasterizes binary per sample); `--export-svg` emits native `<clipPath>` defs + nested `<g>` groups. Example `Shapes/Clipping`; snapshot `clip`; `ClipTests`. The load-bearing renderer rules (the vertex-buffer ring, the SDF tagged union, coverage models, the present/float pipeline) live in *Shaders & the Metal back end* + *Rendering performance* above. `Docs/Drawing/Drawing.md`.

- **Wide gamut & HDR output** (`Sources/Ollin/Color/ColorOutput.swift` + the present twin in `Renderer/ShaderEffects.metal`). **`Sketch.colorOutput`** (declared, like `canvasSize`, since the drawable is built from it once) opens the last step of the pipeline: `.standard` (8-bit sRGB, the default), `.wide` (Display P3 through an `rgba16Float` drawable), `.extended` (+ values above 1 shown as highlights, and an HDR10 video export). **`Color(displayP3:green:blue:)`** / `displayP3Components` / `isOutsideSRGB` name a color outside sRGB, stored as the *extended* sRGB components that decode back to it. **Load-bearing:** the wide/HDR present is a **twin fragment** (`ollin_present_wide_fragment`) beside the byte-for-byte-untouched `ollin_present_fragment` (the fast-math re-contraction rule), and the new `OllinPresentUniforms` fields are **appended** so the shipped fragment's reads keep their offsets — proven by a clean-HEAD worktree double record (185/185 identical); no out-of-sRGB authoring needed a shader change because the shipped `srgbToLinear` already decodes a negative component through the curve's straight segment, so the CPU encode (`linearToSrgb`, `c*12.92` below the knee) is its **exact inverse** and a P3 color survives the ordinary vertex path; `PresentEncoding` distinguishes `.linearDisplayP3` (stops at white) from `.linearDisplayP3Extended` (**starts unbounded**, so a headless render keeps the highlights an exported HDR video is for, with a live host pulling the ceiling down to the screen's reported headroom **every frame** — the system grants and withdraws it); tone-map curves and `.extended` **pull against each other** by construction (`.reinhard`/`.aces` squeeze into 0…1, which is what an HDR display does not want), so the default `.clamp` is the right one there; HDR video is PQ-encoded **on the GPU** into the same float texture and handed over as `kCVPixelFormatType_64RGBAHalf` (VideoToolbox writes 10-bit HEVC Main10 with `kVTHDRMetadataInsertionMode_Auto` supplying the mastering-display + content-light metadata), 1.0 = 203 cd/m² (BT.2408 reference white), peak 1000; **a still keeps the range too, in HEIC** (`--export frame.heic`; the path's extension picks the format, PNG unchanged and still clipping at white). `Export/GainMapExport.swift` writes an **ISO 21496-1 gain map** beside a base picture that is the frame clamped at white (what the PNG carries), returning `OllinApp.StillExport` (`peak`, `keepsHighlights`). **Load-bearing:** the map is **built here, never derived by Core Image**, whose ceiling comes from frame *statistics* and so deletes a small highlight (measured at 4x white: 0.1% of the frame → 0.99x back, 1% → 1.75x, 5% → 3.63x, 25%+ → exact) and which **ignores a declared `contentHeadroom`** despite its header saying it drives the calculation (the value is live: the tone-map filter reads it); a ready-made map passed as `.hdrGainMapImage` writes the **legacy Apple aux type**, not the ISO one, so the file is assembled with ImageIO directly; the map carries a **gain per channel** because clamping is per channel (an amber core of (4.0, 2.2, 0.72) clamps to (1, 1, 0.72): one channel for all three read green +81% / blue +300%, three read within 0.6%); the base is written at quality **1.0 (lossless)** so a still stays as faithful as the PNG; a frame at or below white carries **no map** and says so. Reconstruction is `(base + offset) * 2^(e * GainMapMax) - offset`; ImageIO re-encodes the three-channel map as 4:2:0, so cross-*channel* detail is half-resolution, the overall gain is not. `GainMapExportTests` (12; the per-channel map and the true-peak ceiling both verified red by sabotage). Spatial video stays `.standard`. `Sketch.displayHeadroom` reads what the display actually granted (1 headless). **The float drawable is *cheaper*, not dearer** (M2 release, paired runs: 1080² present 0.44 → 0.23 ms) because the 8-bit path's dither + transfer curve outcost the extra bytes, so the case against `.wide` as a default is consumer compatibility and re-record churn, never speed. Example `Rendering/ColorOutput`; `ColorOutputTests` (10, the gamut-matrix sabotage verified red); no pixel snapshot beyond the byte-identity gate (a wide frame's whole point is invisible in an 8-bit reference). Mechanism + the measurement: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Wide gamut and HDR output*. `Docs/Drawing/ColorOutput.md`; Guide Ch 16 § *Genuinely brighter: HDR output*.

- **Variable-width strokes** (`Sources/Ollin/Drawing/StrokeProfile.swift` + the expander in `Core/Drawer+Tessellation.swift`). **`strokeProfile(_:)`** / `noStrokeProfile()` is stack state shaping width along a path: the **`StrokeProfile`** value type (`.uniform` / `.taper(start:end:)` / `.ramp(from:to:)` / `.nib(angle:thinness:)` / `.values(_:)` + the closure forms) returns a **multiplier on `strokeWeight`**, read at path fraction 0…1 plus the local direction (what `.nib` uses). The fringe expander carries a **half-width per path vertex** (`Drawer.strokeHalfWidths`), so a segment quad becomes a trapezoid, exact for a linear ramp along a straight run. **Load-bearing:** width is sampled **per path vertex, not per segment**, which is *why* joins stay simple (both segments at a corner agree on the width there, so a corner is the constant-width corner problem at the local width) and why joins/caps just read their own vertex's width; a non-uniform profile **subdivides like a gradient does** (`maxLength: 12`) since the profile can only follow the points it's given; **`.uniform` is the untouched path** (`isUniform` gates the expander, the outline builder, and the vector recorder) and the coverage ramp is the shipped one either way, so a profiled and an unprofiled stroke of the same width lay down the same ink and every existing snapshot is unchanged; the **analytic SDF shapes carry one width by construction** and ignore a profile with a one-time `noteOnce`. **Vector export stays true:** `svgRecord` routes a profiled stroke to `variableStrokeOutline` (trapezoids + join wedges + caps, each wound the same way into **one nonzero-wound `Shape`**, so the union needs no boolean work), recorded as a filled path rather than a `stroke-width`; PDF replays the same commands. Example `Shapes/StrokeProfiles`; snapshot `stroke-profiles`; `StrokeProfileTests` (the family, arc-length sampling, outline winding + area, rendered taper falloff). `Docs/Drawing/Drawing.md#strokeProfile`.

- **Stroke dynamics** (`Sources/Ollin/Drawing/StrokeDynamics.swift` + `StrokeMark.swift`). The width-profile sibling driven by *how a mark is made* rather than where you are along it: a **`StrokeMark`** records the pointer (`record(_:dt:pressure:)`, sugar `Sketch.record(into:)`) measuring speed and press force, runs both through a **`StrokeDynamics`** (one **`StrokeResponse`** per axis over a **`StrokeInput`** of speed/pressure/direction/distance), and keeps the resulting width + opacity *at* each point; `drawMark(_:)` strokes it. **Load-bearing:** a profile can't express this because a mark in progress has no length to take a fraction of, and a recorded hand's points are **not evenly spaced** (dense where it slowed), so `.values(_:)` genuinely doesn't fit and `StrokeProfile.values(_:at:)` (values at *given* fractions) is the general form `drawMark` builds; speed is measured over **real elapsed time** with skipped sub-`minimumSpacing` frames **banking** their dt (frame-rate independence, pinned by `recordingIsFrameRateIndependent`), smoothed by a `OneEuroFilter` per axis fed a **scaled** signal (a px/s speed runs in the hundreds, and the filter's `beta` is in the signal's units); the **two axes are separate and each defaults to `.unchanged`** (a shared preset with per-axis defaults silently drove both, the bug the first probe caught); the first point is **held until a second gives it a heading**; the ambient `strokeProfile` **multiplies** with the mark's widths; the mark's opacity rides the existing **per-vertex color alpha** via an internal `strokeOpacityShape` (`.uniform` default → every other stroke byte-identical, and a `.uniform` mark is byte-identical to `drawPolyline`). **Pressure is plumbed** through the one platform-neutral input surface (`Sketch.pressure` / `pressureIsAvailable`, capability latching on the first press), so it reaches standalone + gallery + OllinLive — but **not via `pressureChange`**: the SwiftUI hosting layer consumes `.pressure` events before responder dispatch *and* its recognizers override the view's `.primaryGeneric` with deep-click (reads ~0 across the whole normal force range), so `OllinMTKView` feeds a **view-owned local `.pressure` event monitor** and `mouseDown` re-claims the stream with `pressureConfiguration?.set()`; **never read `associatedEventsMask` on a `.pressure` event** (the access raises and AppKit swallows the raise, silently dropping the report — a pressure event is itself the capability proof), and mouse/drag events from a pressure-capable device **seed zero, then go quiet once the stream is live** (their `pressure` field is the legacy constant 1). Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Trackpad pressure delivery*. **Vector export** keeps width exactly (the profiled filled-outline path) and **flattens opacity to its length-weighted average** with a `noteOnce` (one fill, one alpha per path; free for the one-pen plotter case). Example `Shapes/Brushwork` (class renamed off `StrokeDynamics` to dodge the `SlitScan` shadowing trap); `StrokeDynamicsTests` (mapping, measurement, arc-fraction placement, three rendered probes, no pixel snapshot since dynamics is interactive). `Docs/Drawing/Marks.md`.

- **Brushes** (`Sources/Ollin/Drawing/Brush.swift` + `Core/Drawer+Brush.swift`). The third mark axis beside the width profile and the recorded mark: **`strokeBrush(_:)`** / `noStrokeBrush()` is stack state that **replaces the ribbon with a row of stamps**. The **`Brush`** value type carries a `tip` (`.circle` / `.square` / `.shape(Shape)` / `.image(Image)`), `spacing`, `sizeJitter`, `angle` (`.followPath` / `.fixed` / `.random`), `angleJitter`, `scatter`, `count`, `opacityJitter`, `seed`, plus the `.round` / `.chisel()` / `.spray()` / `.scatter()` presets; size comes from **`strokeWeight`** and color from **`stroke`**, so a brush is texture only. **Load-bearing:** every distance is a **fraction of the stamp's own size**, which is what keeps a brush's texture constant across weights (pinned by `spacingScalesWithTheStampNotTheCanvas`); stamps are placed **by arc length, not per vertex** (a recorded mark's points bunch where the hand slowed, and a brush must not bunch with them); randomness is a **pure function of stamp index + `seed`** via a private `SplitMix`, **never the sketch's `rng`** (the halton/sobol rule), so adding a brush cannot shift a sketch's other rolls; the ambient `strokeProfile` / `strokeOpacityShape` are read per stamp and **multiply**, and the step is taken at the *local* size so a tapering mark crowds rather than going gappy; a stamp draws through the **ordinary draw methods** (`drawCircle` / `drawOrientedBox` / `drawShape` / `drawImage`) with fill/stroke/brush swapped and restored, so vector export, symmetry, clipping, and retained batches all work with no knowledge of brushes (and recursion is impossible); **`strokedAsBrush(_:closed:)` is called ahead of every stroke entry point's `svgRecorder` short-circuit** (`drawLine` / `drawBezier` / `drawPolyline` / `drawMark` / `drawArc`, and `drawShape` / `drawPolygon` which re-enter fill-only then stamp) or the vector path would silently export the bare path instead of the stamps — the `appendFringeStroke` hook is the catch-all for internal callers; a brushed `drawMark` **keeps** its varying opacity in vector export (each stamp is its own shape) where the ribbon must flatten it; opacity over a **gradient** stroke is the one thing a brush cannot vary (it would mean rewriting the ramp) and notes once, as do the analytic SDF outlines (the `strokeProfile` precedent). `Brush` is **not `Sendable`/`Equatable`** (an `Image` tip is neither). Example `Shapes/Brushes`; snapshot `brushes`; `BrushTests`. `Docs/Drawing/Marks.md#brushes`.

- **Instanced meshes** (`drawMesh(_:instances:)` + `MeshInstance` in `Sources/Ollin/3D/MeshInstance.swift`; the `.meshInstanced` recorder in `Drawer.swift`, the encode arm in `MetalRenderer+Effects.swift`, the shadow legs in `MetalRenderer+Targets.swift`, `ollin_mesh_instanced_*` in `Shader3D.metal`). One mesh, many placements, ONE draw: the base mesh expands once per call into its own frame array/ring (**LOCAL space, model NOT baked** — the per-mesh path's expensive per-copy re-bake is the thing this removes) and each copy is an `OllinMeshInstance` (`OllinShaderTypes.h`: model float4x4 + tint, stride 80) applied per vertex on the GPU; the `[MeshInstance]` form composes the CTM per copy at record time (**T·Rx·Ry·Rz·S, pinned bit-exact against the equivalent translate/rotateX/Y/Z/scale sequence**), the GPU-resident form (`ComputeBuffer<OllinMeshInstance>`, riding the batch's `particleBuffer` like the point-cloud path) is absolute world space by design (the kernel owns placement). **Load-bearing:** the instanced vertex emits the solid path's own `MeshOut` and reuses `ollin_mesh_fragment`, with the whole lit binding set shared through the `bindLitMeshFragment` local function in the encode loop (extracted from the `.mesh3D` arm — the two arms must never drift), so copies take fill/finish (PBR included), lights, shadows received, IBL, GI, and fog identically (pinned within tolerance by the A/B render test); the normal transform is the **adjugate of the model's linear part, computed in-shader** (exact under non-uniform scale after normalization) — deliberately no normal matrix in the struct, so a compute kernel fills only model + color; batches carry **explicit vertex/instance start + count** (never next-batch run-length arithmetic — no other batch creator records these starts); copies **cast into the 2D shadow map** (`.meshInstancedShadow`) **and the point cube** (instanceCount = copies × 6, face = iid % 6, copy = iid / 6; the layered-topology gate in `makeShadowPipeline` keys on `pointShadowOp != 0` because *both* point-shadow vertices write `render_target_array_index`), and a frame whose only casters are instanced still renders its maps (the shadow-pass guard takes instanced casters as geometry); the v1 exclusions are **documented, never silent**: textures/wireframe/matcap fall back to the solid look with a one-time note, copies are not in the RT acceleration structure (no ray-traced shadows/reflections *of* them; they still *receive*, and on an RT device a point light's instanced cast is missing since the caster set is traced — the ICB slice's instance-accel work closes this), not in the screen-space pre-passes (contact shadows / SSAO normals / scatter mask) or the mover velocity pass, refused inside `makeBatch` (the retained-mesh shadow rule), spatial export records the array form per copy and refuses the GPU form with a note, SVG skips meshes as ever. **Measured (M2, 1080², release, lit + shadowed 12,398-copy field):** CPU record 11.99 ms/frame per-copy loop vs 0.23 ms instanced (53×); GPU identical (~56.6 ms in that stress scene) — the win is CPU elimination, the roadmap's own framing. Example `Rendering/InstancedMesh` (A/B knob vs the per-copy loop); snapshot `instanced-mesh`; `MeshInstanceTests` + `MeshInstanceRenderTests` (the A/B equivalence + the pipeline-build test that keeps the RT-dev-machine-unreachable cube leg compiling) + `MeshInstanceBenchmarkTests` (`Scripts/benchmark.sh instancing`). Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Instanced meshes and mesh fields*. `Docs/3D/Instancing.md`; Guide Ch 22 § *Ten thousand of the same thing*.
- **Mesh fields (GPU-culled worlds)** (`Sources/Ollin/3D/MeshField.swift`; `encodeMeshFieldCulling` + the `.meshField` arm in `MetalRenderer+Effects.swift`; the field shadow legs in `+Targets.swift`; `ollin_mesh_field_*` in `Shader3D.metal`). A retained world of many distinct meshes + copies drawn by ONE `drawMeshField(_:)` per frame: `place(_:at:)` expands each mesh once into a concatenated base-vertex buffer and appends its copies (`OllinMeshInstance`) plus an `OllinFieldEntry` (local bounding sphere, vertex run, copy run, compact region — all in `OllinShaderTypes.h`); per frame a **GPU cull kernel** (one thread per copy, sphere-vs-frustum through the copy's matrix × the draw-time field matrix, Gribb-Hartmann planes from the *unjittered* view-projection) atomically compacts survivors per entry, and an **encode kernel** writes one `MTLDrawPrimitivesIndirectArguments` per entry with **`baseInstance` = the entry's compact offset**, so `[[instance_id]]` lands directly on that entry's slice of the compacted-index buffer and the vertex fetches `instances[compacted[iid]]` with zero per-draw bindings; the render arm issues one *indirect draw per entry* (CPU cost per KIND, never per copy). **Load-bearing:** the draws are GPU-authored but deliberately NOT an `MTLIndirectCommandBuffer` — **Metal rejects the RT-compiled lit fragment inside an ICB pipeline** ("Fragment shader cannot be used with indirect command buffers"), and indirect draws carry the identical payload with no fragment restriction, so copies keep the FULL solid shading (the shared `bindLitMeshFragment` set) on every device; **culling must never change the picture** (a culled copy is off-screen by definition; `cullingChangesNothingInThePicture` pins it, and `field.cullingEnabled = false` is the public A/B switch); the cull runs at drive level (`encodeMeshFieldCulling`, after `encodeCompute` in every depth-carrying drive) and **the draw arm requires `depthFormat != nil`**, which doubles as the stale-arguments gate (the 2D-only accumulating drives never cull, so they never draw a field); surface color bakes at `place` (mesh base color × per-vertex colors × per-copy tint — the draw-time `fill` deliberately does not tint a field); **shadows cast UNCULLED** from the field's own retained buffers (`ollin_mesh_field_shadow_vertex` + the cube sibling with `iid % 6` face routing; a caster behind the camera still shadows the view), with the same RT-device point-caster exclusion instanced meshes carry; one draw per field per frame (a second is noteOnce'd — the cull results live on the field); spatial export walks the CPU-kept placements per copy; `place` after realization bumps `generation` and re-uploads. The 2D shadow pass culls against the **light's own frustum** (exact: anything culled would have been clipped by the map's rasterizer; the cube pass stays uncculled, omnidirectional) — and the shadow-cull dispatch must **rebind the instance buffer at index 0** (the encode dispatch just repointed it at the draw-arguments buffer; missing that rebind culled against garbage matrices and silently dropped real shadows, found by the culled-vs-unculled A/B). **Measured (M2, 1080², release, the example's 240k-copy plain, lit + shadows + fog):** GPU 18.5 ms culled vs 50.8 ms unculled (**2.7×**), CPU record ~0 ms (`Scripts/benchmark.sh field`). Example `Rendering/MeshField` (240k solids, fly-through, culling knob); snapshot `mesh-field`; `MeshFieldTests` + `MeshFieldRenderTests` (field == equivalent instanced draws; culling == no picture change; the RT-unreachable cube shadow leg compiled explicitly) + `MeshFieldBenchmarkTests`. Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Instanced meshes and mesh fields*. `Docs/3D/Instancing.md` § *A field that culls itself*; Guide Ch 22 § *A world the camera trims*.
- **Strand fields (in-draw grass)** (`Sources/Ollin/3D/StrandField.swift`; `ShaderStrands.metal`, the first mesh-pipeline segment; `makeMeshPipeline` + the `.strands` arm in the renderer). **`drawStrands(_ field: StrandField)`** grows grass-like blades ENTIRELY inside the draw call through a Metal 3 mesh pipeline (`MTLMeshRenderPipelineDescriptor`, built from the same runtime-compiled library — the `[[object]]`/`[[mesh]]` stages compile through `makeLibrary(source:)`, `<metal_mesh>` included by `ShaderCore`): no vertex, index, or instance buffer exists anywhere; every blade's root, height, lean, bend, sway phase, and tint derive from hashes of its index (the shader library's `hash22`/`hash33`), so a `StrandField` is a plain **value** with zero GPU state to invalidate. **Load-bearing:** the object stage runs one threadgroup per tile (frustum-culls the tile's AABB — `cullingEnabled` is the pinned A/B: skipping tiles must not change a pixel — and grades the tile's per-blade segment count 4→1 between `detailNear`/`detailFar`; `levelOfDetailEnabled` is that dial's A/B), and the mesh stage emits bundles of `OLLIN_STRAND_BUNDLE` (24) blades, one thread per blade, **into the solid path's own `MeshOut`**, so blades shade through `ollin_mesh_fragment` (full lit set via `bindLitMeshFragment`: lights, shadows RECEIVED, IBL, GI, fog, the current finish) — **a mesh pipeline pairs fine with the RT-compiled lit fragment** (probed; unlike ICBs, which reject it); mesh capacities are sized in `OllinShaderTypes.h` from the bundle and `OLLIN_STRAND_MAX_SEGMENTS` (240 ≤ 256 verts, 192 ≤ 512 prims — change either, re-check both); a dead mesh thread still writes degenerate zeros so the fixed layout stays initialized, and thread 0 alone sets `set_primitive_count`; sway rides the sketch clock (`computeUniforms.time`), so exports reproduce and the same frame renders **byte-identically** (pinned); the blades exist only in-draw, so they **cast no shadows** (they receive), the **spatial exporter refuses with a note**, SVG skips, `makeBatch` refuses. **The honest pitch is architectural, per the platform's own guidance** (mesh shaders earn their place only where draws cannot express the geometry — a blade's per-length bend is exactly that): **measured (M2, 1080², release, the 511,584-blade meadow, lit + shadows + fog): ~22.5 ms GPU, zero geometry memory, zero per-frame CPU; distance grading saves a further ~15% (1.15×) over full detail** (`Scripts/benchmark.sh strands`). Example `Rendering/Grassland` (detail knob); snapshot `strands`; `StrandFieldTests` + `StrandFieldRenderTests` (pipeline build, byte-exact determinism, tile-cull invariance) + `StrandBenchmarkTests`. `Docs/3D/Strands.md`; Guide Ch 22 § *Grass that was never built*.
- **Retained batches** (`Sources/Ollin/Drawing/Batch.swift` + `encodeRetained` in `MetalRenderer+Effects.swift`). **`makeBatch { }`** records a drawing once into a **`Batch`** (persistent, made in `setup()` and held — the `Feedback` shape) and **`drawBatch(_:)`** replays it from the handle's own once-written per-device `MTLBuffer`s — the escape from per-frame re-recording for *static heavy* content (measured M2 1080² release: 150k circles ~13.5 ms/frame per-frame vs ~0.003 ms replayed; example knob flips the two live). **Load-bearing:** recording is an **array swap** on the `Drawer` (per-frame arrays + batch list + gradient-row table), so recorded runs index the handle's arrays and baked gradient rows are **handle-relative** — the replay binds the batch's **own strip texture**, never the frame's; the body runs style-scoped (`pushState`/`popState`) from an **identity CTM** with the target/clip stacks swapped out, and the `.retained` reference batch supplies the draw-time context (CTM + target + clip level + `depth(at:)`); the draw-time CTM rides **flag-gated `Uniforms` fields** every 2D vertex shader applies to its *output position only* (flag 0 outside a replay → ordinary paths byte-identical, snapshot suite unchanged; identity replay byte-identical to the per-frame drawing, pinned by `BatchRenderTests`; SDF analytic AA stays exact under any replay transform because shape-local interpolants never change); **unsupported content is gated at its funnel** with a one-time note (meshes + 3D fields would silently lose shadows/reflections; particles; layer blocks; clipping; `background`; nesting) — never dropped after recording, which would corrupt the next-run count derivation; point clouds record camera-less and replay through the frame's camera (the 2D replay CTM doesn't touch them); **draw-time symmetry does not fold a replay** (record folds inside the block); vector export flips **`OllinApp.isVectorExporting` for the whole `recordVectorFrame` drive** (setup + warmup included) so `makeBatch` captures `SVGCommand`s instead and `drawBatch` splices them under the composed CTM — SVG/PDF see every stamp. Example `Rendering/RetainedBatch`; snapshot `retained-batch`; `BatchTests`/`BatchRenderTests`. Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Retained batches*. `Docs/Drawing/Batches.md`.

- **SDF combinators** (`Sources/Ollin/Drawing/SDF.swift` + `Renderer/ShaderCombinator.metal`; 3D in `Sources/Ollin/Drawing/SDF3D.swift` + `Renderer/ShaderRaymarch.metal`). Compose signed-distance *fields* so shapes **merge** instead of stack — the field-combining axis the per-instance SDF path can't express. The typed **`SDF`** value type (leaf region shapes + `.union`/`.smoothUnion(_:k:)`/`.subtract`/`.smoothSubtract`/`.intersect`/`.smoothIntersect`/`.morph`, the hg_sdf joint + detailing ops (the chamfer/stairs/columns trios + `engrave`/`groove`/`tongue`/`pipe` — crisp nearer-side/body color; the detailing ops are value-type-only (the morph precedent), columns gets block forms; they assume frankly-crossing surfaces — near-parallel faces within the radius echo the pattern, the documented envelope), and `.rounded`/`.onion`/`.at`/`.rotated`/`.scaled`/`.stretched`/`.colored`/`.mirrored`/`.repeated`/`.repeatedRadially`) draws via `drawSDF(_:)` honoring the current fill/stroke/CTM, the stroke tracing the *merged* outline; the scoped **block forms** (`smoothUnion(k:){ … }` + hard/mirror/repeat variants, and the stateful **`sculpt { }`** block whose `add()`/`carve()`/`blend(_:)` verbs mutate per-child mode/melt mid-block — built for the live-coding loop) capture bare region draws at `Drawer.appendSDF` as leaves, reaching **every** fillable region shape. Rendering is an **"SDF VM"**: the tree flattens (CPU) to a flat `SDFNode` program (own `SDFGroupInstance` + `.sdfGroup` pipeline *beside* the per-instance `SDFInstance` path, which is full at 144) walked per pixel with two fixed-depth stacks (cap 16); smooth ops **also lerp the operands' colors** (the melt look). **Load-bearing invariants:** **leaves carry no transform** (all positioning is XFORM nodes → method-chain order exact: `a.at(p).repeated()` tiles the moved shape, `a.repeated().at(p)` shifts the tiling); the leaf distance **`ollin_sdf_distance`** is **kept in sync by hand** with `ollin_sdf_fragment`'s switch (a new region shape goes in both; the per-shape switch stays untouched so single-shape snapshots are byte-identical); **polar/radial repeat is the shared sel-5 XFORM** (2D fold around origin, 3D around an `axis`), the **single-evaluation** kind (wedge-symmetric content exact, else a seam); **region shapes only** (lines/open-arcs/Béziers skipped with a one-time log); a field past the caps (depth 16, ~256 nodes) is logged + skipped, never mis-drawn; the buffers thread all 9 `encode` sites. **2D gradient paint** samples in **field space** (`in.field`; along-path falls back to solid; a solid-leaf field is byte-identical). **Per-axis sizing:** `stretched(x:y:[z:])` (iq `opElongate`, an **exact** SDF, the preferred tool) vs `scaled(x:y:[z:])` (true non-uniform, only a **conservative bound** — rescales by the **min** scale component for a safe Lipschitz march, so blends distort under strong anisotropy). The **raymarched 3D sibling** — **`SDF3D`** + **`drawSDF3D(_:)`**: the same ops over the 3D leaves (sphere/box/roundBox/torus/capsule/cylinder/cone/octahedron/ellipsoid/plane + line-between-points/hexPrism/pyramid/cappedTorus/link) plus the sculpting distortions `.twisted`/`.bent` and `.displaced`/`.roughened` (every distortion/displacement is a distance *bound*, so flatten attaches a conservative Lipschitz rescale that keeps the march hole-free), **sphere-traced** through the active camera and **depth-composited with the rasterized meshes** (a parallel path: own `SDFNode3D` + `.raymarch` pipeline). **Load-bearing:** the march runs in **world space** (the 4-tap gradient normal + written `[[depth(any)]]` come out world-space with no normal matrix); one **fullscreen triangle per field** bounded by its world AABB (a miss bails O(1)); shading **reuses `meshLitColor` unchanged**, **honors `material(_:)` per batch** (`ensureSDF3DBatch` records the finish — the generic `ensureBatch` records none, the bug that left field materials silently ignored) and **adds the same IBL ambient the mesh fragments add** (incl. the traced-reflection swap under `rtReflections`; gated on `iblEnabled` → byte-identical with no environment; `resolveFieldLighting` carries the same setup so the half-res tiers light identically). The infinite **plane** is the one *unbounded* leaf (flattener marks the field `unbounded`, marches the near..far span; value-type-only). The block form reaches 3D too — SDF-able mesh builders capture leaves via `Drawer.drawMeshPrimitive`, positioned by the model matrix decomposed to translate+rotate+uniform-scale (the only similarity an SDF respects); a non-primitive `Mesh` is ignored (one-time log). **Screen-space gradient paint** (3D, by each hit's projected screen position); **analytic silhouette AA** (the ray's closest approach vs the pixel cone). **Shadows** (gated on `castShadows()`, byte-identical off): self-shadows via iq's **standard** `min(k·h/t)` penumbra — *not* the previous-step refinement, which divides by zero on a receding ray and false-shadows lit faces — plus the full **field↔mesh cast/receive matrix across every light type**; the per-receiver-pixel point-light cast is trimmed by a conservative AABB early-out + a half-res field-shadow pass. **Quality dial** (`raymarchQuality`/`raymarchResolution`): a field's cost is **bound to pixel count, not step count**, so the dial scales internal **resolution** via a reduced-res pre-pass (point-sampled depth keeps the silhouette crisp) whose fraction is a **coverage-adaptive budget, not a fixed downscale** (`min(1, fraction/√coverage)` — a dollied-out field traces at full res, the zoom-out-blur fix); **the coverage estimate must stay honest or the blur returns**: a combine's AABB grows by each op's *provable* outward reach (smooth/chamfer/stairs `k/2`, columns/pipe/tongue `k`, subtract/intersect/morph **zero** — morph's `k` is a blend fraction, not a length), never a blanket `k`, which compounds per nested op until a dollied-out field reads near-screen-filling (a real re-blur bug), and per box the estimate takes the smaller of the corners' clipped NDC rect and their convex-hull area; the automatic tier is **live-preview only** (export resolves `.detail`, full-res → default-tier snapshots byte-identical; an *explicit* fraction/tier is honored on export, adaptively); rides the shared **`RenderQuality`** model with the other three quality knobs. `ollin_sdf3d_eval`'s switch stays in sync with `SDF3DShape`. Examples `Shapes/Combinators*` (2D) + the `3D/Raymarched*` set; snapshots `sdf-combinators` + its `-gradient`/`-stretch`/`-joinery`/`-detailing`/`-sculpt` and `-3d*` variants. Full mechanism, rationale, and the field-shadow matrix: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *SDF combinators*. `Docs/Drawing/Combinators.md`.

- **User-supplied shaders** (`Sources/Ollin/Effects/Shader.swift` + the wrapper/compile/error path in `MetalRenderer`). A sketch writes its own fragment shader and runs it through the effect graph. The contract is **Ollin-native, not raw Metal**: define `float4 shade(float2 uv, ShaderInfo info)` (uv 0…1 top-left, return straight sRGB — input layers are read back as straight sRGB too) and Ollin generates the surrounding fragment + vertex. The **`Shader`** value type (Sendable) carries inline-`String` or `resource:in:` source, `params` (up to 32 floats, read via `param(info, i)`), and a **`Modules`** opt-in set; routing **reuses the effect-graph enums** — a `.shader(Shader)` case on `Generator`/`Filter`/`Combine.Kind`, so input count *is* the variant (`generate(.shader(s))` 0, `layer.filtered(.shader(s))` 1, `a.combined(with: b, .shader(s))` 2) — with no new render-graph plumbing. Each shader compiles **once per composed-source hash** (its own `makeLibrary(source:)`; failed compiles are cached too, so a broken shader doesn't retry every frame; all caches clear on a framework-shader reload). **Friendly errors are the headline:** `Shader.init` captures its call site and the wrapper emits a `#line` directive, so Metal diagnostics land at the **real, IDE-clickable** sketch line (`ShaderErrorTests` pins the off-by-one semantics for no-newline literals); errors surface via stderr (once per hash) *and* the pull-model `onUserShaderError` channel into the OllinLive overlay — a shader error and a Swift compile error never clear each other. The **`OllinShaderLib`** helper segment (color/hash/noise/sdf/domain + `palette`/OKLab + the once-compute-only helpers) is **one source of truth used three ways**: the framework's own segments build on it, it splices into every user shader, and `composeComputeSource` splices it into every compute kernel (one helper set, `hashNM` names, everywhere); the 2D `sd*` distance catalog lives in its `sdf` section, shared with `ShaderShapes`; `using: [.noise, .sdf]` keeps only marked sections — a *compile-time* lever, default `.all`. **Hot-reload** covers both forms in OllinLive: an inline shader reloads with the sketch; a `.metal` *resource* shader reloads on its own with **no swiftc pass** (`Shader(resource:in:)` stores the bundle-resolved path; a watched-`.metal` change routes to `SketchRunner.invalidateUserShaders()`); a framework-segment `.metal` still routes to the full library reload (dispatch by path). Examples `Shaders/HelloShader` (a Toby-Howard-homage plasma generator) / `ShaderFilter` / `ShaderBlend` / `ShaderFile` (the hot-reload demo); snapshot `user-shader`. Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *User-supplied shaders*. `Docs/Shaders/Shaders.md` + the per-function `Docs/Shaders/ShaderLibrary.md`.

- **Visual chains** (`Sources/Ollin/Effects/Visual.swift` + the `visual` section of `OllinShaderLib.metal`). The fluent composition tier over the effect substrate: **`Visual`** is a value type (struct over an `indirect enum Node`, the `SDF` shape) recording a chain of per-pixel ops in five families — sources (`.oscillator`/`.noise`/`.voronoi`/`.shape`/`.gradient`/`.solid`/`.layer`), coordinate warps (`.rotated`/`.scaled`/`.pixelated`/`.repeated`/`.kaleidoscope`/`.scrolled`), color adjustments (`.brightness`/`.contrast`/`.saturation`/`.inverted`/`.posterized`/`.thresholded`/`.luma`/`.hueShifted`/`.tinted`/`.colorCycled`/`.channel`), combines (`.blended(with:_:amount:)` over the typed `BlendMode`, `.mixed`/`.differenced`/`.masked`), and modulations (driver overloads of the warps: `.displaced(by:)`, `.rotated(by:)`, `.scaled(by:)`, `.pixelated(by:)`, `.kaleidoscope(by:)` — one chain's color perturbs another's sampling coordinate). `compile()` walks the tree once and emits a `shade(uv, info)` body over `ollin_vis_*` library functions; the whole chain (drivers included) is **one fragment shader, one pass**, run through the user-shader path (`Shader` + the compile-once source-hash cache). **Load-bearing:** the generated source depends on the chain's *structure only* — every numeric argument becomes a params-buffer slot in traversal order (`VisualTests` pins this), so a chain rebuilt each `draw()` hits the cached pipeline and animated values/`@Param` knobs never recompile (structural selectors — blend mode, channel — are spelled as literals *by design*); the params buffer is **16 rows / 64 floats** (`OLLIN_SHADER_PARAM_ROWS`, raised from 8 — `paddedParams` and the wrapper both read the macro), overflow bakes literals with a one-time note; the codegen's fresh-variable counter **starts at 1** (`st0` is the root the body declares — 0 collides); `.layer` leaves dedupe by target identity and cap at the wrapper's **2 textures** (routing by count: 0 → `generate`, 1 → `.filtered`, 2 → `.combined`; extras sample black + one-time note); `.layer(_ feedback:)` reads the previous frame (video feedback); `BlendMode.visualIndex` is kept in step with `ollin_vis_blend`'s switch; ops aspect-correct *inside* the function (coordinates stay 0…1 so `fract` wraps compose), colors are straight sRGB per the user-shader contract, and `.noise` is signed so displacement wobbles about zero; `Modules.visual` splices the ops into every user shader (dependency `visual → hash+noise` in `filterLibModules`). Example `Shaders/VisualSynth`; snapshot `visual-chain` (all five families + a layer read); `Docs/Shaders/Visuals.md` + the module's function table in `Docs/Shaders/ShaderLibrary.md`.

- **Layered effects** (the substrate; `Sources/Ollin/Effects/`). Draw into off-screen layers, filter them on the GPU, composite back with blend modes (the OPENRNDR `RenderTarget`/`Filter`/`compose` model): `renderTarget(scale:)` → an off-screen `rgba16Float` `RenderTarget`; `withTarget(_:) {}` redirects drawing into it (scoped like `withState`; `background(_:)` inside clears *that* layer); `target.image` composites it back; `target.filtered(_:)` → a new layer; `postProcess(_:)` filters the whole frame. **`Filter`** is a ~55-entry catalog in six families — blur/glow, color/tone, stylize/optical, retro, uv-warp distortion, and the **design filters** (`.flutedGlass`/`.water`/`.paperTexture`/`.melt` transform the layer; `.liquidMetal`/`.heatmap`/`.gemSmoke` read its **alpha shape** — draw a shape, filter it); **`.melt`** is the luminance melt (one displacement vector both warps the field's domain and shifts the layer sample — keep them shared, that coupling *is* the recipe; sampled brightness mixes into the field before the 4-stop sRGB ramp; the sway sines are non-commensurate, so it never perfect-loops — example `Images/LuminanceMelt`, snapshot `luminance-melt`); full per-filter list in `Docs/Drawing/Effects.md`; new built-ins are written from the published technique, credited in `ATTRIBUTION.md`, never in `.swift`. **`Generator`** + `generate(_:)` fills a procedural pattern into a target: the four basics (`.noise` takes a `warp:` domain-warp knob — 0 skips the warp path so it stays byte-identical), the **`.cellular`** Worley source (`.cells`/`.borders`/`.mosaic` styles; feature points wander sinusoidally on hashed orbits, so `phase` is periodic over 2π and a whole lap loops — example `Effects/Cellular`, snapshot `noise-toolkit`), the **design-pattern set** (cross-read against Paper Shaders, written from the canonical techniques, per-technique credits in `ATTRIBUTION.md`), the **pattern fields**, and the **escape-time fractals** plus **`.orbitTrap`** (the same iteration colored by the orbit's closest pass to a point/cross/circle/square trap through an exp `glow` ramp, the trap turned by `angle`; escaped and trapped pixels color alike, the escape test only ends the orbit; example `Effects/OrbitTraps`, snapshot `orbit-trap`); `generate(_:width:height:)` fills an explicit-size layer so patterns compose per-layer instead of squashed full-canvas fills (the Shader and Visual `generate` forms have the same overloads). **Feedback** — `feedback(scale:)` → a *persistent* `Feedback` layer (made once in `setup()` and held — its identity carries the ping-pong state across frames) + `withFeedback(_:) { prev in … }`. **Simulation fields** — `simField(_:scale:)` → a persistent `SimField` running a `Sim` (`.reactionDiffusion(feed:kill:)` Gray-Scott / `.gameOfLife` Conway / `.lenia(...)` continuous CA / `.ripples(speed:damping:)` wave-equation pool / `.sandpile(pour:topplings:)` Bak-Tang-Wiesenfeld / `.fluid(...)` real-time fluid / `.multiScaleTuring(scales:seed:)` McCabe / `.cyclic(...)` Griffeath's cyclic CA / `.excitable(...)` Greenberg-Hastings / `.briansBrain()` Silverman / `.hodgepodge(...)` Gerhardt-Schuster's oscillating-reaction machine) that you seed/force by drawing into it (`withField(_:_:)`, `force:`); `SimField.image` is the raw state, `.filtered(_:)` recolors it through the `Filter` catalog, and **reading either registers the field so it steps** (`Drawer.ensureFieldSteps`) — a sim only evolves while its layer is one of the frame's render targets, which used to mean a `withField` block was mandatory, and a self-organizing sim that needs no seeding would otherwise sit frozen and read black with no hint why (an empty-block wart the Turing work surfaced; existing sketches register via `withField` first, so target order is unchanged). **Modulated regimes** — `.reactionDiffusion(feed:kill:toFeed:toKill:)` + `SimField.modulation`: a layer's brightness lerps feed/kill per texel (black → the first pair, white → the `to` pair), so one continuous field wears different regimes in different places and the pattern crosses the boundary instead of seaming (a camera matte as the map is the `Vision/TuringMirror` example; snapshot `effects-simfield-modulated`). Load-bearing: the modulated step is a **separate fragment selected only when the map layer was drawn this frame** (membership in the frame's render targets, which resolve before the sim passes) — the plain path stays byte-identical, an undrawn map falls back to the uniform black-end field rather than sampling stale, and a `filtered(_:)` output can't be a map (filters resolve after the sims). Params row z/w carry the `to` pair; x/y stay the plain pair the unmodulated kernel reads. **Depth-capturing targets** — a target a 3D scene is drawn into resolves a sampleable depth buffer; `target.depth` is a gray layer (0 near … 1 far) that feeds `.defocus` (set the camera `near`/`far` to bracket the scene); two-tier opt-in so *2D never pays*. **Compose DSL** (`Effects/Compose.swift`) — `compose { layer { … }.post(_:).blend(_:).scale(_:) … }`, composited bottom-to-top in declared order; pure sugar over the substrate. **Multi-input combine** (`Effects/Combine.swift`) — `base.combined(with: aux, op)` with six ops: `.mask` / `.displace` / `.mix` / `.defocus` (near/far-separated bokeh-gather depth of field; artifact history in [[ollin-depth-of-field-gather-artifacts]]) / `.ambientOcclusion` (SSAO over depth + the mesh-normal G-buffer) / `.screenSpaceReflections` (SSR: trace → resolve → temporal → composite); `aside { }` + `.masked(by:)` / `.displaced(by:amount:)` / `.mixed(with:amount:)` / `.defocused(by:)` is the combine's `compose` sugar. **Load-bearing invariants (terse — mechanism and why in [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Layered-effects substrate* + § *Screen-space combine effects*):** the `Drawer` stays a pure recorder and the renderer resolves the deferred graph at `render()`/`image(of:)` time, entirely GPU-resident (layers are attachments, filter inputs sampler binds, never a CPU round-trip), premultiplied linear, the no-targets path byte-identical; every `GeometryBatch` begin snapshots **all** the `*Start` offsets (omitting one zeroes a *preceding* batch's count — the `SceneDefocus` bug); `withTarget`/`withFeedback`/`withField` push/pop the drawing state around the block (a `blendMode` set inside a layer block must never leak to the canvas — was a real bug); design-pattern fragments animate by an explicit `phase` param (deterministic export, no hidden clock) and blend palettes **internally in sRGB** (mixed in linear they read washed-out pastel — a real bug); `Feedback.filtered(_:)` mirrors `SimField.filtered(_:)`; **`Sim.params` is an array of `SIMD4` rows** the renderer binds after the texel row (a new sim never widens the `runSimulation` call site, it returns more rows), **`SimField.sim` is a public var** re-read every frame (live growth knobs; the evolved state carries), and **Lenia** is the paper-faithful continuous CA (exponential kernel core in up to 3 β-rings, **normalized in-loop by the summed weight**, gaussian growth, clipped dt integration; Orbium defaults R=13/T=10/μ=0.15/σ=0.015) whose dying / labyrinth / rings / maze looks are all real regimes — **seed it densely** (sparse mass starves, the documented envelope, not a bug); **`.ripples`** is the height/velocity interactive-water kernel (state h in r, v in g, both signed; 6 substeps; clamped taps, soft absorbing border rim) whose **per-sim inject is load-bearing**: `Sim.injectFragment` routes it to `ollin_sim_inject_height`, which *adds* the drawn mark's premultiplied luminance to height only — the generic replace-by-alpha inject pins the surface and writes color into the velocity channel (the exact bug that parked the 2026-07-07 attempt, see [[ollin-ripples-sim-parked]]); the **coupling gain `speed` must stay under 2** (clamped to 1, default 0.5): 2 is the CFL boundary where the Nyquist checkerboard sits on a repeated λ=−1 root and grows linearly, settling ~22× the ring amplitude under any damping and shading into glitter — substeps went 3→6 so the ring pace is unchanged (wave speed ∝ √gain); soft radial-gradient dabs are the drop contract (hard discs legitimately ring at every frequency), and the raw `image` is a debug view (shade with `.relight`); **`.sandpile`** is the Bak-Tang-Wiesenfeld toppling CA on the generic single-texture step path (no dedicated pipeline; Dhar's abelian property licenses any parallel schedule, and `SandpileTests` pins the settled pile **cell-for-cell against a sequential CPU reference**, the edge variant verified red against its counterfactual) with five terse invariants: the step is the **k-fold parallel form** `q' = fract(q) + Σ floor(q_n)/4` — topple as many times as a cell can per pass, identical to single toppling in the critical regime but load-bearing at a hot source, where single toppling pools for thousands of passes (a saturated blob's interior is net zero: lose four, receive four back); grains stored in **quarters** (stable cells read 0/¼/½/¾, gradientMap-ready; exact in half float to 2048 grains), state **read from `.r` only** (a luminance dot is off by an ulp and the toppling floor/fract must be exact), boundary **open — reject the position before sampling, never wrap or clamp** (the clamping sampler reads the edge texel back as its own neighbor, a reflecting wall; on a torus sand accumulates until every cell topples forever), and the inject **adds `rint(luma·pour)` whole grains** (an AA fringe pours whole grains or none; nothing erases); `topplings` is `subSteps`, the pacing dial (an avalanche front moves one texel per pass), the **classic relaxed figure is drop-and-relax** (one heavy mark on one frame, then settle — a steady heavy pour keeps a molten super-critical core by design), and `runSimulation` binds **`sim.params` to the inject pass too** (pre-existing injects read none — byte-identical); **the state automata** (`.cyclic` / `.excitable` / `.briansBrain` / `.hodgepodge`) share one encoding — integer state s of N levels stored as s/(N−1) in `.r`, decoded with `rint` (exact in half float well past N=200) — and three load-bearing pieces: `.cyclic`/`.hodgepodge` **start as seeded random states** via the first-allocation fill (`Sim.stateSeedFill` → `ollin_sim_state_seed`; a uniform field is a fixed point of both rules, so the Turing `fill:` hook generalized to `initialNoiseFill`), the draw-to-spark pair carries **snapping, alpha-gated injects** (`ollin_sim_inject_excite`/`_brain`: a solid mark writes the nearest meaningful state, a soft fringe writes nothing — a blended AA rim decodes as refractory/resting cells that block every birth, the bug that killed the first Brian's Brain seed), and `.hodgepodge` is **Dewdney's formulation** of Gerhardt-Schuster (healthy ⌊A/k1⌋+⌊B/k2⌋; infected ⌊S/(A+1)⌋+g, S summing the cell and all its neighbours, the +1 counting the cell itself among the infected — the divide-by-zero guard; ill → 0; everything caps at n). A solid blob legitimately **dies** under Brian's Brain (the interior rests together and a flat edge shows three neighbours where a birth needs exactly two) — sprinkle loose soup instead, a rule fact verified on a CPU model before suspecting the renderer. `StateAutomataTests` pins each rule **cell-for-cell against a sequential CPU reference** (both neighbourhood shapes, the toroidal wrap, the injects; sabotage-verified red), with two probe gotchas worth keeping: per-texel stamps must be **`drawPolygon` unit quads, not `drawRect`** (an SDF rect's ~1-render-px analytic halo leaves edge-adjacent texels hovering at the injects' 0.5 alpha gate), and headless `image(of:frame:)` runs **frame+1 generations** (it renders draws 0...frame inclusive); **`.relight` measures slope over a span, not a texel** (taps on the half-texel grid for a free 2×2 box, gain divided by the span, span growing with resolution past 1080) — slope weights a wavelength by 1/L, so a per-texel Sobel lights few-texel content as steeply as the real relief; smooth relief is preserved to ~1% (`effects-relight` did **not** need re-recording). **Don't soften a finish's specular exponent to chase speckle** — a broad lobe lifts the highlight on *flat* areas too (a `pow(sh,60)` `.liquid` washed the Guide's Organism background several shades lighter), so fix the normal, not the lobe; headless `image(of:frame:)` renders *every* warmup frame when `drawer.usesFeedback` (feedback / sim fields / SSR — else the ping-pong never evolves); fluid keeps its own `fluidSlots` beside `FeedbackSlot` so the single-field sims stay byte-identical; **`.multiScaleTuring`** is McCabe's rule (activator/inhibitor disc averages at several scales, least-disagreement scale fires, renormalize each step) and runs its **own multi-pass pipeline** (`runMultiScaleTuring`) like the fluid while sharing the one-pair `FeedbackSlot` (so flip/prune/repeat are the shipped ones), with four load-bearing pieces, each of which was a real defect first: the field **starts as seeded noise, not a constant** (a flat field is a fixed point of the rule, so `feedbackSlot` grew a `fill:` override beside `restState`); the blur pyramid is **Gaussian (Burt-Adelson binomial), not a 2x2 box** (a square kernel has square preferred directions, so the labyrinth came out rectilinear); a radius **gathers a 9-tap disc two rungs *finer* than the radius match**, never a single tap at the matching rung (that rung holds one sample per feature, so the pattern locks to the lattice, unfixable by any kernel since the information isn't there); and **variation is averaged over a per-scale `variationRadius`** (read at a point, a fine scale's disagreement crosses zero along every contour of its own structure and, since *least* disagreement wins, it claims a dense web of pixels and buries the coarse scales). Two parameter facts are equally load-bearing because renormalization couples the scales: **equal `amount`s across rungs** (the hardest-pushing rung sets the range and squeezes the rest toward mid gray) and a **ladder starting at radius 2** (radius 1 works on single texels and reads as speckle). `TuringScale.maxScales` (6) must equal `OLLIN_TURING_MAX_SCALES`, and `MetalRenderer.turingPyramidLevels` (12) must equal `OLLIN_TURING_LEVELS` (it sizes the texture-array binding; a shallower pyramid repeats its top rung to fill it). Deliberate v1 cut: the winning-scale index is **not** carried in the state (colour-by-scale would muddy a channel the display and the extent reduce both read as gray); `layer(_:)`'s draw closure is `@escaping @_implicitSelfCapture` (without it, bare draws inside `layer { }` need `self.` and the p5 feel breaks); SSAO's and SSR's stabilizer sets each fixed a worse artifact — don't undo any of them ([[ollin-ssao-normal-gbuffer-followup]], [[research-before-implementing]], [[verify-perceptual-temporal-fixes]]); DoF/SSAO/SSR `quality` is a `RenderQuality` tier resolving to a per-GPU budget (DoF: the bokeh *tap* budget via `resolveDofTaps` — M2 1080²: `.default` 192 ≈ 14 ms; **benchmark back-to-back on a cool machine**, single runs drift 50% under thermal load, enough to invert an A/B). **`.defocus` is two passes:** a **pre-pass** reduces the aux depth to `(scatter, depth, receive)` per pixel and the gather reads *that*, so a tap is one sample and no math (~35% faster than decoding depth per tap). The two sizes answer the depth map's **anti-aliased rim** from both sides and are load-bearing: **receive** = neighborhood **max** (the seam dilation, kills the dotted in-focus ring), **scatter** = neighborhood **min at radius 2** (kills the hard-edged `maxBlur`-radius halo a *sharp* object leaked; radius 2 not 1, or the gather's bilinear averages half the rim back in). The gather's own rules: both accumulators seed with the **center texel** (black-seeded near field composites a dark ring at partial coverage), **alpha rides with the colour** (premultiplied layers), a farther tap is **occlusion-clamped to 2× the center's blur** (else a heavy backdrop dissolves a barely-defocused midground for the full `maxBlur`, the pop that made a rack unwatchable), compositing is **far first, near over it** (`mix(mix(center, bg, dof), fg, α)`; one `max(dof, α)` mix double-applies the coverage), a pixel **under a near blur lets the bg field gather from inside that blur** and **fg coverage is an area fraction of the *near* blur, not the disc** (`total·(r/maxBlur)²`) — without both, a foreground blurs outward but keeps a razor *inner* edge (a 40% step in one pixel). The snapshots' mean-diff tolerance averages every one of these away (they all live in a band around a silhouette), so **`DefocusTests` pins them behaviorally** — reach for a probe here, not a whole-frame diff. Credits: AsyncGraphics + ofxFX + orx-fx/OPENRNDR + Apple MPS (`ATTRIBUTION.md`, never in `.swift`). `Docs/Drawing/Effects.md`; examples `Effects/*` (Bloom / ColorFilters / BlurFilters / StylizeFilters / RetroFilters / Glitter / Distortion / Patterns / MeshGradient / DesignPatterns / DesignFilters / Feedback / Compose / Aside / Defocus), `Simulation/GrayScott` / `GameOfLife` / `Lenia` / `Ripples` / `Sandpile` / `Fluid` / `CyclicAutomaton` / `Excitable` / `BriansBrain` / `Hodgepodge`, `3D/SceneDefocus` / `AmbientOcclusion` / `ScreenSpaceReflections`; snapshots `effects-*` + `lenia` + `ripples` + `sandpile` + `cyclic-automaton` + `excitable-medium` + `brians-brain` + `hodgepodge` + `mesh-gradient` / `design-patterns` / `design-filters` / `scene-defocus-3d` / `ssao-3d` / `ssr-3d`. `RipplesTests` pins the wave sim behaviorally (rest stays rest, rings expand, channels decay).

- **Watercolor wash** (`Sources/Ollin/Effects/WatercolorSim.swift` + `runWatercolor` in `MetalRenderer+Effects.swift` + the `ollin_wash_*` section of `ShaderSim.metal`). The three-layer wet-paint simulation (shallow water / pigment deposition / capillary; Kubelka-Munk optical rendering; credit in `ATTRIBUTION.md`): **`Sim.watercolor(pigments:...)`** runs on a **`WatercolorField`** (a `SimField` subclass, made with `watercolor(...)`; `SimField` is no longer `final`), painted through `withField` with **seed rgb = up to 3 palette pigments (premultiplied = dilution) and alpha = water**; `WatercolorPigment` carries K/S triples + density/staining/granulation (12 published measured presets; `overWhite:`/`overBlack:` derives K/S via the paper's inversion). **Load-bearing:** the velocity update is **`A + μB`** (the continuous equations' `+μ∇²u`; the printed pseudocode's sign reads inverted and anti-diffusion detonates) and relaxation runs **divergence-reducing** in gather form; the staggered grid packs (u right face, v top face, p, M) per texel, **off-canvas taps read dry via bounds-rejection** (the clamp sampler would reflect the edge), and dry-bordering faces re-pin to zero in *every* velocity-writing pass; pigment advects in **4 substeps of dt = ¼ with |u| ≤ 1** so the four-face outflow can never exceed the cell (pure gather, no rescale); `transfer_dep`/`transfer_pig` encode from **one snapshot** (exact conservation); the **inject's dry-brush height gate applies to both halves** (flow *and* pigment) or gaps hold flat unsimulated stamps; `dry()` bakes the wash into the dried R/T glaze stack (**fresh textures swapped into the slot**, never in-place) and **caps saturation below the mask-expansion threshold** or the sheet re-wets itself and dry() never sticks; **`blot()`** lifts water keeping pigment parked (the paper's authored damp state), and a **held** water touch blooms the backrun where a single tap only nudges (one frame's pressure relaxes away); K-M math runs in **display-space sRGB** (linearized only at output; CPU `KubelkaMunk` reference and `ollin_wash_km_layer` kept in step); a stroked mark rings stamps with pigment-free water, so painting blocks call `noStroke()`. Deterministic end to end → pixel snapshot `watercolor-sim`; `WatercolorSimTests` (K-M inversion round-trip + edge-darkening / dry-brush / dry-freeze / backrun-counterfactual / byte-replay probes); example `Simulation/Watercolor`; Guide Ch 19 § *Paint that behaves: watercolor*. Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *The watercolor wash*. `Docs/Simulation/Watercolor.md`.

- **Compute & GPU particles** (a *core* capability, GPU/Metal in `Ollin`). Runtime-compiled MSL kernels over persistent buffers/textures feeding the instanced render path — 10⁶ particles update + draw GPU-side, the sandpainting depth-of-field at scale. Types in `Sources/Ollin/Compute/`: `ComputeKernel` / `ComputeBuffer` / `PingPong` / `ComputeParams` / `Particles` (buffer half) and `ComputeTexture` / `PingPongTexture` / `Simulation` (texture half — reaction-diffusion / cellular automata / image kernels drawn as an `Image`). Facade: `compute(_:over:)` / `compute(_:reading:writing:)` / `compute(_:writing:)` / `updateParticles` / `updateSimulation` / `drawParticles`. Kernels inline or in a `.metal` file (`ComputeKernel(resource:in:)`); shared types + the full `OllinShaderLib` (the same hash/noise/curl/disc + palette/OKLab/`sd*` helpers fragment shaders get, `hash12` not the old `hash21`) auto-splice. **Gotchas:** ping-pong (not in-place) for any buffer the render path also reads; a file using `OllinParticle` must `import COllinShaders`; the `COllinShaders` clang module is stale-cache-prone (`swift package clean` after adding a shared struct); kernel compile errors are swallowed → missing particles, not a crash; headless export runs intermediate sim steps via `stepCompute`; seed once via a flag (`frameCount` is 1 on the first `draw()`). `Docs/Shaders/Compute.md`; examples `Compute/CurlField`, `Compute/ReactionDiffusion`, `Rendering/DepthOfField`.

- **GPU artificial life** (`Sources/Ollin/Compute/SpatialHash.swift` + `ParticleLife.swift` / `PPS.swift` / `Physarum.swift`). The compute path's first *cooperative* primitive + three emergent-behavior sims on it. **`SpatialHash`** (public) is a GPU uniform-grid fixed-radius neighbor search (Hoetzlein **counting sort**: clear → count → serial exclusive-prefix-sum → scatter, the four kernels sharing one source); it's the enabler `ParticleLife`/`PPS` query and a sketch builds its own sim on via the `neighborStep(_:over:reading:writing:)` facade + the `OLLIN_FOR_NEIGHBORS` macro. **Load-bearing:** the shared struct is **`OllinSpatialGrid`** in `OllinShaderTypes.h` (stride 32; **distinct from the pre-existing `OllinGridParams`**, the 3D ground-grid uniform, whose name it must not reuse); the grid/neighbor helpers (`ollin_grid_cell`, `ollin_torus_delta`, the `OLLIN_FOR_NEIGHBORS`/`OLLIN_END_NEIGHBORS` macro pair) live **unmarked** at the tail of `OllinShaderLib.metal` so `filterLibModules` always keeps them (compute-only; never bloats a user fragment shader's `using:` subset); cell edge = query **radius** over a **toroidal** domain so all in-radius neighbors sit in the wrapped **3×3** block, which needs `gridW`/`gridH` **≥ 3** (init clamps, so `worldSize` may round up past `bounds`) or the wrap double-counts a cell; **atomics** power the count/scatter (`atomic_fetch_add`) and never appeared in this codebase before, and the **serial compute encoder** (default `makeComputeCommandEncoder()`) orders the build passes with no explicit barriers (same as `Simulation`'s substeps); buffer-index contract `0/1` particles read/write, `2` sortedIndices, `3` cellStart, `4` cellCount, `5` grid, `6+` sim-specific (`ParticleLife`'s matrix at 6); `nil` gaps in a `RecordedDispatch.buffers` array bind nothing (index = array position). All three sims reuse **`OllinParticle`** (no new struct): type/heading ride the free `seedA` scratch; each is a `@MainActor final class` recording its step by appending `RecordedDispatch`es directly (the `Particles.recordStep` model, **not** the 1-2-buffer `compute()` facade). `Physarum` uses **no hash** (trail path): agent buffer + `r32Float` **`PingPongTexture`** trail + an **atomic `UInt32` deposit grid** (fixed-point, avoids Metal's undefined concurrent-`texture.write` race), stepped clear→agents(sense/steer/move/deposit)→diffuse+decay→colorize; draw via `.image`. **Load-bearing anti-lattice-artifact trio:** **bilinear** trail sensing + a **per-step motor noise** (heading jitter) + a **1-2-1 Gaussian** diffuse (not a box mean); without them the faithful-Jones steering (which keeps heading exactly when all three sensors read equal, i.e. in the empty region an agent forages into) sends escaping agents ballistically straight, and those coherent parallel escapes self-reinforce on the square lattice into **cardinal-axis spurs** (the motor noise is the decisive one, turning the ballistic escape into an even-spreading wander). **Reproducibility caveat (load-bearing):** the counting-sort **scatter order within a cell is GPU-atomic-race-ordered** and the sims are chaotic, so there's **no frame-exact export guarantee and no pixel snapshot** (matching the compute path); correctness is pinned instead by `SpatialHashTests` (`hasMetal`-gated: counts sum, exact prefix sum, `sortedIndices` a permutation, per-cell bucketing, and a neighbor **set** matching brute force — all order-independent). Facade factories `spatialHash(in:radius:count:)` / `particleLife(count:kinds:radius:…)` / `primordialParticles(count:radius:…)` / `physarum(agents:resolution:…)` default `bounds` to the canvas and `seed` to `variation`. `Docs/Simulation/ArtificialLife.md` + `Docs/Shaders/Compute.md#spatialhash`; examples `Simulation/ParticleLife` / `PrimordialParticles` / `Physarum` + `Compute/NeighborSearch` (the raw primitive, the one example that `import COllinShaders` for `OllinParticle`, wired via the extended `example(…, dependencies:)`).

- **Emergent matter and heritable rules** (`Sources/Ollin/Compute/ParticleLenia.swift` + `SwarmChemistry.swift`; shared header structs `OllinLeniaParams`, stride 112, and `OllinSwarmChemistryParams`, stride 48, both bound at buffer 11). Two more per-particle-rule sims over `SpatialHash`, deliberately **two systems rather than one**: a rule that makes matter organize and a rule that is itself heritable are different questions, and fusing them (genome + bonds + energy + construction) is the step from a recipe to an environment, which the framework lane refuses. Both reuse `OllinParticle` and follow the artificial-life reproducibility rule (**no pixel snapshot**: a neighbor sum's order is atomic-race-ordered, so a run is not bit-reproducible even at one seed, and the tests measure what each term *does* against the run with that term made inert). **`ParticleLenia`** is the energy-based formulation (Mordvintsev/Niklasson/Randazzo): one neighbor pass accumulates the ring-kernel field `U`, its gradient, and the repulsion gradient, then `∇E = ∇R − G'(U)∇U` and the particle steps down it. **Load-bearing:** `wK` is **derived, never tabulated** (the closed form `πσ(μ√π(1+erf(μ/σ)) + σe^{−(μ/σ)²})` reciprocal, which reproduces the paper's 0.022 at μ=4/σ=1) so `muG` keeps meaning the same crowding when the ring moves; growth is the **only attractive** term and repulsion the **only** one with an opinion at short range (the kernel is a *ring*, so coincident particles add nothing to each other's field and growth alone would let them pile up) — the two oppose each other on **nearest-neighbor distance**, which is what both counterfactual tests read, and neither is measurable as population *spread* (the opening disc already sits near the settled size); the seeded disc holds **~1 particle per unit² of area** (a canvas-wide scatter opens outside everyone's kernel and shows a minute of nothing); a step is never larger than the **published 0.1**, so more pace means more substeps, each with its own hash rebuild. **`SwarmChemistry`** (Sayama 2009 kinetics + the 2011 evolutionary layer) gives every particle its own eight-value **`Recipe`** in a ping-ponged float buffer (stride 12: 8 values + lineage + pad, buffers 6/7), transmitted **on contact** with mutation, direction set by a **`Competition`** (`.faster` / `.slower` / `.majority`). **Load-bearing:** the published ranges were chosen for a world whose particles sit ~50 units apart and they **carry length** (separation is length²/step²), so recipes are *stored* in published units and the kernel converts by a **`lengthScale` derived from the world's own density** (lengths and speeds by it, separation by its square) — dropped in unconverted on a 1080 canvas, separation runs ~8.6× too strong and the swarm is an even gas; `perceptionLimit` and `contactRadius` derive from the same spacing, so a particle sees about as many others as one in the published world did and a sketch names only `count` and `kinds`. **Randomness is listed beside cohesion/alignment/separation, not instead of them** (replacing them means a recipe with `c4 = 0.33`, which real published recipes have, spends a third of its steps with no cohesion at all and nothing coheres), and "steer randomly" is the same `desired − velocity` toward a random heading at the recipe's own normal speed in both the straying and the randomness branch. Transmission is a **pull**: a thread takes the best of the contacts that beat it, ties broken on the lower index so the neighbor sort's order cannot decide it; `.majority` compares same-line neighbor counts stored **one step stale** (a thread can count its own neighborhood, not a neighbor's). **Mutation is per contact, not per generation** (default 0.01 against `Evolution`'s 0.04) because a crowded particle makes contact several times a second; a mutant **keeps its parent's line**, so lines only ever die out and the tally is a contest. Opening state is a design decision both times: a **handful of recipes each held in quantity**, since one random recipe per particle leaves nothing for a structure to be made of. Facades `particleLenia(count:spacing:)` / `swarmChemistry(count:kinds:)` + `updateParticleLenia` / `updateSwarmChemistry` + `drawParticles`. Readbacks `snapshotRecipes()` / `snapshotLineages()` / `lineageCounts()` stall on the GPU (call them a few times a second). `ComputeBuffer.snapshot()` now returns the pending seed before first bind, so a sketch can read a sim's state in `setup()`. Examples `Simulation/ParticleLenia` + `SwarmChemistry`; `ParticleLeniaTests` (8) + `SwarmChemistryTests` (9), each verified red by sabotage; `Docs/Simulation/ArtificialLife.md`; Guide Ch 20 §§ *Matter that decides what shape to be: Particle Lenia* + *A rule that spreads by winning arguments: swarm chemistry* with a figure each.

- **Steering at scale** (`Sources/Ollin/Compute/Swarm.swift`; shared header struct `OllinSwarmParams`, stride 96, bound at buffer 11). The Reynolds steering vocabulary as a GPU sim over `SpatialHash`, reusing `OllinParticle` (`seedA` = last heading, `seedB` = the wander walk's angle). **`Swarm`** carries eight behaviors as **weights** (`separation`/`alignment`/`cohesion` need neighbors; `seek`/`flee`/`arrive`/`wander`/`flow` do not), plus `maxSpeed`/`minSpeed`/`maxForce`/`separationRadius`/`viewAngle`/`slowingRadius`/the wander + flow knobs, all live; facade `swarm(count:perceptionRadius:colors:size:bounds:seed:)` + `updateSwarm` + `drawParticles`, `bounds` defaulting to the canvas and `seed` to `variation`. **Load-bearing:** every behavior returns **`desired − velocity`** so all eight forces share one unit and their weights are comparable, and the weighted sum takes **one truncation at `maxForce`** (the published combination method) before the paper's `truncate(velocity + acceleration, maxSpeed)` integration; **`arrive` is the one behavior that must not be normalized** (its ramp lives in the magnitude); **`flee` is not `seek` negated** (negating the force leaves `desired + velocity`, not `desired − velocity`); the three neighborhood behaviors share **one pass** over the 3×3 block and honor a **field-of-view** cut (`viewCosine`); the world is a **torus** like its `SpatialHash` siblings (so no containment behavior, deliberately); speeds/forces are **per second** where the CPU `Vehicle`'s are per step; **the hash build is skipped when all three flocking weights are zero**, which is what lets a million wanderers/seekers run (measured M2 release 1080²: 1M stepping at 60 fps, drawing them is the limit; 30k flocking drawn at 60; 120k at 34). **`minSpeed` (default 0 = the published model) is an addition**, and the fix for a dense swarm setting solid: a stalled agent is a wall the crowd jams against, so a floor keeps it flowing. **Three numbers are coupled and a wrong-looking swarm is usually one of them:** neighbors seen = `count·π·r²/area` (~20 works, far more washes structure into the global mean), `separationRadius` ≈ 1–2× mean spacing (much larger saturates into an even gas), and the turning circle `maxSpeed²/maxForce` should be a few times `perceptionRadius` (tighter and agents orbit inside their own neighborhood). Follows the artificial-life reproducibility rule (**no pixel snapshot**; the neighbor sort is atomic-race-ordered and flocking is chaotic) — `SwarmTests` pins each behavior against its counterfactual instead, and **alignment is tested by *local* order** (agreement with one's own neighbors, 0.98 by 4 s) because global consensus measures mixing time, not the rule (it does get there: 0.10 at 1 s → 0.97 at 20 s). **Gotcha the tuning cost a session:** a still frame shows *density*, not movement (agents move a few points per frame), so the look needs a **translucent rect** over an accumulating canvas — while accumulating, `background(_:)` *wipes* whatever alpha its color carries, so it leaves no trail at all (the rect form accumulates on the headless `--export --frame N` path too, via `drawer.accumulates`, so a Guide figure gets real trails). A dense space-filling swarm organizing into lanes, cells, and a labyrinth is the rules working, not a fault. Example `Simulation/Swarm`; `Docs/Simulation/Swarm.md`.

- **Chaotic systems on the GPU** (`Sources/Ollin/Compute/AttractorFlow.swift` + the *Chaotic systems* tail section of `OllinShaderLib.metal`; shared header struct `OllinAttractorParams`, stride 208, bound at buffer 11). The million-particle leg of the strange attractors: **`AttractorFlow`** runs one of the eight published continuous systems (`AttractorSystem`, the `Sim` struct-plus-internal-`Kind` shape, factories mirroring `StrangeAttractor`'s defaults and `.attractor` handing back that CPU twin) under a million particles, each advanced by fourth-order Runge-Kutta on the GPU, drawn as camera-facing splats. **It reuses `OllinPoint`, not `OllinParticle`**, because a flow is 3D, which needed the one new draw seam: a **GPU-resident point cloud** (`Drawer.recordPointCloud(buffer:count:)` opens a `.points3D` batch carrying `particleBuffer`, and the encode binds that instead of the frame's uploaded point list). **Load-bearing:** that batch leaves `currentKind = nil` so a following `drawPointCloud` opens its own batch (a merged CPU cloud's points would be silently passed over, since a batch carrying a GPU buffer draws only that buffer); it is gated out of `makeBatch` like `drawParticles`. The MSL side is **unmarked compute-only** at the tail of `OllinShaderLib.metal` (the spatial-hash precedent, always spliced): the eight derivatives + the three iterated maps + **`OLLIN_RK4_STEP(state, h, derivative)`**, a *macro* because Metal has no function pointers here (the `OLLIN_FOR_NEIGHBORS` precedent) whose `derivative` is an expression in the sample point `_p`; `ollin_attractor_field`'s switch is kept in step with `AttractorSystem.shaderIndex` **by hand** (the `ollin_sdf_distance` rule). **One CPU orbit at build does everything else** and is why a sketch names no per-system numbers: it measures `center`/`extent`, the pace, and the speed range, *and* it is where the particles start.
