Four invariants, each a fixed defect: the reach is a **percentile, not the extremes** (rare excursions make a maximum grow with sample size, the four-wing measuring 2.0 over 120k points and 3.5 over 400k, which moved the framing, the dot size, and the stray test with it); particles seed **on the attractor**, not in a box around it, because the transient is the system's own contraction rate (a fraction of a second for Lorenz, a minute of watching a filled ball for Aizawa) and the CPU tier's `settle` throws it away for the same reason; the seeding **jitter is load-bearing** (exactly on the orbit every particle rides *one* trajectory forever and the picture is that curve with dots sliding along it; a hair off, chaos separates them into a million within a few laps); and the survey **filters non-finite points**, since constants nobody checked have no attractor and the diverging orbit's NaN otherwise spreads to every measured figure and traps the seeding range (a real crash the tests caught). The step is `speed` worth of the *system's own* time split into substeps **never larger than the published step** (past it the integrator is solving a different system), so the `maxSubsteps` ceiling slows the flow rather than coarsening it; a particle past `extent * 12` is dropped back in the middle rather than left to streak. **Color is speed, brightness is density**, so the default ramp shifts hue and holds brightness roughly level (a dark-to-light ramp puts two facts on one channel and a slow crowded region reads like a fast empty one), and `opacity` is low by design so the pile-up carries tone. Follows the artificial-life reproducibility rule (**no pixel snapshot**) — `AttractorFlowTests` (12) pins the region the cloud occupies against its own CPU orbit, each with a counterfactual (a different system lands elsewhere; the sabotage check: a wrong constant in the kernel moves the cloud's middle 13 units and fails it). Measured M2 release 1080²: 1M particles stepping and drawing at 55 fps, 0.2 ms CPU. Facade `attractorFlow(count:_:seed:)` + `updateAttractorFlow` + `drawParticles`, `seed` defaulting to `variation`. Example `Simulation/Attractor`; `Docs/Drawing/Attractors.md#flow` + the function table in `Docs/Shaders/ShaderLibrary.md`; Guide Ch 22 § *A million riding the same field* + AttractorFlow figure.

- **Evolution** (`Sources/Ollin/Compute/Evolution.swift` + `Sources/Ollin/Geometry/Population.swift`; shared header struct `OllinEvolutionParams`, stride 272, bound at buffer 11). Selection as a recipe, in the two sizes the technique actually comes in. **`Evolution`** is the GPU tier: tens of thousands of individuals each fly a genome of steering impulses over a fixed trial, are scored on closest approach to a `target` past up to eight `obstacles`, and are replaced in one breeding pass. **`Population`** + **`Genome`** are the CPU tier, Karl Sims' normalized parameter-set genotype judged by a person (`breed(from:)` for interaction, `breed(scoredBy:)` for a measure). **Load-bearing:** the population lives in a **compute buffer, not a `SimField`** because selection needs each child to read an *arbitrary* rival's score (a texel-local step cannot reach one), and it is **tournament** selection because that is the one method needing no total, no sort, and no normalizing (it only ever *compares*), so a generation breeds in one dispatch with no atomics and no ordering between threads, and a score's **scale never matters, only its order** — where the CPU tier can afford the wheel of fortune, whose sum is exactly what a GPU pass would have to stop and agree on; **no explicit elitism** because every child draws `2 * tournament` rivals, so the best is passed over about `e^(-2·tournament)` of the time (three generations in ten thousand at the default). Genomes ride their **own buffer** ping-ponged beside the particles (both swap together in the breed pass, buffers 6/7); the sim reuses `OllinParticle` with **`life` = flying, `seedA` = closest approach, `seedB` = the score**. **The opening generation is a design decision, not a detail:** genes drawn independently are a *random walk* whose steps cancel, so a whole population would mill about the start with nothing to select between — genomes open as smooth **arcs** (a heading with a per-gene turn), measured keeping ~0.48 of their length as displacement against a walk's ~0.18. **Everything about the pace is derived from the start-to-target distance** (top speed crosses it in ~2 s, trial is 1.7 crossings, thrust reaches top speed in a quarter trial), re-derived every frame so dragging the target re-paces the run, and `spanToTarget` is **floored** because a target on the start would otherwise divide every derived figure by zero. Scoring: closest approach **squared** (pressure where gains are small), a wall keeps **a tenth**, an arrival is **its own band above every near miss** and higher the sooner (the two bands must not overlap or a population that found the target can be out-bred by one that has not). Mutation **nudges and clamps** rather than replacing, which is why with it off *every gene is bit-identical to one generation 0 held* (the exact statement of what mutation is for, and the test; "stops improving" is **not** true at short range with a large population — initial variety carries it). Randomness is a pure function of index, generation, and the population's own seed, never the sketch's rng. `measuresGenerations` opts into a per-generation readback (`lastGeneration`: best/mean/arrived/closest) taken **without stalling the GPU**, so it can be a frame stale. The default ramp shifts hue and **holds brightness level** (the `AttractorFlow` rule: a whole population leaves at one point, so a dark-to-light ramp reports the launch crowd as the leaders). `Population` owns a seeded `SplitMix64` (`SplitMix64` gained `Sendable`), **carries the chosen genomes through untouched** (`keepsParents` — one breeding is a big step when a person is judging), does **nothing at all** when nothing is chosen, counts a negative score as zero and picks evenly when every score is zero; its `mutationRate` default is Sims' 0.2 against the GPU tier's 0.04, because a few hundred looks have to cover ground a scored run covers in millions. Follows the artificial-life reproducibility rule (**no pixel snapshot**; one flight's ulp changes a tournament) — `EvolutionTests` (13) + `PopulationTests` (15) pin each piece against its counterfactual, every one verified red by sabotage. Facade `evolution(count:genes:from:to:seed:)` + `updateEvolution` + `drawParticles`, and `population(count:genes:seed:)`. Examples `Simulation/Evolution` + `Simulation/Breeding`; `Docs/Simulation/Evolution.md`; Guide Ch 20 § *Letting the sketch find it: evolution* + Evolution figure.

- **GPU fluids & soft bodies** (`Sources/Ollin/Compute/ParticleFluid.swift` + `SoftBodies.swift`). Particle dynamics in a walled box over `SpatialHash`, reusing `OllinParticle`; shared header structs `OllinSPHParams` (stride 112) / `OllinSoftBody` (32) / `OllinSoftBodyParams` (64). **`ParticleFluid`** is 2D SPH (Müller 2003 symmetrized EOS pressure + Clavet 2005 near-density/near-pressure for anti-clumping and emergent surface tension; quadratic/cubic spike kernels normalized for 2D by integration): per substep, predict (gravity + one-frame `pull`/`push`) → rebuild hash over **predicted** positions (fixed 1/120 s look-ahead) → density pass → force/integrate pass, all **pure gathers** off one snapshot so pair forces cancel equal-and-opposite with no atomics. **Load-bearing:** densities are normalized to the *seeded lattice* (`restDensity` 1 = as packed, knobs survive a radius change); the box is **walls, not a torus** — kernels use plain deltas and the `r < h` test rejects the wrapped 3×3's cross-wall candidates (safe because the hash world rounds down by less than one cell); the velocity clamp (0.45·h per substep) makes tunneling impossible, so a hitch can't detonate the sim; wall under-density compression is countered by near-pressure (raising `nearStiffness`, not clamping pressure, is the intended lever). **`SoftBodies`** is meshless shape matching (Müller 2005): a serial one-thread-per-body reduce (the prefix-sum trade) fits centroid + closed-form 2D rotation, then particles steer to rotated rest offsets at `squish` (converted per substep, feel is frame-rate-independent); contacts are **cross-body only** and inelastic (overlap repulsion + each side sheds half the approach speed). **Load-bearing:** rest offsets are taken about the **rest centroid** (the lattice mean, not the disk center) — anything else leaves `Σ(goal − x) ≠ 0` and the body silently self-thrusts (a real bug: blobs hovered and pinned into corners; pinned by `FluidDynamicsTests.isolatedBodyConservesMomentum`); spawns separate via `relaxCircles` before particle fill (an overlapped spawn explodes). Both run fixed substeps against a clamped frame clock (≤ 1/30 s), pack params at buffer 11 per substep, and follow the artificial-life reproducibility rule (**no pixel snapshots**; behavioral `FluidDynamicsTests`). Facades `particleFluid(count:radius:)` / `softBodies(count:radius:)` + `updateParticleFluid` / `updateSoftBodies` / `drawParticles` overloads default `bounds` to the canvas and `seed` to `variation`. Examples `Simulation/ParticleFluid` (dam break, drag to grab) + `Simulation/SoftBodies` (jelly pile, drag to knead). `Docs/Simulation/Fluids.md`.

- **Text.** Three font kinds over one `drawText` / `textToShapes` / metrics surface (`textFont` / `textSize` / `textAlign` / `textWidth`): `BitmapFont` (default Cozette, BDF + Playdate `.fnt`), `OutlineFont` (Core Text `.ttf`/`.otf`, each glyph a `Shape` taking fill *and* stroke, variable-font axes; default `.systemMedium`), `StrokeFont` (Hershey `.jhf`, plotter polylines). `textMode(.atlas)` flips outline text to an SDF glyph atlas (`GlyphAtlas`, exact EDT, fourth glyph pipeline). Per-glyph / on-path / box-wrap / metrics helpers; standard notices `drawStatus` / `drawCaption`. **Gotchas:** glyph `Shape`s must be `.nonZero` (even-odd cuts `e`/`a`/`g` terminals); San-Francisco overlapping sub-contours need a Clipper2 union clean at reference scale 1024 then RDP-simplify (`OutlineFont.simplifyClosed`) per render size, or a per-frame fps regression; warp via bounded `signedNoise`, not raw `curlNoise`; `textToShapes` returns RAW uneven Core Text vertices (`resampled(spacing:)` respaces them evenly for dot/jitter effects); bare `textFont(.builtin)` is ambiguous — use the typed form. `Docs/Drawing/Text.md`.

- **Text in every script** (`Sources/Ollin/Text/TextClusters.swift` + `ColorGlyphs.swift` + `LineBreaks.swift`, plus the layout core in `OutlineFont`). The four places the one-glyph-per-letter assumption breaks, each fixed at the layout tier so every text path inherits it: **`TextDirection`** (`textDirection(_:)`, stack state, `ParamOption`) forces the base writing direction where the text can't say it (`.automatic` default = today's behavior, byte-identical); **cluster-aware runs** replace per-glyph runs in `glyphRun` (per-glyph `drawText`, text-on-path), so `GlyphRunItem`/`TextGlyph` carry **`text: String`** (+ `character` as the first char, `isPicture`) rather than one `Character`; **color glyphs** (emoji) rasterize through `ColorGlyphCache` and draw on the **image path** in the outline *and* atlas modes; **`LineBreaks`** replaces the space split in `wrapToWidth`. Readbacks: `OutlineFont.fontsUsed(for:)` / `missingCharacters(in:)` + the font-kind-agnostic `Sketch.textMissingCharacters(_:)`. **Load-bearing:** the cluster unit is the **grapheme**, not the string index (a Devanagari syllable's glyphs report *different* indices inside one grapheme — bucketing by index splits it into pieces that overlap on the canvas), a grapheme with **no glyph of its own joins the piece before it** (a ligature is reported at its first character in either direction; one at the very start waits for the first piece instead), and clusters sort by **`originX` with a build-order tie-break** so a right-to-left line comes out left-to-right on the canvas (`TextGlyph.index` 0 is the letter read *last*, which is what a sweep across the drawing wants); a cluster's advance is the **sum of the shaper's own advances**, never a difference of neighboring positions (an Arabic mark is stored *after* its base at a *larger* x, so the difference goes negative, clamps to 0, and the total is wrong); `glyphRun` applies **both** pen offsets (`x` and `y`) or Arabic harakat and combining marks drop onto the baseline (measured up to 0.063 em, invisible in a whole-line bounding box, so the test compares glyphs one at a time); a color glyph is told from an ordinary one **only by the absence of a path** (there is no flag), rasterizes once at **160 px = the largest `sbix` strike Apple Color Emoji carries** (read from the font's own table, so a bigger raster only upsamples), takes neither `fill` nor `stroke`, is **left out of `textToShapes`** with a `noteOnce`, and counts as a skipped image in a vector export; the SDF atlas is one channel so a picture can never ride it (its own image batch, in call order); `LineBreaks` creates its tokenizer with **no locale** (measured identical to `en_US`/`th_TH`/current across English, Japanese and Thai, so an export can't depend on the machine) and a wrapped line is trimmed of its trailing space; the paragraph-style setting holds a *pointer*, so the two forced-direction styles are built with **nested `withUnsafePointer`** (letting it escape reads freed stack memory and the direction silently does nothing). Bitmap and stroke fonts have no shaping engine and stay left-to-right by design. **No pixel snapshot** (the layout and the emoji bitmaps come from the machine's own OS and installed faces, which a reference would pin instead of Ollin's code, matching the repo's system-font-free snapshot convention) — `ComplexTextTests` (21, all eight mechanisms verified red by sabotage) + two Metal-gated render probes pin it instead. Example `Text/Scripts`; Guide Ch 8 § *Not every language works like English* + EveryScript figure; `Docs/Drawing/Text.md#scripts`.

- **Writing in columns, and justification** (`TextDirection.topToBottom` + `textJustify(_:)`, over `lineStarts` / `layoutBlock` in `OutlineFont.swift`). Tategaki: text down the page in columns that fill **right to left**, plus flush-both-edges justification in either axis. **Load-bearing:** the axis is its own *`TextDirection`*, not a second knob, and asking Core Text for `kCTVerticalFormsAttributeName` does **both** halves at once — it substitutes the font's own sideways glyphs *and* returns positions already running down the column with the font's vertical origin applied, so **Ollin rotates nothing and translates nothing** (probe-verified: `（` goes 27.6×92.2 → 92.1×27.6, the comma moves corner to corner); whether **Latin turns is the face's decision** (Hiragino carries a turned `A`, San Francisco does not), which is why the doc promises the font's shapes rather than a rotation. One `lineStarts` returns the point each line measures from — a line's left edge on its baseline, a column's center axis at its top edge — so **one glyph formula (`start + (x, -y)·size`) serves both axes** and `placedGlyphs`/`placedAtlasGlyphs` cannot drift; a column is **one em across** by definition (fixed block width whatever the text) and column pitch is the same `textLeading`. The two `textAlign` axes **swap roles** (vertical says where a column starts, horizontal places the block), `textWidth` measures *along the writing axis*, and `drawText(_:in:)` wraps against the box **height**. In `glyphRun` the piece's distance is **accumulated from advances, not read off a glyph** (Core Text's y = 0 is the column *top*, one ascent above the first baseline — measuring from the baseline slides every column, the defect `perPieceDrawingPutsInkWherePlainDrawingDoes` pins) and only the along-axis coordinate is made relative (`ShapedCluster.anchor`, sorted **descending** for a column since pen y falls as you read). **Text on a path stays horizontal** (the curve already carries the direction) and bitmap/stroke fonts note once and stay horizontal. **Kinsoku needed no code:** the system break set `LineBreaks` already asks is UAX #14, which encodes the Japanese rules, so a comma arrives joined to the character it follows and greedy whole-piece filling *is* the push-out behaviour — the old "the finer rules are not applied" claim was **stale**, and `japaneseKeepsForbiddenCharactersOffTheEdgesOfALine` pins it with a naive per-character wrap as the counterfactual. **Justification is `CTLineCreateJustifiedLine`** (the engine knows each script's opportunities: spaces in English, between characters in Japanese), needs a box (only a box states the target), never squeezes, and **the last line of each paragraph is left natural** — which is why `wrappedLines` reports `paragraphEnds` *while it still knows them*, since joined lines lose the distinction. `TextJustification` lives on the drawer for the length of one box call. No pixel snapshot (system fonts + system layout, the complex-scripts rule); `VerticalTextTests` (18) + one `ComplexTextTests` addition, all eight mechanisms verified red by sabotage — including a **reading-order** test the first sabotage pass proved was missing (a reversed column measures perfectly and lands its ink correctly; only the character each piece stands for gives it away). Example `Text/Columns`; `Docs/Drawing/Text.md#vertical` + `#justify`.

- **Hanging punctuation** (`textHangingPunctuation(_:)` / `noTextHangingPunctuation()`, over `HangingPunctuation` in `LineBreaks.swift`, `Drawer.hangingWidth(of:)`, and `layoutBlock`'s per-line `hang`). A stop or comma at a line end may sit past that end (ぶら下げ) rather than taking the character it follows to the next line. **Load-bearing:** the hangable set is **stops and commas only** (`。 、` plus their full-width and half-width forms, and the Latin `.` `,`) — a closing bracket is line-start-forbidden too, and hanging one would put it outside the thing it closes; the hang width is measured as **the difference the last character makes to the line** (`textWidth(line) - textWidth(line.dropLast())`), not read off the character alone, so it carries whatever the shaping did about the pair, and only a line actually ending in a stop pays the second measurement. Two places consume it and both matter: the **wrap fit test** subtracts it (that is the whole visible effect, and it is why the piece the stop rides on can stay), and **`layoutBlock` reports a per-line `hang` that `lineStarts` subtracts from the extent**, so a right-aligned or centered line lands where the line *without* its stop would land and a justified line stretches to `extent + hang` (the part that counts reaches the box, carrying the stop past it). Like justification it is **scoped to the box call** (`textHangsInBox`, set and cleared by `drawText(_:in:)`; only a box has an edge to hang past) while the setting itself is ordinary drawing state that `withState` restores. The extent half is outline-font work; bitmap and stroke fonts still wrap this way and still align on the whole line. Note the effect **only appears where a stop would not otherwise fit**, so a demo has to be tuned to a box width that produces one (`Text/HangingStops` is, and a width sweep is how it was found). No pixel snapshot (system fonts + system layout); `HangingPunctuationTests` (10). Example `Text/HangingStops`; `Docs/Drawing/Text.md#hanging`.
- **Mongolian columns** (`TextDirection.topToBottomLeftToRight`, over the same `lineStarts` / `penOrigin` / geometry cache in `OutlineFont.swift`). Vertical text whose columns fill **left to right**, with the letters joined. **Load-bearing, and the opposite of the tategaki path:** this script is UAX #50 `vo = R`, so its glyphs are stored in *horizontal* orientation and a line is **shaped horizontally and then turned a quarter turn clockwise**; asking for `kCTVerticalFormsAttributeName` instead gives every letter a **full em advance and breaks the cursive joins** (probe-verified against `CTLineDraw`'s own vertical output — the joined spine is the thing a still frame shows). The turn is `(lx, ly) → (-ly, lx)` in local canvas space and matches Core Text's own rendering glyph for glyph; `CTRunGetTextMatrix` reports the *opposite* sense for these runs (its vertical text space is itself flipped), so **never apply the run matrix here** — a wrong turn still stacks and still measures, and only the joins failing to meet gives it away, which is what `aColumnIsTheLineTurnedAQuarterTurnClockwise` pins against the horizontal twin. A column is **as wide as the face's ascent and descent** rather than one em, and its axis sits one **descent** in from the block's left edge, because the turn puts the ascender side to the *right*; the pen formula becomes `start + (y, x)·size` (both offsets added, no y-flip), and the **turned glyph geometry is cached under its own key**, built by turning the upright one, so a turned column stays a translate of cached vertices and the flatten happens once for the pair. The atlas quad takes the same turn corner by corner (a quarter turn keeps a quad a quad and carries each corner's UV with it). The face **must have the script**: the column width comes from the base face's own metrics, and the system font does not have it. No pixel snapshot (system fonts + system layout, the complex-scripts rule); `MongolianTextTests` (12), the turn verified red by sabotage. Example `Text/MongolianColumns`; `Docs/Drawing/Text.md#mongolian`.
- **Images.** `Image` value type (ImageIO decode — PNG / JPEG / HEIC / TIFF / GIF — from path / url / data / resource / CGImage; `loadImage` sugar), drawn by `drawImage` on the textured-quad pipeline in call-ordered batches; lazy device texture cached on the `Image`, premultiplied, loaded `.SRGB:true`. `tint(_:)` / `noTint()` stack state. Pixel get/set via the `Image[x, y]` subscript (premultiplied, top-left origin); blank `Image(width:height:color:)` + bulk `Image(width:height:premultipliedRGBA:)`. **Gotchas:** `MTKTextureLoader` honors `.SRGB` only for ImageIO-backed CGImages — a context-made CGImage washes out lighter, so `Image.texture(for:)` self-heals; don't add a y-flip to the pixel buffer (it's already top-down). `Docs/Drawing/Images.md`.

- **Image as input** (glyph mosaic `Sources/Ollin/Text/GlyphMosaic.swift`, pixel sorting `Image/PixelSort.swift`, single line `Geometry/SingleLine.swift`, slit scan `Image/SlitScan.swift`, halftone `Image/Halftone.swift`, string art `Geometry/StringArt.swift`). Six CPU renderings that turn a picture into something else, all following the Dither bulk-pixel precedent (`premultipliedPixels()` in, `Image(width:height:premultipliedRGBA:)` out) and all deterministic. **Glyph mosaic** — `drawGlyphMosaic(_:columns:characters:in:inverted:colored:glyphScale:)` + the data form `glyphMosaic(of:)` (`[GlyphMosaicCell]`): brightness → glyph by **measured ink coverage** in the active font (bitmap lit-pixel count / outline signed shoelace area / stroke pen travel; ramp cached per (font, characters), normalized to its densest member); **empty floor** below half the sparsest glyph (shadows stay voids); transparency carries no ink in either mapping; `GlyphSet.technical/classic/blocks` curated sets (Cozette covers the whole technical set); `glyphScale` default 0.85 keeps gutters so dense glyphs read as discrete marks (`1` tiles blocks seamlessly) — for the reference "marks" look, use a set that tops out below the solid blocks (the example does). **Pixel sorting** — `Image.pixelSorted(_:by:threshold:reversed:)` (Asendorf's interval model): threshold-bounded runs along rows/columns sorted by brightness/hue/saturation, keys from straight color, alpha rides its pixel, position tie-break → byte-deterministic; the iconic jagged look needs *texture* in the source (run boundaries must vary per line — a clean gradient sorts invisibly, and ascending on an already-dark-to-bright gradient is a near no-op; `reversed: true` is the dramatic direction there). **Single line** — `singleLine(through:closed:)` (pure, rng-free: grid-accelerated NN construction + neighbor-list 2-opt, ring searches use the `(r−1)·bucketSide` stopping bound; open form cuts the longest edge) + the `singleLine(of:points:in:iterations:cutoff:closed:)` sugar over the seeded stipple; `cutoff` rounds bright grays to paper (the Kaplan contrast rule — without it light regions collect a wandering thread). **Slit scan** — `SlitScan` class (`push` + `image(delay:)` closure/map forms): rolling CPU frame ring, delay 0 = newest / 1 = oldest held, first push fixes the size (mismatch/GPU-backed frames skipped with a one-time note), memory is w×h×4×frames so push modest sizes. **Halftone** — `drawHalftone(_:pitch:angle:in:inverted:colored:)` + the data form `halftone(of:)` (`[HalftoneDot]`): classic AM dot screening as real circles (45° default screen); coverage is the per-cell mean of (1 − linear luminance)·alpha over **exact rotated-cell pixel binning**, and the radius **inverts the shared `halftoneCoveredFraction` spot function** — the same function the separations' raster screen thresholds (one source of truth in `Image/Halftone.swift`; `PrintSeparation.halftonePlane` calls it), so tone is area-exact and shadow dots merge into the checkered diamonds; the separations' 2% printable-dot clamp applies (below → no dot, above 98% → the corner-flooding `pitch/√2` cap); `pitch` floors at 2 (dot-count guard); dots emit through `drawCircle`/`drawCircles`, so SVG/PDF export writes true circles (the plotter path). **String art** — `StringArt(of:center:radius:pins:chords:ink:minimumSpan:inverted:resolution:)` (`Geometry/StringArt.swift`), a stateful stepper with **no rng at all** (the same picture always winds the same thread): one continuous thread over a ring of pins (pin 0 top, clockwise), each chord the greedy pick by **mean remaining darkness** along it (mean, not sum, so a short dark chord beats a long gray one), the ink then paid down along the very samples the score walked; a pin pair never rewinds, `minimumSpan` (default pins/10) keeps chords off the rim, and the winding stops at the `chords` cap **or** when the best mean drops to `ink/2` (the over-inking floor). The working grid reads the picture's **largest centered square** at `resolution` cells a side, linear-light darkness `(1 - luminance)·alpha`, zero outside the inscribed circle; `inverted` winds luminance instead (bright thread, dark ground). `thread` is one open `Contour` (the single plotter line), `sequence` the pin order (the hand-winding recipe). Bold tonal masses read best; whole-frame soft gradients wind into fuzz. Pair `ink` with the drawn stroke alpha so the solver's pass matches what a chord actually darkens; a caption over a `noClear()` accumulation must keep word-for-word stable text or it smears. The direction of the image-to-grid mapping is pinned by a sabotage-verified seek-the-dark test. All six need CPU pixels (texture-backed images go through `snapshot()` first; `pixelSorted` returns self, the others note once and return empty/nil). The example sketch classes `SlitScan` and `StringArt` shadow their types → `Ollin.SlitScan` / `Ollin.StringArt` (the `@SwiftUI.Environment` precedent). Examples `Images/GlyphMosaic` / `PixelSort` / `SingleLine` / `SlitScan` / `Halftone` / `StringArt`; snapshots `glyph-mosaic` / `pixel-sort` / `single-line` / `slit-scan` / `halftone` / `string-art`; `GlyphMosaicTests` / `PixelSortTests` / `SingleLineTests` / `SlitScanTests` / `HalftoneTests` / `StringArtTests`. `Docs/Drawing/GlyphMosaic.md`, `Docs/Drawing/PixelSorting.md`, `Docs/Generators/SingleLine.md`, `Docs/Video/SlitScan.md`, `Docs/Drawing/Halftone.md`, `Docs/Generators/StringArt.md`.

- **Data as input** (`Sources/Ollin/Data/Table.swift` + `JSON.swift`). The load-once material for data-driven drawing: **`loadTable`** reads CSV/TSV into a **`Table`** (a `RandomAccessCollection` of `Row`s; `row["col"]` / `row[2]` plus `number`/`int`/`bool`/`color` and their `at position:` twins, `column(_:)` / `numbers(_:)`) and **`loadJSON`** reads a document into **`JSON`** (a `@dynamicMemberLookup` enum; reach by key/index then ask `.string`/`.number`/`.int`/`.bool`/`.color`/`.array`/`.object`/`.keys`/`.count`/`.isNull`). Both follow the palette-importer posture: **total decode, `nil` rather than a throw or a trap**, and `resource:in:` with **no default bundle** (the framework-wide rule). **Load-bearing:** the CSV parse is the published format as a **byte scanner** (every byte it compares is ASCII and no byte of a multi-byte UTF-8 character can be mistaken for one, so cell text rides through untouched), and its case order is too: **separator and line endings are tested before the after-closing-quote whitespace case**, since a tab is both a separator and whitespace and the separator reading must win (a TSV silently lost a column otherwise); the **BOM is stripped** or it joins the first column's name invisibly and every lookup of that name fails; a **quoted cell is verbatim while an unquoted one is trimmed** (quoting is how a file says it means the spaces, and the whitespace on either side of the quotes is not part of the cell); the blank-line rule **keys on whether the record held quotes**, because a line holding `""` is a row where a truly blank line and the newline closing the last real row are not (dropping it left a 0-row table that crashed the suite's first `table[0]`). Two things are guessed and **an explicit answer overrides each** (the `palettePerLine: Bool?` precedent): the separator (most frequent of comma/tab/semicolon/pipe on the first line, **outside quotes**) and the header (**a first row holding no numbers**). `numbers(_:)` **drops** non-numbers (a series to scale by) while `column(_:)` **pads to the row count** (stays row-aligned) — the one place the two reads deliberately differ. Rows share **one** name-to-position map by class reference rather than each copying a dictionary, and a repeated column name resolves to the first. JSON: a **missing key reads as `.null`** so a path chains without a check at every joint, `.array`/`.object` are **non-optional** so a loop over an absent key runs zero times, and `true` is told from `1` by **`CFBooleanGetTypeID`** (the system reader hands both back as an `NSNumber` holding 1). Deliberately small: a document with a shape worth naming is `Codable`'s job, and the docs say so. Examples `Data/Readings` (a CSV as a range chart, notes holding commas and doubled quotes) + `Data/Places` (a JSON survey, a station with no color landing on the fallback); `TableTests` / `JSONTests` (no pixel snapshot: both are CPU value types). Guide Ch 9 § *Numbers you didn't type: CSV and JSON* + DataAsMaterial figure. `Docs/Helpers/Data.md`.

- **Audio** (`OllinAudio` satellite). `AudioAnalyzer` (a rolling last-`fftSize` sample window — Hann + vDSP FFT re-run per chunk, so `waveform` is a true rolling window and chunk size never costs resolution) → `amplitude` / `spectrum` / `waveform` / band queries (`bass` / `mid` / `treble` / `magnitude(in:)`), plus the draw-shaped `bands(_:)` (log-spaced, adaptively normalized, attack/release) and beat detection (`beatCount` / `timeSinceBeat` / `beat` / `beatSensitivity`): spectral flux over **log-compressed magnitudes** (gain cancels in the log difference, so the threshold is loudness-invariant), peak-picked with the **online conditions** — trailing local max + an **additive** margin over a trailing mean (multiplicative-only false-fires on steady material — don't regress it) + a sample-counted refractory — and the whole beat surface on the **sample clock** (`timeSinceBeat` = samples, not wall clock), so detection is deterministic + testable. Sources: `AudioInput` (mic), `AudioPlayer` (file), `Tone` (oscillator), `Soundtrack` (any core `AudioTapSource`, i.e. a playing video's audio). **Headless export is deterministic for `AudioPlayer`** (the `HeadlessVideoReader` sibling): under `isRenderingHeadless` it skips the engine and follows the export clock — a `FrameAdvancing` sample playhead feeds each frame's slice of the decoded buffer to the analyzer (analyzer runs at the *file's* rate there, not the mixer's — hardware-independent), so frame `k` reads the file at `k/fps` and audio-reactive exports reproduce byte-identically; create the player by the end of `setup()`. Mic/`Tone`/`Soundtrack` stay live-only. Example `Audio/Spectrum`; `Docs/Helpers/Audio.md`.

- **Synthesis** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/`). The playing side: **`Synth`** (`@MainActor`, `AudioSource`) is a polyphonic instrument a sketch asks for notes (`play(_:velocity:for:)` / `noteOn` / `noteOff` / `allNotesOff` / `play(chord:)`), over the **`Voice`** value type (waveform + `Envelope` + optional `Voice.Filter` + `detune` + `gain`, presets `.pluck`/`.bass`/`.pad`/`.bell`/`.stab`/`.breath`/`.sine`), pitched by **`Pitch`** (`ExpressibleBy{Integer,Float,String}Literal` → `60` / `60.5` / `"C4"`; MIDI as a `Double` so bends and non-equal tunings have somewhere to land), with `Delay` / `Reverb` slots on the output. **Load-bearing:** the **renderer is pure and engine-free** (`SynthRenderer`: events in, buffer out, no clock, no AVFoundation, noise from a per-voice SplitMix), which is what makes the sound testable at all and what a sound-carrying export would be built on — a feature that reaches for the engine from inside it is going the wrong way; the sketch reaches the render thread through a **lock-free SPSC `EventRing`** of trivially-copyable events (preallocated slots, two atomic counters, one written by each end, **drop rather than wait** when full) and continuous values ride **atomics** (`gain` as a `Double` bit pattern, `activeVoiceCount` published once per block) — never a lock and never a plain property, the render-thread rule's audio cousin; the render block is built in a **free function** (the `@MainActor`-closure SIGILL rule); **the effect chain runs in the output's channel layout, not mono** (the units reject a format the hardware end does not use, and the failure is a *thrown exception*, not an error to catch — the bug that killed the example on first run), and the units are **wired once and mixed dry** rather than attached on demand (rebuilding a running graph gaps the sound); **voice stealing takes a fading note before a held one** (so a melody over a chord eats its own earlier notes, pinned by measuring the chord's pitch against the held-melody counterfactual) and a stolen voice gets a **~5 ms fade** while the new note's attack runs **from the level already reached**, which is what keeps both ends of a steal click-free; oscillators are **PolyBLEP-corrected** (triangle by integrating the corrected square at gain `4·dt`, measured peak 0.80…1.10 across 55…3520 Hz) and the filter is the **exact Cytomic trapezoidal SVF** (`g = tan(π·fc/sr)`, `a1 = 1/(1+g(g+k))`, bounded `v1/v2/v3` tick), chosen because a cutoff that moves every sample must stay where it was asked for and stay stable; `Envelope.level(at:heldFor:)` is the **closed form of what is actually played** (pinned against the runner to 0.02, one-sample time base) so a sketch can draw the sound it designed. Deliberate v1 cuts: one voice per `Synth`, no sequencer, no sampler, no 3D placement, no audio in an export. `Tone` folded onto the shared `Waveform` + anti-aliased oscillator (its naive saw used to alias). No pixel snapshot (sound); `SynthTests` (22) pins it behaviorally, and `SynthAudition` renders a listenable WAV under `OLLIN_AUDITION` (a sound cannot be snapshotted, so the audition render is the figure). Example `Audio/Synth`; Guide Ch 27 § *A sketch that plays* + Voices figure; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md`.

- **Algorithmic composition** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Composition/`). The note-deciding half beside synthesis, which shapes the sound: **`Rhythm`** (Bjorklund's even spread as `Rhythm(k, in: n)`, written-out patterns via `ExpressibleByStringLiteral`, `onsets`/`intervals`/`rotated`/`inverted`, 11 named traditional rhythms), **`Scale`** (root + intervals, `Mode` catalog, degrees running both ways *past* the ends, `snap(_:)` for a pitch decided by something that is not music, `chord(on:notes:spacing:)` stacking out of the key), **`Chord`** (root + `Quality` + inversion + `spread(over:)`), **`Arpeggio`** (a pattern over a chord's notes), **`MarkovChain<Element>`** (order-n with backoff, `continuations` reading the learned counts back), **`StepCounter`** (beats in, step numbers out), and **`Note`** (pitch + velocity + length **in beats**, played via `Synth.play(_:tempo:)`). **Load-bearing:** every type **answers a step number and owns no clock**, which is exactly what keeps the tier free of `OllinMIDI` (satellites can't depend on each other) and what lets `time`, a detected beat, and a `TempoClock` drive the same pattern unchanged; randomness is **seeded per object, never the sketch's `rng`** (`Arpeggio.random` is a pure function of step + seed, the halton/brush rule; `MarkovChain` keeps its own generator, rewound by `reset()`); the Markov table is a `Dictionary` but **every decision walks an array** (successors in first-seen order, `continuations` tie-broken by that order) so a seeded walk can never depend on hash order — the determinism rule; **`.upDown`/`.downUp` drop both ends of the way back** (repeating the turnaround reads as a stutter, pinned across counts 2…6) and **`.asPlayed` is the only pattern that does not sort** (a hand-made voicing is otherwise silently re-laddered); `StepCounter` returns a **`Range`** because a frame is longer than a step at any real tempo, fires **step 0 on the first call** (a pattern starts on the downbeat), **re-fires the landing step when time goes backwards** (a loop's downbeat), and **jumps rather than emptying the pattern into one frame** past `maxCatchUp` (the `maxTimestep` precedent); **`Chord.inversion` must not wrap** (a full turn is the chord an octave up, a negative one drops notes from the top — the mod-only form silently returned the original). Bjorklund is written from the paper's construction, and where its two notations for E(7,16) disagree the **gap vector is the one the construction gives** and the one Ollin follows (`Rhythm`'s maximal evenness — gaps taking at most two lengths differing by one — is property-tested over every k,n up to 32). `Scale.Mode` / `Chord.Quality` / `Arpeggio.Pattern` are `ParamOption`s, so a key or a figure is a live knob. Deliberate v1 cuts: no sequencer (it would need a clock, which is the one thing this tier refuses), no chord-symbol parsing, no chord progressions, no microtonal scales. No pixel snapshot (pure CPU values); `CompositionTests` (42, twin-pair based) + `CompositionAudition` (a 40 s piece rendered through the engine-free renderer). Example `Audio/Generative`; Guide Ch 27 § *Music the sketch works out for itself* + Euclidean figure; `Docs/Helpers/Composition.md`.

- **Listening: speech and sound events** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Listening/`). The audio sibling of the vision trackers, and the tier's *input* half beside synthesis: **`SpeechListener`** turns talking into words and **`SoundClassifier`** names everyday sounds, both binding to any **`AudioTapSource`** the way a tracker binds to any `FrameSource`. **No new core seam** (the shipped `AudioTapSource` is exactly right); what was added is conformance on `AudioInput` / `AudioPlayer` / `Tone` (an `AudioTapRelay` rides the existing analyzer tap, forwarding the **whole** buffer as mono, since an analyzer keeps one windowful but a listener transcribing words needs every sample) plus an internal **`AudioTapHub`**, one per source (the `SourceAnalyzers` mirror), which owns the single tap slot and fans out weakly, so a `Soundtrack` + a `SpeechListener` + a `SoundClassifier` over one microphone coexist where the old direct-slot grab silently unseated the previous consumer (`Soundtrack` now routes through it and releases the slot when the last listener goes). **Speech is the modern on-device `SpeechAnalyzer`/`SpeechTranscriber`, not `SFSpeechRecognizer`**, which settles the permission question the design note guessed at: **probed, a bare `swift run` binary transcribes with `authorizationStatus == .notDetermined` and no prompt** (fully on-device, no TCC consent for recognition; the microphone keeps its own, attributed to the launching terminal as always). **The result surfaces are picked per question, not inherited:** speech gives `caption` (the running guess, tail included, trimmed to `captionWords`) for *drawing*, `transcript` + the draining `phrases()` for *acting* (the whole point: an uncommitted result **replaces** the tail because that is what the recognizer is doing to it, so a trigger on `caption` acts on a word that can be taken back); classification gives a **level** (`confidence(of:)` / `top` / `classifications`) beside a **trigger** (draining `events()` + non-draining `timeSinceHearing(_:)`, both on the sample clock), where an event is a **crossing from below** so a continuing sound fires once and not once per window. **Load-bearing:** the recognizer takes a moment to start and audio arriving first is **held, not dropped** (a bounded 5 s pre-roll flushed in order, since the first words into a microphone are the ones worth hearing) with `isListening` the read for it, distinct from `isAvailable` (which is true until something is *wrong*, model install included); both listeners are **live only** and say so under `OllinApp.isRenderingHeadless` rather than sitting silently empty, with the **deterministic one-shots** (`SpeechListener.transcribe` / `SoundClassifier.classify` over samples / URL / `resource:in:`, no default bundle) the way into an export, reached from `setup()` through a new no-argument **`waitFor { }`** (the `OllinVision` sibling; parks the caller, so never from an async context and never touching the sketch inside the closure); the classifier runs off the audio thread on its own serial queue (drop past 4 queued, a classifier being far slower than the audio it judges) while speech converts off it and **drops nothing** (a dropped block is a missing word); a sample rate of 0 must be rejected before `SNAudioStreamAnalyzer`, which **throws an uncatchable ObjC exception** on a zero-rate format (a real crash the tests caught). **`windowDuration` defaults to 1.5 s on measurement, not by feel**: swept 0.5...3 s against synthesized tones and taps, **1.0 s is the one setting where a click train reads as a synthesizer** (0.60) while 1.5 s keeps taps at `click` 0.74 and silence under 0.21. A user's own Core ML classifier is in scope (`model:` / `modelAt:` an `.mlmodelc`, the `ModelTracker` precedent + the stable-compiled-path gotcha). Deliberate cuts: no per-word confidence, no speaker separation, no wake word, no `@Param` binding (a confidence is already a plain `draw()` read; `@Smoothed` if it jitters). No pixel snapshot (a sound cannot be snapshotted): `ListeningTests` (18) run over audio the tests synthesize, each claim against a counterfactual, and the two end-to-end speech checks **speak to themselves** through `AVSpeechSynthesizer.write` so nothing recorded is committed. **Gotcha that made both speech tests pass vacuously at first: the synthesizer delivers on the main queue, so the caller must let the main thread go** (await in an async context, pump the run loop in a plain synchronous one like a figure's `setup()`); blocking on a semaphore collects zero frames and the test soft-skips silently. Example `Audio/Listening`; Guide Ch 27 § *Words, and what that noise was* + the Listening figure (`figure: unstable`, both models being the system's: four real transcriptions of growing prefixes of one spoken sentence, the third of which is confidently wrong, beside three arithmetic sounds and what the classifier called them); `Docs/Helpers/Listening.md`.

- **Sonification** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Composition/Sonification.swift`). Numbers read out as notes, the reading half beside the deciding half: **`Sonification`** is a value type spreading a series over a pitch range, snapped through a `Scale`, read by `[step]` / `note(at:)` / `notes()` / `pitch(for:)` — it **answers a step number and owns no clock** like the rest of the tier, so `StepCounter` drives it. Four sources, one call each: `[Double]`, `Table` column, `Heightfield` `row:`/`column:`/`from:to:count:`, `Image` `row:`/`column:` (luminance). Knobs: `in scale:`, `pitches:` (default `"C3"..."C6"`), `bounds:` (`.extremes` / `.robust(ignoring:)` / `.fixed`), `polarity:`, `length:` (beats), plus `amplified(by:)` / `inverted()` / `reference(at:)`. Written from the published technique (Grond & Berger, *The Sonification Handbook* ch. 15; credited in `ATTRIBUTION.md`, never in `.swift`). **Load-bearing:** pitch is spread **linearly in MIDI note number, never in hertz** (hearing is logarithmic, so an even-in-hertz spread crushes the bottom half of the data into the top of the range — the figure draws both, and `pitchIsSpreadEvenlyInSemitonesNotInHertz` pins the two mappings *far apart* so the test can't pass by accident), and loudness **linearly in decibels** (`velocity = 10^(dB/20)`, so the floor is a real fraction rather than silence) — both are the perceptually linear units, which is what makes a linear transfer function correct; **loudness does nothing unless asked** (`loudness == nil` → every note at full level) because how loud something sounds depends on how high it is, so it is for a *second* series, not for saying the same thing twice; `.robust` uses **interpolated quantiles** (one outlier at 1000× otherwise flattens a whole series into one note — pinned against its `.extremes` twin at span <1 vs >30 semitones); values outside the domain are **held at the ends**, a flat series reads at the **middle** (no divide by zero), and an empty one plays nothing; the **reference tone** is the published grid-line idea (without it a listener needs absolute pitch for a note to mean anything) and lands exactly where that value would in the reading. No rng anywhere, so a reading is a pure function and an export repeats. `SonificationTests` (20, exact values + twins); `SonificationAudition` (four takes of one landscape row: chromatic / snapped / inverted / with a reference). Example `Audio/Sonification` (a terrain profile drawn and read out at once, playhead on the sounding note); Guide Ch 27 § *Numbers you can hear: sonification* + Sonification figure; `Docs/Helpers/Sonification.md`.

- **Composition polish: progressions, tunings, following a beat** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Composition/Progression.swift` + `ChordSymbol.swift` + `Tuning.swift` + `BeatFollower.swift`). Four of the five gaps the Composition page listed as *not here yet*, all pure values answering a step number and owning no clock like the rest of the tier. **`Progression`** is a cycle of chords **written as scale degrees, not names** (roman numerals `I`…`VII`, case ignored, any separator), so the qualities fall out of the key and the same four numbers are major or minor with nothing changed; `pitches(at:)` / `degree(at:)` / `root(at:)` / `chord(at:)` / `transposed` / `rotated`, named set (`.pop` / `.fifties` / `.twoFiveOne` / `.blues` / `.andalusian` / `.circleOfFifths`), and **`wandering(_:order:seed:)`** over a `MarkovChain` learned **round the loop** (`loops: true`, so the last chord returning to the first is a move) and **seeded per call, never the sketch's rng**. **`Chord(symbol:)`** + `ExpressibleByStringLiteral` parses chord symbols (root / accidentals / quality / `/bass`); **load-bearing: the quality is tried on the whole suffix *before* a trailing number is read as an octave**, or `Cmaj7` strips the 7 and becomes major in the wrong register (the bug the tests caught), and a bad symbol is **nil** from `init(symbol:)` while the literal notes once and lands on C major (the `Pitch` precedent). `Progression(symbols:)` / `Progression([Chord])` set the parallel **`written`** store, so a progression is *either* degrees-in-a-key *or* named chords, and `count` / `pitches` / `root` / `rotated` / `transposed` honor both. **`Tuning`** is the `Scale` sibling for pitches described by **ratios and a period** rather than semitones (`[degree]` / `snap` / `cents` / `frequency` / `rooted(at:)`, `.equal(_:period:)` + `.just` / `.pythagorean` / `.quarterTones` / `.nineteen` / `.thirtyOne` / `.bohlenPierce`): ratios fold into one period and dedupe within 0.01 cent, degrees run past both ends, and because `Pitch.midi` is a `Double` a non-twelve degree simply lands between the keys. **Deliberate: `Scale` is untouched and stays twelve-tone** (a tuning is a separate type, not a setting, so nothing existing changed) and chords are not built out of one. **`BeatFollower`** + the pure **`BeatEngine`** (the `TempoClock`/`TempoEngine` split: onset times in, musical time out, testable by handing it a beat) turn detected onsets into `beats` a `StepCounter` runs on, plus `tempo` / `steadiness` / `rhythm(steps:)`. **Load-bearing:** a tempo outside the believable range is **halved or doubled until it lands inside** (a detector firing on every eighth reports twice the tempo, which is the same music), the tempo is the **middle of the recent gaps, never the average** (one missed beat doubles a gap, which moves an average and not a middle), and the position **re-anchors to the nearest whole beat** on each onset so a missed one costs nothing. `ProgressionTests` (31, exact values + twins) + `ProgressionAudition`. Example `Audio/Changes`; Guide Ch 27 §§ *Chords that come out of a key* / *Twelve is a choice: tunings* / *Playing along with the room: tempo sync*; `Docs/Helpers/Composition.md`.

- **Physical models: the plucked string** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/PluckedString.swift` + `StringVoice.swift`). A voice worked out rather than drawn: **`VoiceSource`** (`.wave(Waveform)` / `.string(PluckedString)`) is now what a `Voice` is built from, with **`waveform` kept as a computed view over it** so every existing call site, preset, doc, and example is unchanged; **`PluckedString`** carries the four controls a player has (`pick` / `hardness` / `decay` / `damping`) with presets `.nylon` / `.steel` / `.harp` / `.muted` (and `Voice` presets of the same names), and `Envelope.plucked` is the stay-out-of-the-way gate the string's own fade runs under. The render side is the extended Karplus-Strong loop: delay line → one-zero loss filter → first-order tuning allpass → loop gain. **Load-bearing:** the loop must come out **exactly one period long**, so the loop filter's own delay (`shade`, which *is* its coefficient) is subtracted from the budget *before* the integer split and the allpass supplies the rest — which is why **`damping` cannot move the pitch** (pinned by a twin pair differing only in `damping`; the analytic `soundingFrequency` is within 0.01 cents across MIDI 21…105 where rounding the loop would be **>40 cents** out at the top); the integer part is `floor(remaining − 0.2)` so the fraction lands in **[0.2, 1.2)**, the range where a first-order allpass is well behaved (a fraction near zero puts its pole on the unit circle); the **excitation is built in a scratch half of the buffer**, because the pick-position comb reads the burst it is overwriting, and the comb wraps **circularly** so its notches land *on* the harmonics rather than near them; **the burst must be written where the first read will reach it** (`write = count`, not 0 — leaving the write head at the start points it at the empty tail and the string is silent, the bug that shipped nothing at all until a probe caught it); velocity multiplies into the excitation's bandwidth as well as its height (playing harder is brighter, the dynamic-level filter's actual musical job); every string's delay line is **one allocation taken in `SynthRenderer.init`** (raw memory, the `EventRing` precedent) sized by `lowestStringFrequency` (16 Hz), since the render thread may not allocate, and a note below it plays sharp rather than outrunning the buffer; `detune` gives a second string, symmetric with the oscillator path. `SynthEvent` stays trivially copyable (`PluckedString` is four `Double`s), which is what lets a voice change ride the event ring. No pixel snapshot (sound); `PluckedStringTests` (11, twin-pair based: pick position vs the even harmonics, hardness and velocity vs the spectral centroid measured **at the note's own harmonics** rather than on a fixed grid, decay vs level at one second, damping measured **above 4 kHz** because a one-zero loss removes almost nothing near the fundamental) + `PhysicalModelAudition`. Example `Audio/Strings` (click a string where you want to pluck it; the drawn shape is the modal sum that pluck excites, which is the same fact as the comb); Guide Ch 27 § *A string, worked out rather than drawn: the plucked string* + PluckedString figure; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#physical-models`.

- **Physical models: the struck shape** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/ModalBody.swift` + `ModalShape.swift` + `ModalVoice.swift`). Modal synthesis, and the reason it earns its place: **`StruckShape(_ shape: Shape, modes:resolution:)`** measures a drawn outline and **`body(struckAt:decay:damping:hardness:)`** hands back a **`ModalBody`** (`.drum` / `.plate` / `.bar` / `.bell` / `.wood` / `.glass`; `Voice` presets `.drum` / `.bar` / `.chime` / `.glass`), so a shape a sketch drew rings at the frequencies its own geometry allows. The frequencies are the **Dirichlet eigenvalues of the Laplacian on the shape** (a membrane held at its rim), solved by measuring the outline onto a grid then banded Cholesky + subspace iteration with Rayleigh-Ritz + cyclic Jacobi on the small projected problem, all written here (no LAPACK interface). **Load-bearing:** the model is **verified against physics, not itself** — a circle returns the Bessel-zero ratios (1, 1.593, 2.136, 2.295…) and a square √((m²+n²)/2) to under 0.5%, which is what `ModalBodyTests` pins; a **symmetric shape returns its tones twice** (a pattern that fits at one rotation fits at another) and that is correct, not an artifact, so **a gain readback must sum over a degenerate pair** (which member takes the strike is arbitrary; the sum is the invariant, and the first test draft failed exactly here); `ratios` come out **scale-free** (the grid spacing cancels), so size never changes the instrument, only the note does; **`ModalBody` stores its modes in `SIMD16<Double>` pairs, not an array**, because `Voice` rides `SynthEvent` through the lock-free ring and a heap reference would refcount on the render thread (hence `maxModes = 16`, and `modes` is a computed view); a tone landing **above Nyquist is dropped, never folded** (it would arrive as something that was never struck); each resonator's input is scaled by **sin(its own angle)** so every tone comes out at the gain asked for rather than at whatever its frequency gives, and the strike is divided by the summed gains so sixteen tones are not sixteen times as loud; the excitation is a **raised-cosine push whose width is `hardness`** (a wide one reaches only the low tones); **`resolution` defaults to 40 because accuracy plateaus there** (measured: 28 → 1.11% error, 36 → 0.34%, 64 → 0.22%, at 0.9 s / 1.7 s / 8.8 s in a **debug** build and 0.01…0.07 s in release), and the solver's hot loops run over **raw pointers on flat storage** for exactly the unoptimized-build reason the point-cloud work documents. **`StruckShape` exists because striking must be free** — it keeps the eigenvectors so a new strike point is a lookup, where `ModalBody(shape:)` re-measures every call (pinned equal to each other). No pixel snapshot (sound); `ModalBodyTests` (19) + `PhysicalModelAudition`. Example `Audio/StruckShapes`; Guide Ch 27 § *A shape you can hit: modal synthesis* + StruckShapes figure; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#physical-models`.

- **Sampled instruments** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/SampledInstrument.swift` + `SFZ.swift` + `SamplerVoice.swift`). The one source that is recorded rather than worked out: **`Synth.instrument`** holds a `SampledInstrument` and `Voice(sampled: Sampled)` says how to play it, read from **SFZ** (the text format free libraries ship in) or from `Recording`s in hand. **Load-bearing:** the recordings live on the **`Synth`, never inside the `Voice`** — a `Voice` rides `SynthEvent` through the lock-free ring and must be copyable a word at a time (the constraint that caps `ModalBody` at 16 and `Patch` at 8), and megabytes of heap cannot ride along, so `Sampled` carries only numbers (`_isPOD` pinned) and the instrument is set separately; the read head is **linearly interpolated** (nearest-sample stepping is an audible grainy edge on every note not at its recorded pitch, which is most of them) and moves at `2^(semitones/12) × recordedRate/playRate`, so pitch and length move together like a tape; **the nearest recording wins** where several cover a note (moving a recording far is the one way a sampler audibly goes wrong and the one thing choosing well avoids), and a note outside every range is **stretched from the nearest rather than silent**; an unknown SFZ opcode is **skipped, not refused** (the format has hundreds this does not model, and a library using them should still load), a missing audio file costs that part of the range not the instrument, `<group>`/`<global>` opcodes are inherited, note names read either way round (`60` or `f#3`), and Windows backslash paths are turned round; **the renderer stops applying velocity to a sampled voice** because `velocitySensitivity` is the sampler's own setting and would otherwise be scaled twice (0 = as loud as recorded, for an instrument whose recordings are already its dynamics) — scoped to `.sampled`, every other source unchanged. **The bundled instrument is generated, not sourced** (`Scripts/make-sample-instrument.swift`, a free-free bar's inharmonic ratios at five pitches, 384 KB, `.copy` resource): Ollin's own, no third-party licence, no `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` entry. Docs list where to get real libraries **with their licence tiers** — CC0 (VCSL, VSCO 2 CE, Iowa) bundlable, Freesound per-clip, and Philharmonia free to make music with but explicitly **not** redistributable as a sampler instrument. `SamplerTests` (16). Example `Audio/Sampler` (five recordings against one stretched, which is not a subtle difference); Guide Ch 27 § *An instrument somebody recorded*; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#sampled-instruments`.

- **Effects as a chain** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/Effect.swift` + the wiring in `Synth.swift`/`SynthExport.swift`). The output side of routing-as-a-value, closing the symmetry the patch tier opened: **`Synth.effects: [Effect]`** is an ordered chain (`.delay` / `.reverb` / `.equalizer(Equalizer)` / `.distortion(Distortion)`) replacing the two fixed slots. **Load-bearing:** **order is the feature** (a distorted echo and an echo of a distorted sound are different, pinned by exporting both and diffing); **`delay`/`reverb` survive as computed views over the chain** (read the first of that kind, write **replaces in place** or appends, nil removes — replacing must not walk an effect down the chain, pinned) so every existing sketch is untouched; the graph rebuilds **only when the sequence of kinds changes** (`wiredKinds`), so a setting change never disturbs the wiring; the rebuild happens **on the running engine without stopping it**, which is a *measured* decision, not an assumption — probe: reconnecting while running throws nothing and costs 1 sample, stopping first costs the same 1, and a pure knob change also shows 1, so the "gap" is the test tone's zero crossing and there is no gap at all (this reverses the old "wire once and mix dry, rebuilding a running graph gaps the sound" comment, which was never measured); all four unit kinds chain at the output format without throwing (probed, since AVFoundation refuses formats by *throwing at connect time*); the live and placed paths now share **one `rebuildEffectChain()`** hanging off `chainHead` (the listener once placed, the source otherwise), and **`OfflineRender` builds the same chain from the same list** or an export would run different effects from the ones the sketch was heard through (the stage-1 lesson). `Delay`/`Reverb` gained `Codable` and `Synth.configure` became `nonisolated` so an `Effect` can be a value. Deliberate cuts: the chain is per instrument, not per note, and there is no seam for an effect a sketch wrote itself. `EffectChainTests` (13). Example `Audio/Patching` (the finish knob, whose last two options differ only in order); Guide Ch 27 § *And the rest of the instrument*; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#effects`.

- **The patchable graph** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/Patch.swift` + `PatchVoice.swift`). Routing as a value: **`Patch`** is an operator graph (`.tone(_:ratio:level:)` / `.modulated(by:index:)` / `.mixed(with:)` / `.fedBack(_:)` / `.at(level:)` / `.at(ratio:)`, presets `.bell` / `.brass` / `.glass` / `.buzz` / `.struck`), reached as `VoiceSource.patch` + `Voice(patch:)` + `Voice.fmBell` / `.fmBrass` / `.fmBuzz`. **The bare form is untouched** — the presets sit on this the way the bare draw calls sit on `Drawer`. **Load-bearing:** the shipped constraint (a `Voice` rides `SynthEvent` through the lock-free ring, which is why `ModalBody` caps at 16) is met **the same way** — a patch is **fixed lanes, capped at 8 operators**, `SIMD8` throughout with an output **bitmask**, so it stays POD (`_isPOD(Patch/VoiceSource/Voice/SynthEvent)` pinned); past the cap it **returns the patch unchanged and notes**, never silently drops an operator (a patch with a piece missing is a different instrument); **`modulated(by:)` puts the modulator's lanes FIRST** so every modulator sits in an earlier lane than what it pushes and **one forward pass** evaluates the graph (pinned: every `modulatedBy` index < its own lane, at any build depth); an operator's **`level` is the mix level where it is heard and the modulation index where it is not**, which is the FM convention and why no extra lane is needed; the sum is divided by the number of output operators so mixing does not double the level. `PatchVoice` keeps **phases in `SIMD8` lanes, never an array of `Oscillator`** — an array is a reference the voice struct carries and a voice is copied at note start, which would refcount on the render thread. **`waveformSample` was extracted** from `Oscillator.next` so both read the shapes through one function (no drift), and `Oscillator.next` gained a **`phaseOffset` defaulting to 0** that moves where the shape is *read* without moving the phase — byte-identity pinned by `aSingleOperatorPatchIsExactlyAnOscillator` across four waveforms, plus every non-patch preset still rendering. Deliberate v1 cut: a patch is oscillators only (no per-operator filter/envelope/effect; those stay the `Voice` around it). `Waveform` conforms to `ParamOption` alongside. `PatchTests` (16). Example `Audio/Patching`; Guide Ch 27 § *Building an instrument instead of choosing one*; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#patch`.

- **Driven physical models: the bowed string and the blown tube** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/DrivenModels.swift` + `DrivenVoice.swift`). The other half of the model tier: a pluck and a strike are **set going once**, a bow and a breath are **kept going**, so a note has a middle. `VoiceSource` gains `.bowed(BowedString)` / `.blown(BlownTube)` (+ `isDriven`), `Voice` gains `bowed:` / `blown:` inits + presets (`.violin` / `.cello` / `.bowed` / `.clarinet` / `.reed` / `.hollow`), `Envelope.sustained` is the stay-out-of-the-way gate, and **`Synth.drive`** (`0…1`) is the continuous control: an **atomic Double bit pattern like `gain`**, read once per block, shared by every note (one bow, one breath). Both models are digital waveguides over the shipped delay-line machinery; credited in `ATTRIBUTION.md` (Smith's PASP, McIntyre/Schumacher/Woodhouse 1983, Smith 1986), never in `.swift`. **Load-bearing:** the bow is the published scattering junction (`vΔ = vb − (fromNut + fromBridge)`, `toBridge = fromNut + ρ(vΔ)·vΔ`, `toNut = fromBridge + ρ(vΔ)·vΔ`) over **two** lines whose round trips sum to one period, split at the bow position; the friction curve is **flat while stuck then falls as a cube**, and **bow speed against the slip threshold is the whole regime** — below ~1 the string is caught and released once a cycle and the spectrum is the 1/n Helmholtz sawtooth (measured cello `[1.00 0.51 0.34 0.25 0.19 0.10]`), above it the string tears loose twice a cycle and **jumps to the octave**, which is real over-bowing, not a bug (force widens the threshold, so a light bow can be over-driven and a heavy one stays solid); **`stickingGrip` is 0.9, deliberately short of a perfect hold** (at exactly 1 the stuck bow is a lossless reflector, so the note settles against the threshold and bowing faster changes nothing — even at 0.9 the drive range only spans ~8 dB, so **force is the loudness knob and drive the expression one**, pinned at >1.5× rather than at the speed ratio); the tube's **round trip is half a period, not a whole one** (stopped at the reed, open at the bell), which is the single line that produces odd-harmonics-only (measured `[1.00 0.01 0.28 0.01 0.12 0.00]`) and puts it an octave and a fifth below an open pipe; the reed reflection is **derived from its two physical limits** (shut = rigid termination, open = mouthpiece held at breath pressure) which expands exactly to the published form, so nothing was copied; **both loops carry a tuning allpass and only one side may round** (the bow's nut side rounds and the bridge side takes the remainder, the tube's bore takes the fraction) or the loop is up to half a sample out, which is nothing low down and most of a semitone at the top — pinned <1 cent across MIDI 33…93 at four bow positions and three dampings; **`drive` is ignored by every non-driven source** (byte-identical at drive 0 vs 1, pinned), and both structs stay plain Doubles so they still ride `SynthEvent` through the lock-free ring. **Deliberate cut: no jet-driven tube.** A flute jet was built and **declined on measurement** — it sat at a DC operating point where the cubic's slope is negative, so the loop was far too lossy to oscillate and the output was identical (rms 0.0363) across every jet delay; two attempts, then stopped per the anti-thrash rule rather than keep patching. `DrivenModelTests` (16, twin pairs) + `DrivenModelAudition` (9 takes, drive shaped through each). Example `Audio/Bowing`; Guide Ch 27 § *A note you keep playing: bowed and blown*; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#physical-models`.

- **Spatial audio and sound in an export** (`Sources/OllinAudio/Synthesis/Spatial.swift` + `SynthExport.swift` + the core seam `Sources/Ollin/Core/ExportAudioSource.swift`). **`synth.place(at:heardFrom:)`** puts an instrument in the 3D scene with a `Camera3D` as the listener (`position` / `hearingRange` / `unplace()`), and **`--export-video` / `--export-loop` carry the sketch's own music into the file** with nothing to switch on. **Load-bearing (spatial):** placing is a *different graph*, not a setting, so the first `place` **rewires** (a source node's channel count is fixed at construction, so the stereo node is replaced by a mono one); the **environment node must come first**, before the effects (a mono chain *through* `AVAudioUnitDelay`/`Reverb` throws `-10868` at connect time, probe-verified, the stage-1 format lesson again), and the 3D position rides the **source node** (`AVAudioSourceNode` conforms to `AVAudioMixing`; the effect units do not). **Load-bearing (export):** the core cannot depend on a satellite, so `ExportAudioSource` is a `package` seam in `Ollin` that `Sketch` finds by Mirror the way it finds `FrameAdvancing` values; under `isRenderingHeadless` a `Synth`'s notes are **written down rather than played** (`emit` routes to `record`, `start()` no-ops) against a clock advanced by `advance(by:)`, and a note's length rides in **seconds not samples** so the file renders at its own rate; the soundtrack replays through the *same* clock-free `SynthRenderer` inside an `AVAudioEngine` in **offline manual-rendering mode** (which is what lets the delay and reverb render offline too), chunked so a note starts on the exact sample it was asked for. **The interleaving trap, which cost a session:** `AVAssetWriter` paces file-mode inputs in ~1 s chunks and **holds every other track until the lagging one delivers its chunk** — and this soundtrack *cannot* lead by a second, because the notes for those frames have not been asked for yet, so the writer held video forever at ~frame 67 and the export looked like a hang (it also hung with **zero notes played**, since declaring the track is enough). The fix is three parts, all load-bearing: the audio input is **`expectsMediaDataInRealTime = true`** (exempt from chunk gating), audio is pumped **before** each video frame so it never lags, and the video wait is **bounded and fails loudly** rather than spinning. Verified: 1200-frame `--export-loop` completes in 19.8 s and two runs decode **bit-identically** (max sample difference 0.0). Envelope: GIF carries none, and an instrument must be a sketch property to be found. `SoundExportTests` (8). Examples `Audio/Spatial` + `Audio/SoundInAnExport`; Guide Ch 27 § *Spatial audio, and sound you can keep*; `Docs/Helpers/Synthesis.md#placing-a-sound` + `#sound-in-an-export` + `Docs/Output/Export.md#sound`. **Placing carries into the export** (`RecordedPose`/`ListenerPose` in `Spatial.swift`, the environment node in `OfflineRender`): `place(at:heardFrom:)` under `isRenderingHeadless` writes the pose down against the export clock instead of rewiring, and the soundtrack renders through an `AVAudioEnvironmentNode` in manual rendering mode (probe-verified: it spatializes offline, distance attenuates on the inverse law, and the source→env→delay→reverb chain connects without throwing). **Load-bearing:** the environment node is built **only for an instrument that recorded a pose**, so an unplaced export keeps its own chain, and because a chain's shape is fixed when it is built, a pose arriving after that **notes loudly** rather than silently centering (the one degrade that stays); poses **collapse to changes only** (a sketch calls `place` every frame, so a still instrument costs one pose, not one per frame, pinned at 1 vs 600); a note breaks the render block but **a pose rides to the next block boundary** (an onset is a transient and must land on its own sample, a position is not, and a block is a fifth of a frame); `.auto` is the algorithm both paths ask for, and with no output device it resolves to a plain left/right rather than the head model (HRTF works offline but needs ~0.5 s to settle, measured, so it would wreck a short export); the hearing range lives on the `Synth` (`placementRange`), not the placement, so an export can read it without a live environment node existing. **Fixed alongside, a real shipped defect:** the offline chain built its source at **mono** and fed a stereo mixer, so every exported soundtrack was **entirely in the left channel with the right at exact digital silence** (measured L rms 0.0657 / R rms 0.0 on the shipped code); an unplaced instrument is now built at the output's channel count the way the live path builds it, and the render block writes its one stream into both channels. `SpatialExportTests` (12, counterfactual twins: left-vs-right, near-vs-far, listener-moved, unplace-recenters, a walk crossing the ears measured as **balance rather than level** since a voice's own envelope otherwise reads as the crossing) + `SpatialAudition`.

- **OSC** (`OllinOSC` satellite). OSC 1.0 wire format written from spec (`OSCArgument` / `OSCMessage` / `OSCBundle` / `OSCTimeTag` / `OSCPacket`, total `init?(data:)` decode — malformed datagrams return `nil`, never trap) over UDP (`OSCSender` / `OSCReceiver`). Three read surfaces: latest-value cache (`float` / `int` / `string` / `bool`), event drain (`messages()`), and `@Param` binding (`bind("/addr", to: $param, from:)`). Example `Integration/OSCLoopback`; `Docs/Integration/OSC.md`.

- **DMX lighting** (`OllinDMX` satellite). Art-Net 4 + sACN (ANSI E1.31-2018) written from spec over `Network.framework` UDP, the OSC recipe applied to lighting: total-decode `ArtDmxPacket` / `SACNDataPacket` (golden-byte-pinned against both specs' published tables), **`DMXUniverse`** (512 channels, **1-based** like every console; out-of-range reads 0 / writes ignored, never a trap) + **`DMXFixture`** named layouts (`.rgb` / `.rgbw` / `.drgb` / `.dimmer` presets + role lists, `nextAddress` chaining; RGBW takes the min-subtract white split, and a dimmerless fixture scales its color so `dimmer:` means brightness either way), **`DMXSender`** (`DMXSender()` = sACN multicast to the per-universe `239.255.hi.lo` group — the zero-config default; `sACN:` / `artNet:` unicast forms — Art-Net 4 *forbids* ArtDmx broadcast) and **`DMXReceiver`** (latest-value reads + `@Param` binding 0…255 → range; **no event drain by design** — DMX is levels, not events; `start(universes:)` joins sACN multicast groups via `NWConnectionGroup`). **Load-bearing:** the wire cadence lives in the pure **`DMXPacer`** (send-on-change capped at 44 Hz = a DMX512 gateway's max refresh, the E1.31 three-repeat tail after a change, then ~0.9 s keep-alives — so a sketch just calls `send` every frame and the pacer is unit-tested clock-in-hand); **`close()` must wait for its sACN goodbyes** (3 stream-terminated packets per universe; cancelling the `NWConnection` immediately drops the still-queued sends — a real test-caught bug, fixed with a completion-counted wait); receiver ingest applies the standard's rules (highest priority wins under the 2.5 s source timeout, the signed-8-bit sequence window `≤0 ∧ >−20` dropped, preview data + alternate START codes ignored, a terminate clears the universe) and `handle(_:)` is internal so tests feed crafted datagrams with no sockets; ArtDmx sequence wraps 1…255 skipping 0 (0 = disabled) and its port-address packs Net(7):SubNet(4):Universe(4), valid 1…32767. Envelope (stated in the docs, not faked): streaming tier only — no ArtPoll/ArtPollReply, no sACN discovery/sync packets, no RDM; multicast *receive* is best-effort until hand-verified against a real console (loopback tests are unicast). The spec-required "Art-Net™ Designed by and Copyright Artistic Licence" credit lives in `Docs/Integration/DMX.md` + `ATTRIBUTION.md` — keep it. **LED mapping** (`LEDMap` in `OllinDMX` over the core **`CanvasSampler`** seam, `Sources/Ollin/Renderer/CanvasSampler.swift`): strips (polyline/ring) / matrices / loose points laid over the canvas, sampled each frame and sent as universes. **Load-bearing:** sampling is a **small GPU compute pass on the rendered-texture extension hook** (`wantsRenderedTexture`; never the full-frame CPU readback) — `CanvasSampler` is `package`-access (satellite-seam tier, same package), compiles its kernel from an **inline source string outside the segment concatenation** (plain `float4`/`uchar4` buffers, no shared struct to drift), averages each point's box patch **in linear light** and re-encodes to **display (sRGB) bytes** (radius 0 round-trips the stored byte exactly; the sRGB-space average is the pinned counterfactual), clamps off-canvas points to the edge texel, and is synchronous (own queue + wait — the Syphon cross-queue hazard rule); universes pack **whole LEDs** (a straddling LED jumps whole to the next universe's channel 1 — 170 RGB / 128 RGBW per universe, the pixel-controller convention) and a run spans consecutive universes from its start; strip LEDs space **by walked length** (endpoints included; `closed:` spaces i/N around the loop, no doubled seam); a matrix defaults to **straight rows** (pixel controllers are configured with the panel's wiring and own the zigzag — `serpentine: true` only for direct-wired panels); default `sampleRadius` = the patch the LED stands for (half spacing / half of the smaller cell side, banded 1…32); colors land **through the fixture path** (`DMXUniverse.set(_:color:dimmer:)`), so RGBW white-split and master `brightness` ride the shipped rules; `Fixture.address(ofLED:)` is the wire readback a drawn preview uses; **live-only** (headless exports never fire the texture hook, the frame-sharing precedent). Example `Integration/LEDMapping` (loopback: strip in wire order + panel lit from *received* universes); `CanvasSamplerTests` (Metal-gated) + `LEDMapTests`; mechanism in `ARCHITECTURE.md` § *The canvas sample pass*. Example `Integration/DMXLoopback` (both ends on loopback, the drawn stage lit from *received* channels); `DMXWireTests` / `DMXReceiverTests` / `DMXLoopbackTests`. `Docs/Integration/DMX.md`.

- **MIDI** (`OllinMIDI` satellite). MIDI 1.0 written from spec (`MIDIMessage` incl. `.songPosition`, total `init?(status:data1:data2:)` / `init?(umpWord:)`) over the modern Core MIDI UMP API (`MIDIInput` / `MIDIOutput`, virtual source via `openVirtual`). Read surfaces: control cache, held-note state, `messages()` drain, `@Param` binding. **`@Param` smoothing** (`.eased` / `.smoothed`) lives on the param, so a softened knob applies to MIDI, OSC, and inspector drag alike (rides the per-frame advance pass with `@Eased` / `@Smoothed`). **Tempo sync** — `TempoClock(from: input)` follows MIDI clock into musical time (`tempo` / `beats` / `beatCount` / `phase` / `beat` decaying pulse / `bar` / `barPhase` over declared `beatsPerBar` / `progress(over:)`, the `loopProgress` sibling). **Load-bearing:** *position counts ticks* (each F8 is exactly 1/24 beat — the grid can't drift); the smoothed interval window (~2 beats, flushed on a >±40% jump or a >0.25 s break) only glides `phase` *between* ticks, clamped so position never runs backward; `start`/`continue` arm and **begin on the next tick** (the MIDI convention; song position = sixteenths × 6 ticks, and an SPP jump clears the pulse anchor so the next tick lands on it exactly); a transport-less master (DJ gear) **free-runs** from its first tick; the math is the pure `TempoEngine` fed host-time-stamped events through `MIDIInput`'s internal listener seam (packet `timeStamp` → seconds; `MIDIOutput` stamps sends with real host time so in-process loopback timing is exact), so `TempoClockTests` pin it deterministically. Examples `Integration/MIDILoopback`, `MIDIMonitor`, `TempoSync` (internal-timer loopback: an in-sketch master sends clock through a virtual source; turn it off and real gear drives the same sketch); `Docs/Integration/MIDI.md`.

- **Syphon** (`OllinSyphon` satellite). Live GPU frame-sharing with other Mac apps over the **vendored** Syphon Framework (BSD-2, `External/CSyphon`, Metal portion only — wrapped, off the public surface). `SyphonServer` / `publishSyphon(name:)` publishes the rendered texture; `SyphonClient.newFrame()` draws an incoming feed as `Image(texture:)`. Brought two core seams (shared with future layered effects): the GPU-texture frame hook (`wantsRenderedTexture` + `frameRendered(_:texture:)` → `MetalRenderer.texture(of:)`, no CPU readback) and texture-backed `Image(texture:)`. **Gotchas:** publish `flipped: true` + client flips back (orientation); hand the server a non-sRGB `.bgra8Unorm` view and read back through a `.bgra8Unorm_srgb` view (color — both need `.pixelFormatView` usage); single-GPU Macs only. Examples `Integration/SyphonLoopback`, `SyphonViewer`; `Docs/Integration/Syphon.md`.

- **Game controllers** (`OllinController` satellite; `Controller.swift` + `ControllerButton.swift` + `ControllerHub.swift` + `Sketch+Controller.swift`).
