A modern controller read in `draw()` over GameController: **`controller`** (player one) / **`controller(_ player:)`** / `connectedControllers` / `controllerCount` hand back a **`Controller`** value snapshot (sticks / dpad / triggers / `isDown`/`wasPressed`/`wasReleased` over the `ControllerButton` enum / `isConnected`+`didConnect`+`didDisconnect` / motion / touchpad / battery / `unavailableReason`), with the bare settings `controllerMotion(_:)` / `controllerDeadzone(_:)` / `controllersRunInBackground(_:)` / `discoverControllers()`. **A satellite, not core input, and the reason is the seam, not convenience:** mouse / keyboard / pressure are core *because* they arrive as `NSEvent`s through `OllinMTKView` and need the AppKit/UIKit seam, where a controller is handed to the *process* by a system service, touches no view, and is character-identical on macOS and iOS, so core membership would buy the iOS leg nothing and cost every 2D sketch a framework link. **Load-bearing:** the tier is **polled once per frame** (`sampleIfNeeded(frame:)` keyed on `frameCount`, so every read within a frame agrees and the hardware is hit once), and that is *why* it is testable at all — a **synthetic `GCController`** (`GCController.withExtendedGamepad()`) accepts written values and reads back through the shipped path, but does **not** fire the system's change handlers and never joins `GCController.controllers()`, so a handler-driven design would be unverifiable with nothing plugged in and the hub takes its list through an injectable `source` seam; the **classic `extendedGamepad` profile is the one to build on, not `GCControllerLiveInput`**, whose `buttons`/`axes`/`dpads` come back *empty* on a synthetic controller (probed). **Nothing arrives off-thread**, so there is no lock and no `@unchecked Sendable` producer here, unlike every other satellite. Presses/releases are **edges differenced between two samples** (`ControllerButtonMask`, one bit per button, `wasDown`/`down` on the slot) with **no event drain by design** — a press lasts several frames, so unlike MIDI a hand cannot press and release between two of them. Sticks/dpad/touch are **y-flipped once into canvas terms** (up reads negative, so `p += stick * speed` moves up the screen); the deadzone (default 0.1) is **radial and rescaled**, never a per-axis cut (which jumps at the edge and bends a diagonal hold). **Player slots are held, not recomputed** (a controller keeps its number while connected, a newcomer takes the lowest free one, so unplugging player 2 cannot renumber player 3), assigned by walking the slot array in order rather than a dictionary (the determinism rule). Motion is **opt-in** (`sensorsActive` costs battery; `hasMotion` is false both with no sensors and with none asked for) and **Xbox hardware has none at all**; the touchpad comes from **`physicalInputProfile.touchpads[GCInputDualShockTouchpadOne]`**, never `GCDualShockGamepad.touchpadPrimary`, which is typed as a plain direction pad and so cannot tell a finger resting dead center from an untouched surface (only the named element carries `touchState`). **Permission: none** — probed, a bare `swift run` binary reads a controller with no TCC prompt and no entitlement (unlike the screen); what *is* real is `shouldMonitorBackgroundEvents`, NO by default since macOS 11.3, so a sketch behind another window reads centered until `controllersRunInBackground(true)`. Headless reads neutral with `unavailableReason` set and a one-time stderr note. Deliberate cuts, each named in the docs rather than dropped: rumble / adaptive triggers / the light bar (outputs, so they belong to the haptics work), **Apple Pencil** (waits on the iOS leg; its force joins the shipped `pressure` rather than becoming a second thing), attitude (no public quaternion type yet), racing wheels and spatial accessories. Example `Integration/ControllerInput` (a pad as a drawing instrument, `map` drawing every input as it is read); `ControllerTests` (29, `.serialized` on the one process-wide hub, each invariant verified red by sabotage; the single hardware test is `.enabled(if:)` so a machine with no pad reports it **skipped and says so** rather than passing vacuously); no pixel snapshot (live input). `Docs/Integration/Controller.md`; Guide Ch 27 § *Something to hold: game controllers* + ReadingAPad figure.
- **Screen and window capture** (`OllinScreen` satellite; `ScreenCapture.swift` + `ScreenSource.swift`). Any display / app / window as a live `FrameSource` + `VideoFeed` over ScreenCaptureKit: the *non-cooperative* counterpart to Syphon (which needs the other app to publish). **`ScreenCapture(_ source:)`** + `start()`/`stop()`, drawn with `drawFrame`/`frame`, GPU-resident (IOSurface → `CVMetalTextureCache` → `Image(texture:)`, the `VideoPlayer` path, `.bgra8Unorm_srgb` against `config.colorSpaceName = .sRGB`); knobs `source` / `excludesOwnWindows` / `showsCursor` / `frameRate` / `scale` are all live (a filter-only change pushes through `updateConfiguration`, a filter-changing one rebinds). **`ScreenSource`** is the nameable value (`.mainDisplay` / `.display(id)` / `.app(name-or-bundleID)` / `.window(title:app:)` / `.windowID`), with `displays()`/`windows()`/`apps()` for discovery — **named-in-code is primary and the system picker is a deliberate cut** (`SCContentSharingPicker` is a Control-Center *application* singleton with an observer protocol, host-app shaped, and a picked filter is a click nobody recorded; enumeration gives the same discovery). **Load-bearing:** the **stream must be retained** or it is deallocated and silently stops delivering with no error and no callback (cost a probe to find), and it lives in a `StreamHolder` so the nonisolated `deinit` can end it; self-exclusion keys on **pid, never bundle identifier** (a `swift run` sketch is listed in `SCShareableContent` with an *empty* bundle id, so `ScreenMatch.app` must reject an empty needle or every such process matches ""); frames arrive on a background queue so the `SCStreamOutput` is a file-scope non-`@MainActor` class (the render-thread rule) handing the buffer over an `NSLock` box (a `CVPixelBuffer` is explicitly **not** `Sendable`, so `OSAllocatedUnfairLock`'s generic form can't hold one); only `SCFrameStatus.complete` samples carry pixels (idle/blank ones are also delivered); the captured size is `filter.contentRect * filter.pointPixelScale` (points → backing pixels) times `scale`; the CPU `CGImage` conversion for the tap runs **only while a tap is installed**; a source naming something absent is a **wait, not an error** (a 1 s retry loop, `isRunning` = wanted vs `isReceiving` = arriving), and a stream the system ends re-binds. The **matching rules are extracted as pure functions** (`ScreenMatch`) because `SCShareableContent` has no public init, so a fake screen cannot be staged; app names match **in full** (else `"Mail"` takes MailMate) while window titles match **as a substring**, ties broken by lowest window id so a run repeats. **Permission is the design problem and it is a terminal's, not the sketch's:** a `swift run` / `ollin` / OllinLive / gallery binary has no bundle identity, so TCC attributes screen recording to the **launching terminal** (verified: `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` true and a full stream from a bare binary, with no entry of its own in System Settings), one approval covering every sketch from that terminal; surfaced as `isAvailable` / `unavailableReason` / `requestAccess()` (all `nonisolated`), a missing permission drawing its reason rather than throwing, and granting it never reaching a running process. Deliberate v1 cuts: **system audio** (belongs with the audio analyzer surface, not bolted to a video frame source) and the picker. Example `Integration/ScreenCapture` (the display filtered, `L` listing what is capturable as the code line naming it, and the feedback tunnel on a knob); `OllinScreenTests` (26; the rules always-on, the end-to-end capture soft-gated on the permission — a consent a machine cannot grant itself); no pixel snapshot (a capture is whatever is on screen). `Docs/Integration/ScreenCapture.md`; Guide Ch 28 § *Drawing with the screen: ScreenCapture* + ScreenAsMaterial figure.

- **Virtual camera** (`Apps/OllinCameraApp` host + CMIO system extension, plus the `OllinCamera` satellite). Registers a system-wide "Ollin Camera" every webcam app reads; `publishVirtualCamera()` letterboxes the canvas texture into a 1280×720 IOSurface-backed `CVPixelBuffer` enqueued on the device's sink stream, with a built-in "no signal" test card while idle. **Gotchas:** the sink is the **direction-0** stream; `CMIOStreamCopyBufferQueue` returns a nil queue unless the queue-altered proc is non-nil (pass an empty closure); the extension is an installed, signed, notarized `.app` (xcodegen, *not* SwiftPM) with strict activation rules (bundle-id filename, `CFBundlePackageType = SYSX`, run from `/Applications`, install with `ditto --noextattr --norsrc`) — see the app README. Example `Integration/VirtualCamera`; `Docs/Integration/VirtualCamera.md`.

- **Video playback** (`OllinVideo` satellite). `VideoPlayer` (`@MainActor`) plays a file as a live texture-backed `Image` via `AVPlayerItemVideoOutput` → `CVMetalTextureCache` → `drawImage` (zero CPU copies); `play` / `pause` / `seek(to:)` / `loops` / `rate` / `volume`, `duration` / `size` (async), `snapshot()` for CPU/vision. Conforms `FrameSource` + `VideoFeed`, so a tracker attaches and analyzes the footage as it plays (via a second video output). **Headless export is deterministic:** under `OllinApp.isRenderingHeadless` (a `package` flag every offline driver sets) the player swaps to a virtual playhead advanced by the sketch's per-frame pass (`FrameAdvancing`, now `package` so satellites conform; isolated `@MainActor` conformance) and decodes by timestamp through a sequential `AVAssetReader` (`HeadlessVideoReader`), so exported frame `k` always shows the clip at `k/fps` after `play()` — create the player by the end of `setup()` or the advance pass never finds it. **Soundtrack tap:** `audioTap` (the core `AudioTapSource` seam) delivers mono PCM off an `MTAudioProcessingTap`; the tap's C callbacks touch only a storage object the tap itself retains (released in `finalize` — never reference the player from the callbacks; the tap can outlive it), and OllinAudio's `Soundtrack(of:)` runs the analyzer surface over it (rebuilds once at the stream's real sample rate). **Gotchas:** `.bgra8Unorm_srgb` texture; no y-flip (top-down); keep a short `CVMetalTexture` ring alive; `isMuted = true` starves the audio tap (a hard mute stops audio processing) — use `volume = 0`; a `Soundtrack`/vision tracker reads nothing during a headless export (live-clock paths). Examples `Video/VideoPlayback`, `Video/SoundReactive` (spectrum bars + beat ring over its own clip's music); `Docs/Video/Video.md`.

- **Physics** (`OllinPhysics` satellite). One `World` stepped each frame, hybrid by design: a **soft Verlet side** (`Particle` — a `final class`, no stored velocity, `previous`-based; `Spring`, a positional distance constraint; `World` with gravity / drag / bounds / bounce / opt-in disk `collisions` broad-phased through a uniform spatial hash) and a **rigid side** backed by **vendored Box2D** (MIT, `External/CBox2D`) — `addBody` / `Body` / `Collider` (`.circle` / `.box` / `.capsule` / `.polygon`) / `connect` / `Joint` (`.revolute` / `.distance` / `.weld` / `.prismatic`) / `grab` (mouse joint), sharing the world's gravity / bounds / bounce / drag, bridged by `pixelsPerMeter`. **Gotchas:** `drag` compounds per step (a weightless drift field needs `drag = 0`); Box2D's world pool is a non-thread-safe global → every Box2D-touching test in one `@Suite(.serialized)`; spawn a `Body` *inside* `bounds`, not above; disk-collision resolution walks particles in **index order with a fixed cell scan** — never iterate the spatial-hash Dictionary directly (per-process hash order reordered the position-mutating pair resolution, so a seeded pile settled differently on every run; a real Guide-figure flake). Examples `Physics/Packing` + `Blobs` (soft), `Stack` / `Tumble` / `Chain` (rigid); `Docs/Simulation/Physics.md`. **The 3D rigid side** (`World3D.swift` / `Body3D.swift` / `Joint3D.swift` / `Sketch+Physics3D.swift`, backed by **vendored Jolt Physics** — MIT, `External/CJolt`, C++ behind Ollin's own `extern "C"` bridge, the *only* code that includes Jolt headers, so the `JPH_*` define-consistency rule holds by construction): `World3D` (eager handle, `gravity` y-up, `ground` slab, `bounce`, `maxTimestep` clamp, `unitsPerMeter` **default 1** — scene units ≈ meters, unlike 2D's 100) / `addBody` with `Collider3D` (`.sphere` / `.box` / `.capsule` / `.cylinder` / `.taperedCapsule` / `.taperedCylinder` / `.cone` / `.hull` / `.mesh` / `.heightfield(Heightfield, width:depth:height:)` / `.compound([Part])` — **mesh and heightfield colliders are static-only**, a dynamic one is pinned with a one-time note) / `connect` with `JointKind3D` (2D's kinds + `.ball`) / `grab`, and the sketch sugar `withBody(_:)` (pose via `withState` + translate + quat→angle/axis `rotate`, so replay/export/materials all apply untouched) + `body(under:in:)` / `grabBody(at:in:)` / `dragGrab(_:to:)` (camera ray-pick; drags in the screen-parallel plane at the picked view depth). **Load-bearing:** never pass `&tuple.0` across the C boundary — the compiler may materialize a temporary for that one element and silently drop every value the C side writes past it (a real bug: positions read back with y/z stuck at 0 while the sim ran perfectly); all marshaling goes through `CJoltInterop.swift`'s `withUnsafe(Mutable)Bytes(of:)` helpers. **The grab is the reference drag model and must stay it**: a *static* anchor body created but never added to the world, *teleported* per move (velocity stays zero), holding the body on a zero-length distance spring (frequency 2, damping 1) — a kinematic anchor driven by `MoveKinematic` feeds its arrival velocity into the constraint and pumps the body into orbit (a real bug). Jolt keeps **no global world pool** (only an init-once type registry behind `call_once`), so the 3D tests run parallel — no `.serialized` needed, unlike Box2D's. **No pixel snapshots for 3D physics** (the artificial-life precedent): determinism is per-binary — the same build replays byte-identically (test-pinned, incl. across thread counts) but a toolchain rebuild may shift ulps and chaotic scenes amplify them; behavioral `RigidBody3DTests` pin the solver instead, and the Guide's CrateFall figure freezes one collapse. **Joint motors, limits & springs** (stage 2): `.revolute`/`.prismatic` take `limits:` (a `ClosedRange` **relative to the connect pose** — that pose is 0, so the range must straddle it; hinge clamped to min ∈ [-π, 0] / max ∈ [0, π], the solver's window) and the returned `Joint3D` carries `drive(at:strength:)` (velocity motor) / `drive(to:frequency:damping:strength:)` (spring-servo position motor, upstream defaults 2 Hz / damping 1) / `stopMotor()` / `friction` / `softenLimits(frequency:damping:)` / the `angle`/`offset` readbacks. **Load-bearing:** `cjolt_constraint_set_motor` **wakes both bodies** (a sleeping pair never feels a motor change — a late-engaging door closer would hang open forever); joint `friction` applies **only while unpowered** (the solver's contract — one knob is both stiff-hinge drag and the post-`stopMotor` coast-down brake); slider targets/offsets convert by `unitsPerMeter` while angles and the N·m / N effort caps pass through; the motor calls no-op with a one-time note on undrivable kinds (bridge switches on `GetSubType`, so no Swift downcast can mis-drive). `JointMotor3DTests` pins every knob against its counterfactual twin. **Compound & world-geometry colliders** (stage 3): `.compound([Part])` fuses shapes into ONE body via `.part(_:at:rotated:axis:density:)` (part `density` **multiplies** the body's; parts nest; the Windmill's blade cross is the proof — five shapes, one body, drawn by a recursive part walk in `withBody`); `.heightfield` takes a `Heightfield` **sized exactly like its `mesh(width:depth:height:)`** so collider and drawn mesh trace one surface (resampled onto the smallest power-of-two grid covering the source cells, 4…1024 per side, 16-bit samples; beyond the edges is void); `addStaticColliders(from: Scene)` walks `Scene.visitWorlds` (now `package`) baking each mesh node's **composed world transform into the triangles** (scale/shear included) as one static mesh body at identity. **Load-bearing:** `mAllowDynamicOrKinematic` must be **false for any `MustBeStatic()` shape** — allowing the switch makes body creation compute mass properties the shape cannot provide and the library *traps* (latent stage-1 bug: nothing had ever created a `.mesh`-collider body until the stage-3 tests); the static pin asks the created shape, never the descriptor type. Shape marshaling goes through `ShapeDescArena` (arena-owned allocations held with `withExtendedLifetime` across the create) because compound children make the pinned-buffer count data-dependent — fixed `withUnsafeBufferPointer` nesting can't express it. `Collider3DTests` pins the tier (compound mass/balance vs counterfactual twins, part poses, cone = ⅓ cylinder mass, heightfield mappings, scene-collider composition, byte-identical replay). **Contact events & sensors** (stage 4): every touch that started or stopped during a `step` lands in **`world.contacts`** (`Contact3D`: `phase` / `a` / `b` / `point` / `normal` / `speed` + `involves`/`other(than:)`), polled in `draw()` like mouse state, with the per-body views `contacts` / `entered` / `exited` / `touching` / `isTouching(_:)`; **`addBody(…, isSensor: true)`** makes a detector region that reports overlaps and pushes nothing. **Load-bearing:** the listener fires on solver worker threads mid-`Update`, so the bridge's mutex-guarded `ContactRecorder` buffers flat-POD events the main thread drains after `cjolt_world_step` returns (never a callback into Swift, the render-thread rule's C cousin); the buffer **collapses sub-shape pairs into body pairs** by reference count (a crate on terrain reports one landing, not one per triangle) and **sorts before draining** (worker-thread record order is nondeterministic; the Swift `touching` lists are sorted arrays, never `Set`s, for the same reason); `speed` is the **pre-solve** closing speed `(v1 − v2)·n` (the manifold normal runs a→b), and an `ended` event carries **only the pair** (`OnContactRemoved` may not touch the bodies at all, one may be destroyed) so its point/normal/speed are zero and an event naming a removed body is dropped *after* its bookkeeping applies; a **sensor is kinematic and never sleeps** whatever `kind` was asked for (a *static* sensor loses the contact the moment a body settles, which would empty a pressure plate) and lives in its own object layer pairing only with moving bodies, so `sensor.touching` answers standing occupancy where **an ordinary settled pile sleeps and stops reporting** (Jolt's model, pinned deliberately against its sensor twin); sensors are invisible to ray picking (`NonSensorBodyFilter`) and `Body3D.kind` refuses to change one (`noteOnce`); the `ground` slab is now a registered-but-unlisted **`world.groundBody`** (in the id map so a landing names a body, out of `bodies` so no drawing loop skips a 1000-unit box). `Contact3DTests` pins it against counterfactual twins (sensor pass-through vs solid deflection, one event per pair vs two loose bodies, √(2gh) impact speed, read-twice-not-drained, no stale touches after a removal, identical replays). **Characters** (stage 5, `Character3D.swift`): **`world.addCharacter(radius:height:at:)`** is a walking figure driven from `draw()` — `move(_:)` sets the horizontal velocity it's *trying* to walk at (held until changed), `jump(_:)` is granted only if it's on the ground at the next step, and `world.step` sweeps every character along with the bodies (no second update call); readbacks are `position` (the **feet**) / `isOnGround` / `groundState` / `groundNormal` / `groundBody` / `groundVelocity`, the knobs `stepHeight` / `maxSlope` / `pushStrength` / `mass` / `facing`, and the sketch sugar **`withCharacter(_:)`** (feet + facing, `withBody`'s twin). **Load-bearing:** a `CharacterVirtual` is a shape swept by hand, **not a body in the world**, so it needs its own bridge object (`CJoltCharacter`) and its own update call — run **before** `cjolt_world_step` (the reference's order) via **`ExtendedUpdate`**, the one that does stair-stepping and stick-to-floor (both distances are its *arguments*, so the wrapper holds them, not the character); the capsule is built **bottom-at-origin** through a `RotatedTranslatedShape` so `position` is the feet, and `mSupportingVolume` is `Plane(up, -radius)` so only the lower cap can hold it up (a hand brushing a wall is not ground); `isOnGround` reads **`GetGroundState`**, never a normal test. The **velocity composition is the library's own recipe** and lives in Swift (`advance`): on the ground it inherits `groundVelocity` (so a moving platform carries it, pinned) and only then may a jump be added, in the air it keeps just its vertical, gravity is added, and the walking velocity is added last and always (air control on, no inertia smoothing — the sketch owns input feel). **`velocity` is intent, not outcome** — the library never writes back the achieved velocity, so a character pinned against a wall still reads a full walking pace; **`actualVelocity`** is derived from the step's position delta (the reference's "effective velocity") and is what a walk cycle must read. **Every character always carries an inner kinematic body** (`character.body`, 0.9× scale, `Layers::MOVING`): it is what gives the character presence among the ordinary bodies, so the stage-4 contact/sensor surface, ray picking, and tunneling protection all work on it — registered in `bodyByID` but kept **out of `world.bodies`** (the `groundBody` precedent, so no drawing loop renders a capsule over the sketch's own figure); characters register in a `CharacterVsCharacterCollisionSimple` so they collide with each other. **`stepHeight` is a probe distance, not a hard ceiling** (the rounded foot rides an edge first, so a ledge ~25% taller may still be climbed — test with a margin, don't fake precision); **`pushStrength` (N) vs ground friction decides a shove** (4 kg crate moves under the default 100 N, 43 kg does not); a slope just past `maxSlope` reads `.onSteepSlope` but a near-vertical face reads `.notSupported`. `Character3DTests` (19) pins each knob against its counterfactual twin (same ramp, one `maxSlope`; same ledge, one `stepHeight`; same crate, one `pushStrength`), plus platform carry, sensor entry, teleport-rereads-ground, and identical replays. (Fixed alongside: `World3D.removeAll()` emptied `bodyByID` while `groundBody` still pointed at the slab, so floor contacts were dropped and a character's `groundBody` read nil after a rebuild.)  **Vehicles** (stage 6, `Vehicle3D.swift`): **`world.addVehicle(_:at:wheels:)`** builds a driveable machine from a chassis collider plus `Wheel3D`s (`position` = the chassis-local mounting point, `radius`/`width`, `steers`/`driven`/`handBrake`, `suspensionLength`/`suspensionTravel`/`suspensionFrequency`/`suspensionDamping`, `brakeTorque`/`handBrakeTorque`, `grip`, `casterAngle`), driven from `draw()` by `throttle` / `steering` / `brake` / `handBrake` (all held until changed, `world.step` hands them over) with the knobs `engineTorque` / `topSpeed` / `wheelContact` / `maxTilt` / `antiRollStiffness` and the readbacks `speed` / `forward` / `up` / `rpm` / `gear` / `isOnGround` plus per-wheel `center` / `spin` / `steerAngle` / `isOnGround` / `groundBody` / `suspensionCompression` / `slip` / `slideAngle`; the sketch sugar **`withWheel(_:)`** poses a +y-aligned cylinder onto a wheel (`withBody`'s twin), and **`balances: true`** makes the leaning two-wheeler. **Load-bearing:** a vehicle is a **constraint on a body, not a body** — the chassis is an ordinary `Body3D` in `world.bodies` (so it collides, reports contacts, and is ray-pickable) and creating one must `AddConstraint` **and `AddStepListener`** (the wheels are collided and driven inside the step listener; without it the vehicle never moves, upstream's own warning), with the constraint destroyed *before* its chassis; the chassis takes an explicit `mass` and a **lowered center of mass** (defaulting to the mean wheel-mount height) through two new `CJoltBodyDesc` fields, and the `OffsetCenterOfMassShape` wrap leaves `GetPosition` at the shape origin so drawing is untouched; **axles are derived from geometry, not list order** (`Vehicle3D.axles(of:)` sorts by `(z, x, index)` and clusters within a quarter of the wheelbase, so a two-wheeler gets two single-wheel axles where list-order pairing would fuse front and back), an axle with any driven wheel takes a differential and every full pair an anti-roll bar, and **at least one axle must be driven** (zero differentials trips the controller's own torque-split assert, so with nothing marked the rear axle takes it); **gearing is solved from `topSpeed`** in the bridge (top gear at max RPM turns a driven wheel that fast) rather than exposing ratios, and the sample's 10× `SetTireMaxImpulseCallback` hack is deliberately **not** adopted (defaults measured by a torque sweep instead); **every wheel setting except `driven` is live** via `cjolt_vehicle_set_wheel_settings` (one `applyWheelDesc` serves build and retune, `const_cast`ing settings the bridge alone owns and the solver re-reads each step; friction curves rebuild from a default tyre before `grip` scales them) while `driven` is the gearbox and is refused after construction with a note; the wheels' collision tester filters out the chassis **and sensors** (overriding the body filter replaces the default self-filter); and a **two-wheeler needs a real `casterAngle`** (~30°) or the balance controller cannot hold a line — note that running dead straight it stays up even unbalanced, so the counterfactual starts it leaned. `Vehicle3DTests` (19) pins each knob against its twin (the sharpest: two cars with slick fronts differing only in which axle is driven travel 2× apart), plus suspension sag, braking vs coasting, the hand brake locking only its own wheels, reverse braking through a stop, the gearing ceiling, wheels in the air, axle grouping, a leaned two-wheeler righting itself, and identical replays. (Fixed alongside: a `Character3D`/`Vehicle3D` a sketch still holds when its world goes now tears down safely — the world marks them destroyed on its way out.) **Ragdolls** (stage 7, `Ragdoll3D.swift` + `RagdollFit.swift`): **`world.addRagdoll(from: scene, at:joints:swing:twist:mass:friction:)`** turns a skinned `Scene` into a tree of rigid bodies (one per skeleton joint, hung on the new **`.swingTwist(at:axis:swing:twist:)`** joint kind) and **`scene.apply(ragdoll)`** writes the simulated pose back onto the skin, the inverse of `apply(_:at:)`; **`drive(toward: scene, frequency:damping:strength:)`** powers every joint toward the pose a scene is holding (the animation becomes a *request*), `goLimp()` cuts it, `pose(from:)` stands the figure back up, `limit(_:swing:twist:)` retunes one joint live, `applyImpulse(_:)` shoves the whole figure, `kind` switches it dynamic/kinematic/static; readbacks are `limbs` (`name`/`body`/`parent`/`collider`/the shape's pose inside the body) + `bodies` + `position` + `isAwake`, and the sketch sugar **`withLimb(_:)`** poses onto a limb's *fitted shape* (`withBody`'s twin). Built on the library's own `RagdollSettings`/`Ragdoll` for `Stabilize()` + constraint priorities + `DisableParentChildCollisions()`. **Load-bearing:** a limb's body stands **at its joint, not mid-bone** (the shape is pushed out along the bone via `RotatedTranslatedShape`), which is *why* the write-back is exact (body transform ≡ joint transform, pinned to 1e-5) and why a motor target is the skeleton's own local rotation; the **skeleton seam lives in the core** (satellites can't depend on each other) as `package` `Scene.skeleton()` / `skinnedVertices()` / `setJointWorlds(_:)` / `nodeWorldTransforms()` in `3D/SceneSkeleton.swift`, and `setJointWorlds` walks **top-down**, **keeps each node's scale** (a rigid pose has none; dividing it out shrinks the mesh) and **re-syncs a TRS animation base** to the posed transform (else a rotation-only track next frame drags the node back to its authored translation); **shapes are fitted to the mesh, not the bones** (vertices assigned to the limb weighting them most, in the joint's frame; axis = covariance's dominant eigenvector by power iteration seeded from the largest-variance axis, radius = 85th percentile of perpendicular distances, ends = 2nd/98th percentiles, so one stray vertex can't decide an arm's thickness) — the bundled figure fits a 0.157 torso against a 0.051 forearm from similar-length bones; mass splits by fitted volume then rebalances through `Stabilize()` (chain totals preserved); **the root has no constraint**, so a powered figure holds its *shape*, not its place (pin `limbs[0].body.kind = .kinematic` for the puppet-on-a-hook; `ragdoll.kind = .kinematic` routes `drive(toward:)` to `MoveKinematic` so it shoves what it walks through); the **target scene and the drawn scene must be separate copies** (a figure driven toward the scene it was just posed from has nowhere to pull); limbs are ordinary bodies registered in `bodyByID` but **kept out of `world.bodies`** (the `groundBody`/`character.body` precedent — the sketch draws the mesh), which needed `body(under:in:)` to look up `bodyByID` instead of `bodies` (also makes a character's stand-in and the ground slab pickable); each figure gets **its own collision group**, so a limb never fights its parent while two figures collide normally; joints not named in `joints:` **ride the nearest limb above them** with their pose and their share of the flesh. `Ragdoll3DTests` (22) pins it against counterfactual twins — the headline being one drop run twice, shape error 0.48 limp vs 0.034 powered. Asset authored by `Scripts/make-ragdoll-scene.swift` (a 16-joint humanoid + a looping wave animation). **Soft bodies** (stage 8, `SoftBody3D.swift` + the `package` `Mesh.welded()` seam in `Sources/Ollin/3D/MeshWeld.swift`): **`world.addSoftBody(from: mesh, at:rotation:axis:mass:stiffness:bend:pressure:damping:friction:bounce:iterations:vertexRadius:twoSided:pinned:)`** turns any `Mesh` into simulated particles (position-based dynamics; cloth that drapes, closed surfaces that hold air), read back as **`softBody.mesh`** (the source mesh with the simulation's positions + derived normals, cached per step) and drawn by the sketch sugar **`drawSoftBody(_:)`**; per-particle control is `pin` / `unpin` / `isPinned` / `move(_:to:)` / `nearestVertex(to:)`, the live knobs are `pressure` / `iterations` / `vertexRadius`, and the mouse pair is **`grabSoftBody(at:in:)` / `dragSoftGrab(_:to:)` / `releaseSoftGrab(_:)`** over a `Sketch.SoftGrip` (a *pinned particle*, since a constraint can't act on a soft body). Bodies live in `world.softBodies`, **out of `world.bodies`** (whose one-pose surface doesn't describe them; the `groundBody`/`character.body`/limb precedent). **Load-bearing:** **welding is a precondition, not a nicety** — Ollin's generators emit flat-shaded meshes whose triangles share no vertex index, so an unwelded soft body has no connectivity at all and falls apart into loose triangles; `Mesh.welded()` merges by position and the `remap` republishes results in the *source* mesh's order (the `MeshReactionDiffusion` precedent), which is what keeps uvs/colors/material intact. **Read-back normals follow the source mesh's own normals, not the winding** (a one-time area-weighted vote at build time against the rest shape; Ollin's catalog doesn't agree with itself here — `Mesh.plane` came back lit from underneath, the subdivision-surface lesson again). **All three knobs are normalized so one number means the same at any size:** `stiffness` maps onto compliance through `scale = meanEdge · √particleCount / (mass · gravity)` (the compliance at which one loaded edge stretches by its own length; a fixed compliance softens a heavy cloth and does nothing to a light one — pinned by `stiffnessMeansTheSameAtAnyWeight`), `bend` divides that same scale by **meanEdge²** (a fold constraint measures an angle where a stretch constraint measures a length, so it carries two fewer powers of length; without the correction the whole 0.2…1 range was already rigid), and `pressure` is the solver's `n R T` expressed as **outward acceleration in gravities** (`a = P·A/(m·V)` worked back through `ApplyPressure`, so `1` just holds the body's own weight up; refused with a note on an open surface, the closed test being every edge in exactly two faces). **Settling takes ~600 steps** — a 200-step probe read non-monotone and nearly flat, which is a measurement artifact, not the mapping. **The envelope is the library's** (not a shortcut): soft bodies collide with rigid bodies but **not each other or themselves** (a folded sheet passes through its own layers), they are **outside `world.contacts`** and `body(under:in:)`, impulses/joints/grabs don't reach one (hence `applyForce` for wind), and **there is no tearing** (a tear must split a shared vertex and rebuild the constraint set, impossible mid-simulation). `SoftBody3DTests` (19) pins it against counterfactual twins (pinned vs free, stiff vs slack stretch, bend-held vs limp droop, firm vs limp ball height/volume/shove, pressure inert on a sheet, drape over a sphere, wind, identical replays). **Water & buoyancy** (stage 9, `Water.swift`): **`world.water = Water(level:density:linearDrag:angularDrag:flow:waves:)`** floats every dynamic body below the surface with **no per-body opt-in** (the `ground` DX), plus `Body3D.buoyancy` (a multiplier on what density alone would do), `world.waterHeight(at:)` / `waterMesh(extent:resolution:around:)` / `waterPhase`, and `Water.Waves(amplitude:wavelength:speed:heading:)`. **Load-bearing:** buoyancy is an impulse the **caller** applies per body per step, not something the solver works out, so the loop lives in `World3D.step` **before** `cjolt_world_step` (the samples' `PrePhysicsUpdate` slot). The library takes a dimensionless factor (fluid density ÷ body density) rather than a density; the bridge forms it as `scale · density · totalVolume / mass` using **the `totalVolume` `GetSubmergedVolume` just returned**, never the shape's own reported volume, so the waterline lands where the displaced volume says (a body of density *d* settles with fraction *d* under, pinned to 0.08; the margin is what **sleeping** costs, freezing the body wherever its last oscillation reached). Ollin's relative `density: 1` already maps to 1000 kg/m³, so `Water.density` rides the same scale and a cork/stone falls out of the collider `density` a sketch already sets. **Waves are a per-body tangent plane** (the impulse takes a surface point + normal *per call*, the boat sample's own approach), the surface function lives in **Swift** with an **analytic** gradient, so `waterMesh`/`waterHeight` and the ridden surface are one source of truth (test-pinned per vertex); the envelope is that a body much larger than its wavelength is approximated. The query (`cjolt_world_bodies_in_box`, broad-phase `CollideAABox` sorted by handle) filters to **dynamic rigid non-sensor** bodies: a kinematic body (sensor, character stand-in) would be nudged off its driven path and the library's buoyancy **asserts on a soft body** (its documented gap, stated not faked). **Waking has three modes and needs them** (`CJoltBuoyancyWake`): a settled floater sleeps and holds its waterline exactly (correct, and free); a **swell** wakes only what its surface passes through (so a sunk stone sleeps on); a **changed setting** must wake everything afloat **wherever it is**, because the equilibrium moved (the first draft had only the swell rule and a probe caught it: raising the level 0→3 left a sleeping crate frozen at the old waterline, outside its own bounds, so it never woke). Water is an **ocean, not a pool** (everything below `level`, out to the horizon; a harbour is static walls). `Buoyancy3DTests` (20) pins it against counterfactual twins (cork vs stone vs no water, waterline vs density, brine, drag settles vs bobs forever, sleeper holds its level, tide reaches it, swell carries the top and lets the bottom sleep, current drifts, `buoyancy` both ways, sensors/soft bodies untouched, drawn surface == ridden surface, identical replays). **World queries** (stage 10, `Query3D.swift`): `raycast(from:to:)` / `raycastAll` / `sweep(_:from:to:rotated:axis:)` / `sweepAll` / `bodiesOverlapping(_:at:rotated:axis:)` / `bodiesContaining(_:)`, all taking `ignoring:` + `includingSensors:` and all answering the shared **`Hit3D`** (body / point / outward normal / distance along the query) between steps, changing nothing. **Load-bearing:** the C filter is a **struct** (`CJoltQueryFilter`: ignore list + `includeSensors` + `includeSoftBodies`) passed by pointer, so collision filtering lands as a *field* rather than a parameter on every call; **what a query is blind to is a decision, not an inheritance** — a sensor is transparent (a region to be inside, not a surface to hit; the mouse-pick rule) and a soft body is too (no rigid pose to hand back, matching `contacts` / `body(under:in:)`), while the ground slab / character stand-ins / ragdoll limbs **are** visible because hits resolve through `bodyByID`, which is what makes a ground probe and a sight check on a walking figure work; **the mouse pick is the same call** (`World3D.pick(from:to:)`, the internal ray with `includeSoftBodies` on) so there is one ray implementation, not two that can drift; a ray's normal needs a `BodyLockRead` (only the body knows which face the sub-shape id names), a sweep's is the negated penetration axis; the bridge returns how many hits it **found** (not wrote), so Swift sizes a 16-slot buffer and grows exactly once; `mesh`/`heightfield` probes are refused with a one-time note (`MustBeStatic()` asked of the *created* shape, the stage-3 rule). Example `3D/Physics/Sightlines`; `Query3DTests` (24). **Collision groups** (stage 11, `CollisionGroup.swift`): a named **`CollisionGroup`** (`ExpressibleByStringLiteral`) on every thing that can be added (`addBody` / `addCharacter` / `addVehicle` / `addRagdoll` / `addSoftBody` / `addStaticColliders`, each also settable live as `.group`) plus a symmetric world table — **`ignoreCollisions(between:and:)`** / `allowCollisions(between:and:)` / `collides(_:with:)` / `collisionGroups` / `World3D.maxCollisionGroups` (64) — and `as:` on all six queries (ask as a body of that group would). **Load-bearing:** the group rides in the **object layer** (`layer = (group << 2) | kind`, kinds NON_MOVING/MOVING/GHOST/SENSOR), *not* Jolt's per-body `CollisionGroup`/`GroupFilterTable`: the layer is the one thing consulted at pair-finding (so the contact listener never hears a filtered touch), in every query's `DefaultObjectLayerFilter`, in `ExtendedUpdate`'s filters, in a `VehicleCollisionTester`'s constructor, and in `SoftBodyCreationSettings`, where the narrow-phase group filter reaches none of those, hardcodes "same sub-group never collides", and is already the ragdoll tier's slot (so the two stay **orthogonal** — a figure keeps its own limb filter and two figures in one Ollin group still collide). **Group 0 is numerically today's layer set, so a world that names no group is byte-identical** (whole suite unchanged, no re-records). Four hand-honored spots, each a would-be half-applied filter: **character-vs-character** is outside the broad phase, so `GroupedCharacterCollision` refills a scratch list per call and delegates to the library's own loop (the group travels as the `CharacterVirtual`'s user data); **wheel testers** take their layer at construction, so a group change rebuilds all three and re-selects the active one, routed through `World3D.bodyChangedGroup` so setting the *chassis body*'s group works too; **buoyancy's `SpecifiedObjectLayerFilter(MOVING)` was an exact-layer match** and had to become kind-based or a grouped body silently stops floating (a real trap); and **motion changes re-derive the layer from the current group** (a body let go from static keeps its group). Queries narrow via a `group` **field** on `CJoltQueryFilter` (never a new C parameter — the stage-10 rule); past 64 groups the extras stay `.default` with a note rather than aliasing an in-use group, and `collisionGroups` is what makes a typo (a *new* group, silently doing nothing) findable. Example `3D/Physics/Sieve`; `CollisionFilter3DTests` (22). **Per-body motion knobs** (stage 12, `Freedom3D.swift`): **`freedom`** (a `Freedom3D` `OptionSet` over the six DOFs — `.all` / `.plane(normal:)` / `.upright` / `.noTurning` / `.noMoving` / raw `[.moveX, .turnZ]`), **`gravityScale`**, and **`checksPath`** (continuous collision detection) on every `Body3D`, each also an `addBody` parameter. **Load-bearing:** a restricted DOF lives *inside* the body's mass properties, so `cjolt_body_set_freedom` re-derives the whole set from the shape and takes the **mass as an argument** (a travel-locked body reports inverse mass 0 and cannot say what it weighed; Swift passes the explicit `mass:` a body was created with, else 0 = the shape's own) — which also made `cjolt_body_get_mass` fall back to the shape's mass properties, since a pinned body reporting `0` reads as weightless when it is the opposite; `Freedom3D`'s raw bits **are** `EAllowedDOFs`' (the bridge casts), a **zeroed desc field means unrestricted** (so every existing `CJoltBodyDesc()`, the ground slab included, is byte-identical) and an **empty mask reads as `.all`** (the library's one invalid case, which would divide by a zero mass); `.plane(normal:)` **rounds to the nearest world axis** (only whole axes can be taken away, so there is no tilted plane); CCD stays an **explicit opt-in** because the solver's own threshold makes it free while slow (two runs byte-identical, pinned) so guessing would only change results for existing fast bodies, and **speculative contacts catch far more than the naive estimate** (a 0.05 pellet vs a 0.04 wall tunnels at 40 units/s, bounces at 20 — a demo needs genuinely thin plates); a `Character3D` shape-casts its own path and a `Vehicle3D`'s wheels ray-cast the road, so only the chassis would want it. Fixed alongside: the **floor slab kept its collision group** across a `ground`/`unitsPerMeter` change (remembered on the world, since `rebuildGround` builds a fresh descriptor). Example `3D/Physics/Bagatelle`; `Motion3DTests` (16); Guide Ch 23 § *Taking a direction away* + Flattened figure.  **Tracks, ropes, freedoms, and joint-to-joint links** (stage 13, `JointPath.swift`): three more `JointKind3D` cases between two bodies plus a second enum **`JointLink3D`** between two *joints*, through a `connect(_ a: Joint3D, _ b: Joint3D, _:)` overload. **`.path(through:looping:alignment:)`** threads a body onto the smooth curve through a ring of points (`PathAlignment` `.free`/`.rolls`/`.followsPath`/`.fixed`; `drive(at:)` = units/s, `drive(to:)` = a 0…1 fraction, `Joint3D.progress` reads it back; a `Contour` form lays a plan-view curve on the ground). **`.pulley(from:over:and:to:ratio:taut:)`** is one rope over two hooks (`ratio` = rope falls on the second side). **`.allowing(_:at:travel:rotation:)`** is the general joint written as the `Freedom3D` set it keeps (`[]` is a weld, the three turns a `.ball`, one bounded turn a hinge). **`.gear(ratio:)`/`.gear(teeth:and:)`** ties two hinges and **`.rackAndPinion(travelPerTurn:)`** a hinge to a slider. Plus the standing gap closed: a standalone `.swingTwist` now takes `drive(at:)`/`drive(to:)` (the **twist only**, swing left free) and the new **`drive(toward:twist:)`** pointing its bone along a world direction, with `Joint3D.twist` the readback. **Load-bearing:** a link names *joints* because what it constrains is the motion they allow (bodies, axes, and the drift-correction constraints all fall out of them), and its moving end is the non-static body, or the second one named, matching every `connect` call's order; the path spline's normals are **parallel-transported** (a per-point up-minus-along flips on a vertical track) and its points go into **body-1 space** so a track rides a moving carrier, with progress **normalized in the bridge** (only it knows the segment count); a **gear ratio is forced positive** with a note (a negative one leaves the position correction pulling against the velocity rule until the pair detonates); a **kinematic pulley end is refused** with a note (the library's pulley part guards on `IsStatic` where every other guards on `IsDynamic`, so it asserts in a checked build and shoves the body in a release one; the vendored source stays pristine); meshed wheels want `ignoreCollisions(between:and:)` or plain cylinders jam; a link records all four bodies and both source joints so removing any of them cuts it. Example `3D/Physics/Contraption`; `JointKind3DTests` (24); Docs §§ *Tracks, ropes, and freedoms* + *Gears and racks*; Guide Ch 23 § *Machines out of joints* + Machines figure. **The soft-body seams** (stage 14, `Colliding3D.swift`): a cloth becomes a member of the world in three ways. **Contacts** — `world.contacts` carries soft touches (`softBody.touching`/`contacts`/`entered`/`exited` mirror `Body3D`'s), which widened the pair type: `Contact3D.a`/`b`, `Body3D.touching`/`entered`/`exited`, and `Hit3D.body` are now **`any Colliding3D`** (a tiny `AnyObject` protocol over `group`/`isAwake`/`wake()`/`userData`, conformed by `Body3D` + `SoftBody3D`) — every public call site was an identity comparison, so the churn was one cast. **Buoyancy** — `SoftBody3D.density` (relative, 1 = water; **derived from mass/enclosed volume when closed**, else 1) floats a sheet particle by particle. **Queries** — the deliberate reversal: every query now sees soft bodies (a curtain blocks a sightline), so `World3D.pick` folded into `raycast` and `body(under:in:)`/`grabSoftBody` cast the hit. **Load-bearing:** the solver's `SoftBodyContactListener` hands over a whole contact *set* per pass and **never reports a parting**, so began/ended are diffed by `finishSoftStep` after `Update` (the same `ContactRecorder`, registered as both listeners), the event's point/normal come from the first reporting particle, `speed` reads `GetLinearVelocity()` *during* the callback (the arrival velocity — the update averages the new one in afterwards, and the other body's would race), and **a sleeping soft body keeps its touches** (the solver stops *asking*, unlike the rigid removal report) — which is also what tells the buoyancy pass a sunk sheet is lying on something; buoyancy takes a **ratio** (ρ_fluid/ρ_body, since a sheet has no volume to displace) and **per-particle surface heights** computed in Swift, never one tangent plane (one plane over a 4-unit raft on a 9-unit swell curls it into a bowl — a real fixed defect), ramped over a **band** of one particle spacing (a flat sheet's particles all cross at once, so a hard test chatters); and a **body heavier than the fluid has no equilibrium**, so if it is asleep, submerged, and touching nothing it has *stalled*, not settled, and is woken (cloth's drag holds a sinker below Jolt's sleep threshold; comparing measured lift against 1 fails, since a floating raft balances at ~0.92). Example `3D/Physics/Raft`; `Contact3DTests`/`Buoyancy3DTests`/`Query3DTests` grown; Docs §§ *Soft bodies* + *Water* + *Queries*; Guide Ch 24 § *A raft made of cloth* + Raft figure. **Tracked machines** (stage 15): **`addVehicle(…, tracked: true)`** builds the third controller on the shipped `Wheel3D` surface: the wheels become road wheels split into a left and a right band by **which side of the hull they sit on** (driver's right is -x; a one-sided machine is refused, never half-built), `steering` runs the inside band at `1 - 2·|steering|` (half lock stops it, full lock reverses it into a pivot on the spot, and it needs throttle since the bands are turned by the engine), and the readbacks are **`isTracked`** / **`trackSpeed(_:)`** / **`wheels(on:)`** over a nested `TrackSide`. **Load-bearing:** the controller's `ConstructWheel` asserts on the wheel settings type, so a tracked build news `WheelSettingsTV` (flat 4.0/2.0 friction coefficients, which is *why* a band keeps pulling while it slides where a tire's slip curve falls away) and `applyWheelDesc` split into a shared `WheelSettings` base plus two overloads, with `cjolt_vehicle_get_wheel` guarding its `WheelWV` cast (a `WheelTV` has no slip, so `slip`/`slideAngle`/`steerAngle` read 0); **every wheel must belong to exactly one band** (the controller fills each wheel's track index from the two lists and an unclaimed wheel keeps its `-1`); neither steering ratio may be **exactly zero** (the controller divides by them and asserts), so a stopped band is spelled as a very slow one; the axle list is still computed and passed but read **only for the anti-roll bars** (they live on the constraint, not the controller); **track inertia is derived, not exposed** (`0.08 · mass · radius²` at the sprocket, measured across sizes: 0.02…0.15 lands within a few percent, so it stays a constant) and a band's brake is the **sum of its wheels' `brakeTorque`** (so the shipped knob keeps meaning something and `handBrake` pulls the same brake). Closed alongside: **a wheel's `driven` is now live** (`cjolt_vehicle_set_drive` rebuilds the differentials or the two sprockets and re-solves the gearing against the new driven radius, so `topSpeed` is kept), and **the rebuild waits for the step** rather than the assignment because which wheels drive is set a list at a time and an eager report-back undoes the assignments still to come. Example `3D/Physics/Crawler`; `TrackedVehicle3DTests` (20); Docs § *Tracks*; Guide Ch 24 § *Turning without steering* + Crawler figure. **Saving a world** (stage 16, `PhysicsSnapshot.swift`): **`world.snapshot()`** captures the whole world as a **`PhysicsSnapshot`** (a `Sendable`/`Equatable` value wrapping its own bytes, with `bodyCount`/`jointCount`, `init(data:)`/`init(contentsOf:)`/`init(resource:in:)`/`write(to:)`) and **`restore(_:)`** builds it back, over the file pair **`save(to:)`** / **`load(contentsOf:)`**; it holds the rigid tier (every `Body3D` with its collider, pose, motion, and every `addBody` knob, every `Joint3D` incl. gears and racks, the group table and its rules, and `gravity`/`ground`/`bounce`/`maxTimestep`/`unitsPerMeter`/`water`) **plus the tiers above it by value** — characters (capsule + knobs + pose + velocity + facing), vehicles (chassis + every `Wheel3D` knob + gearing + the **live drivetrain**: engine RPM, gear, clutch, per-wheel spin rate and rolled angle), and ragdolls (the **`RagdollPlan` fitting**, per-joint limits, and every limb's pose) — and **soft bodies once their mesh is named**. **Naming bulk geometry** (`PhysicsAsset.swift`) is the one referencing seam: **`Body3D.assetName` / `SoftBody3D.assetName`** (a serializable sibling of `userData`) + **`restore(_:resolving:)` / `load(contentsOf:resolving:)`** over a **`PhysicsAssetResolver`** (`(String) -> PhysicsAsset?`, cases `.mesh` / `.heightfield`) + **`snapshot.assetNames`** (what a file will ask for, read without restoring), so the sketch stays the source of truth about where its assets live (the `resource:in:` rule). **Opt-in by design** (a self-contained file is what makes an arrangement committable); measured **105 KB held → 1.1 KB named** on a 65² terrain + 5k-vertex mesh + 20 crates. **Load-bearing:** a name is **per body, top level only** (a mesh nested in a compound is written whole); an **unresolved name costs that one body, not the restore** (skipped with a note, its joints dropped, and the restored-body list keeps **nils in the gaps** so saved indices still line up); the **fingerprint hashes the geometry (FNV-1a), because counts+bounds is blind** to the case that matters most (`Heightfield.diamondSquare` normalizes to 0…1, so two seeds give identical counts *and* bounds), and a mismatch **notes and restores anyway** (the saved poses are the best answer there is); a **soft body needs a name to be saved at all** (it is nothing but its mesh) and carries its build knobs + every particle position/velocity + the pinned set via new `cjolt_soft_body_get_velocities` / `_set_state` (write goes through the COM transform mirroring the read, and sets `mPreviousPosition` too; 289 particles back at 1.19e-07). **Gotcha:** the reader's `count()` bounds a length against the bytes left, which is right for an array *in* the stream and **wrong for a fingerprint's piece count** (geometry deliberately not there) — reading a 4,225-sample terrain that way threw and refused the whole restore. **Load-bearing for the tiers:** the taxonomy is the design (sort by *what is actually heavy in each*: a character and a vehicle hold nothing heavy, a ragdoll **splits** into a fitting that fits by value and a skinned `Scene` that is the sketch's own asset, still loaded and still what `scene.apply(ragdoll)` writes onto, so a figure restores in a run that never read the file); `Ragdoll3D`'s init split into a `convenience` scene form over a designated **plan** form, so a restored figure is built by the same code as a fitted one; a vehicle **must** carry its drivetrain (without it a car at full throttle falls **6.4 units** behind over 2 s, vs 1.5 with it — and the fair comparison is a *rigid* stack captured mid-collapse, which diverges **2.13**, so this is the already-documented in-flight-contact envelope, not a vehicle gap; a parked machine restores at 0); the chassis is written in the vehicle section but still takes a body index after the loose ones (so a joint naming it survives); **never write a pose as a position then a rotation** — the solver holds a body by its center of mass, so the position is computed against the *old* orientation and a shape offset inside its body (a ragdoll limb's `RotatedTranslatedShape`) lands ~5e-7 out (new `cjolt_body_set_pose` over `SetPositionAndRotation`; invisible for a box/sphere); and the **bytes settle after one restore, not on the first capture** (integration drifts a quaternion off unit length and the bridge normalizes anything handed back), which is the property `aWorldOfEveryTierRoundTripsToTheSameBytes` states — reproducing the normalization in Swift was tried and reverted as a half-measure (15 limbs of 16). **Load-bearing:** the format is **Ollin's own, not Jolt's `PhysicsScene`**, because a snapshot must hand back the *Ollin* description (a restored Jolt shape cannot become a `Collider3D` again, and the collider is what a sketch draws from), which makes restore a **replay of the ordinary `addBody`/`connect` calls** so a restored world is one a sketch could have built; a joint's zero is **the pose its bodies held at connect** (`Joint3D.connectPoseA/B`, recorded at creation), so restore stands them back there, makes the joint, then moves them on (exact for every kind at once: a door saved half open is still half open and still stops where it did, where the naive re-connect reads 0); the desc's three new fields (`linearVelocity`/`angularVelocity`/`startAsleep`) follow the **zero-is-the-old-behaviour** rule, velocity riding `BodyCreationSettings` because `SetLinearVelocity` wakes what it moves, and `startAsleep` is what makes a settled pile come back *settled* (0/12 awake vs 12/12 for the same poses built by hand, zero drift over 60 further steps); a **vehicle's chassis is in `world.bodies`** so capturing filters it out rather than saving a crate that restores wheel-less; the Swift group table is rebuilt in the saved (= solver) order and every pair in the union of old and new counts is reset. `PhysicsSnapshot` wraps `Data` (a `Collider3D` is neither `Sendable` nor `Equatable`), so a snapshot through a file and one in memory are the same value. The payload is **LZFSE-packed** (system codec, deterministic bytes; conditional on being smaller, which the codec reports by refusing an equal-size destination) behind a header carrying a flag, the unpacked length, and an **FNV-1a checksum** — load-bearing because **LZFSE's decoder returns the full requested length from a truncated stream** rather than reporting the truncation, so without it a cut-short packed payload unpacks to rubbish that *parses* into some world (the checksum is what makes "refused rather than half-read" true by construction, and it catches a corrupted-but-complete file too). Measured 1912 KB → 966 KB on a scenery-heavy world and 10.7 KB → 1.2 KB on a settled heap of primitives; the same measurement **rejected narrowing mesh positions to `Float32`** (saves less, lossy for the `Mesh` a sketch reads back from `body.collider`, and dominated where it would help most). Round trip is **exact** (worst pose error 0, a ballistic body stepped on from a restore lands where the uninterrupted one does); the envelope is the solver's in-flight contact bookkeeping (a vehicle's wheel-contact cache included), plus grabs and motors, which a sketch says again. Two bridge getters came with it, surfaced as live **`Body3D.friction` / `.restitution`**. Examples `3D/Physics/Cairn` + `Yard`; `PhysicsSnapshotTests` (23) + `SnapshotTierTests` (13) + `SnapshotAssetTests` (10); Docs § *Saving and loading*; Guide Ch 23 § *Keeping what settled* + Kept figure. **Reading UsdPhysics** (`3D/SceneLoaderUSDPhysics.swift` in the core + `OllinPhysics/UsdPhysics.swift`): **`world.addBodies(from: scene)`** picks up rigid-body / collider / joint annotations authored elsewhere, over the shipped OllinUSD parser — the interchange leg, where **lossy-on-the-way-in is the point** (writing the format would not be, which is why the snapshot stays Ollin's own). Core reads into `package` `ScenePhysics*` value types on the `Scene` (the lights/cameras spec precedent), satellite turns them into ordinary `addBody`/`connect` calls. **Every attribute name was verified against `pxr/usd/usdPhysics/schema.usda`**, and the schema drove the design: a collider's shape is **the prim's own geometry** (no shape attribute; `Cube.size` default **2**, `Capsule` height = span between cap centers = Ollin's meaning); **USD stands Capsule/Cylinder/Cone on z** (`axis`, default `"Z"`) so the turn onto y is baked into the shape's pose; **a rigid body owns its whole subtree** so several collider prims fuse into one `.compound`; a **collider with no body over it is scenery**; and a **joint naming one body holds it to the world**, which the bridge already supported (`resolveBody` → `Body::sFixedToWorld` for `CJOLT_BODY_INVALID`, documented in the header all along), surfaced as the new public **`connect(_:toWorld:)`**. **Load-bearing bug:** a collider prim that *is* its body has an **identity local transform**, so shape scale must come from the collider's **world** transform (the body-relative one supplies position/rotation only, measured against the body's rotation+place, not its scale) — taking it from the local transform silently drops every scale, the common case. Out of scope (said, not faked): articulations, drives + limit API, collision groups/filtered pairs; `upAxis`/`metersPerUnit` stay unapplied (the scene-import contract). Example `3D/Physics/Imported` (hand-authored `yard.usda`); `UsdPhysicsTests` (14); Docs § *Reading physics from a file*; Guide Ch 23 § *Keeping what settled*. **Cloth a skeleton carries** (`addSoftBody(…, skinnedTo:carriedBy:sway:backStop:maxStretch:)` + `SoftBody3D.follow(_:)` / `snap(to:)` / live `swayScale` / `followsSkin`): the solver's *skinned constraints* turn a hanging sheet into a cape, `carriedBy:` naming the joint that carries each vertex (mesh-local, the `pinned:` space) and `follow(_:)` handing over this frame's pose. **Load-bearing:** the **bind pose is whatever the figure is standing in at build**, so no weights are authored anywhere (`invBind = jointWorld_bind⁻¹ · placement`) — and since `placement · invBind⁻¹` is that bind transform back **exactly**, a restored surface with no scene in sight opens standing in the pose it was hung in rather than collapsed on its origin (only the **translation** is divided by `unitsPerMeter`, which is the conjugation `S⁻¹[R|t]S`, so bind and pose compose in meters as they did in units); **`pinned:` means held by whatever holds it** (a joint carries it → held to the figure, none does → held to the world, the shipped meaning); a particle held exactly on the skin (`sway` 0) is made **kinematic** in the bridge, which is what lets **`maxStretch:`** (the LRA long-range attachments, driven at `GeodesicDistance`) work from anchors the *skin* holds — measured: a 6 kg cape hangs 8 cm long on its springs and exactly at rest length capped; **`sway`/`backStop` are raw world-unit lengths, a deliberate departure from the stage-8 rule** (that rule is about *compliance*; a distance needs no normalization — probed 0.02→0.0202, 0.05→0.0504, 0.1→0.1009, backstop 0.02/0.1/0.4→0.0200/0.1000/0.4005), so **watch the sign** of a computed `sway` (negative clamps to 0 and hard-skins that part into a board); **`follow` records and `World3D.step` applies** (once per step, the interpolation the solver eases across), `snap(to:)` applies at once (`particlePositions`/`mesh` are right immediately; the body's own `center` re-centers on the next step); **a changed pose is what wakes the body** — the skin constraints are solved *during* the update, so a settled sleeping cape is left behind by a figure that walks away (a real probe-caught defect), and comparing the flat pose against the last applied one is what still lets a still cape sleep; build order is load-bearing (skinned constraints + their inverse-mass zeroing **before** `CreateConstraints`, which derives the LRAs; `CalculateSkinnedConstraintNormals` before `Optimize`; both no-ops when nothing is carried, so every existing soft body is untouched). The **snapshot writes the skin down** rather than naming a scene (the closures are a sketch's, the `pinned` list precedent; ~4.7 KB for a 169-particle cape, no new `PhysicsAsset` case; format version 3). Envelope: still no self-collision, so a cape passes through its own folds. **Ropes** (`Rope3D.swift`): **`world.addRope(through: [Vector3], thickness:sides:mass:stiffness:bend:damping:friction:bounce:iterations:pinned:maxStretch:group:)`** builds the solver's fifth constraint family, **Cosserat rods** (Kugelstadt/Schoemer 2016), as a **`Rope3D: SoftBody3D`** whose particles are the polyline's points one for one (identity welding, so `pin`/`move`/`positions`/`nearestVertex` speak in point indices) and whose `mesh` override sweeps a `Mesh.tube`, so `drawSoftBody` works unchanged; the readback is **`segments`** (`RopeSegment`: `index`/`start`/`end`/`center`/`direction`/`length` + a **`rotation` the rod itself carries**) posed by the sketch sugar **`withSegment(_:)`** (`withBody`'s twin, local **+y along the rope** matching Ollin's y-standing primitives, +x the twist), plus `points`/`length`/`restLength`/`segmentCount` and live `thickness`/`sides`. **Load-bearing:** a rope is the first body with **no faces**, so `cjolt_soft_body_create`'s `mFaces.empty()` guard now asks for faces only when there are no rods, and the rods must go in **before `CreateConstraints`** (which derives the LRAs by walking vertices, not faces, and `CalculateClosestKinematic` walks rods beside edges → `maxStretch:` works faceless, measured 1.80 → 1.00); the bridge **undoes both things the solver does to the rod list** (`Optimize()` reorders, `CalculateRodProperties()` reverses a rod pointing against its neighbor) via a per-rod remap + flip recorded pre-`Optimize`, so `segments` is in the caller's order and direction (a reversed rod's rotation times a half turn about its own x); **`bend` needed its own measured scale** (a bend constraint holds a *rotation*, so it carries no length to compare against): `compliance ∝ meanRod²/(length³·mass·gravity)` with a measured constant puts `bend: 0.5` at drop/span 0.26…0.33 across 12× length, 2× particle count, and 100× mass, where the naive scale ran from 0.13 to 0.92 over the same lengths — and **both sweeps are non-monotone until ~3000 steps** (a still-swinging cantilever measures its swing); `iterations` is the lever on a long finely-divided rope (40 particles over 6 units: drop/span 0.40 at 5 passes, 0.027 at 20); **restoring needs the rod frames or the rope springs** (0.361 units of drift one step after an exact restore, vs 0.0014 carrying them), and the way in is that **`RodStretchShear::mBishop` is read exactly once at runtime** (`Initialize`, to seed each rod's state) with `mLength`/`mOmega0` already computed, so the bridge writes the saved orientations over it after `CalculateRodProperties` and before `Optimize`; a rope therefore **needs no `assetName` to be saved** (its rest shape is a handful of points, format version 4). **Envelope:** no self-collision (a coil passes through its own turns), and `SoftBodyShape` is face-based so a rope is **invisible to `raycast`/`sweep`/`bodiesOverlapping`** (pinned deliberately) while still colliding with the rigid world through its particles' `vertexRadius` — `grabSoftBody` falls back to the nearest particle along the line of sight; one strand only, no branching. Example `3D/Physics/Rigging`; `Rope3DTests` (20); Guide Ch 24 § *A line that knows how it is turned* + Rope figure. Example `3D/Physics/Cape`; `SkinnedCloth3DTests` (15). Examples `3D/Physics/Stack` / `Tumble` / `Chain` / `Cairn` (a heap of stones laid one at a time, saved, and put back exactly) / `Yard` (a truck, a pacing figure, a fallen one, and a named heightfield floor + cloth banner, all kept in one file) / `Crawler` (a machine on tracks, turning without steering) / `Windmill` (motors + the compound cross) / `Rockslide` (heightfield terrain + eroded slope) / `Trigger` (a scoring hoop = solid bead rim + sensor disc, a sensor tray lit by its load, knock rings scaled by contact speed) / `Stroll` (a walkable eroded island: stairs to a sensor-lit lookout, shoveable crates, `stepHeight` as a live `@Param`) / `Joyride` (a car over an eroded island: chase camera, live gearing/springs/grip knobs, wheels colored by `slip`) / `Ragdoll` (a skinned figure that stands and waves powered, collapses limp, and drags by an arm either way; `B` shows the fitted capsules) / `Drape` (a banner pegged to a washing line flapping in a gusting wind, a sheet thrown over a crate, a deflatable beach ball, all draggable) / `Cape` (a figure striding with a cape clasped at the neck, carried by the skeleton under the skin; it collapses with the figure and the leash can be cut live) / `Rigging` (a limp rope, a chain whose links interlock because every other one is rolled a quarter turn about the rope's own axis, and a leafy vine stiff enough to hold a curve, all hanging in the same gusting wind) / `Flotsam` (crates from cork to nearly waterlogged riding a swell at their own depths, a stone anchor on the bottom, a current carrying the lot past, all draggable) / `Sightlines` (a lamp lighting only the crates its rays reach, a drone holding clearance by sweep, a pulse shoving what a sphere overlaps) / `Sieve` (three colors of bead sorted down one ramp by three `ignoreCollisions` calls, the rules withdrawable live) / `Bagatelle` (a pin table held flat by `.plane()`, live knobs for freedom / weight / path-checking, and a plunger shot that leaves through a thin rail the moment sweeping is off) / `Contraption` (a workshop of the machines a hinge cannot make: a gear pair driving a rack off one shaft, a rope trading a tray for a counterweight, a platter allowed only to rise and spin, a cart on a track) / `Raft` (a cloth raft riding a swell with cargo aboard, a sounding line that stops at her deck, a gate that reports her sailing through); `Docs/Simulation/Physics3D.md`; Guide Ch 23 §§ *Things with weight* (CrateFall + Windmill + Rockslide figures) + *Asking what hit what* (Trigger figure) + *Asking what is there* (Sightlines figure) + *Things that pass through each other* (Sorted figure) + *Taking a direction away* (Flattened figure) + *Keeping what settled* (Kept figure); Guide Ch 24 §§ *Someone to be in there* (Walker figure) + *Something to drive* (Joyride figure) + *Letting a figure fall* (Ragdolls figure) + *Cloth that finds its own shape* (Cloth figure) + *A cape on someone's back* (Cape figure) + *A line that knows how it is turned* (Rope figure) + *Water, and what it holds up* (Floating figure) + *A raft made of cloth* (Raft figure) + *Turning without steering* (Crawler figure). Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *The 3D physics bridge* (+ §§ *Cloth a skeleton carries*, *Saving a world*). What stays out is the solver's own gap: soft-body self-collision and cloth-vs-cloth, and tearing it cannot do at all (`DESIGN-NOTES.md` § *3D mode*). **Volume constraints (tetrahedral jelly) are declined on measurement, not absence** — built and probed against the shipped `pressure`, they tie on holding a weight and on dent recovery and *lose* on recovery from a hard squash, because `ApplyVolumeConstraints` forms its residual from an **unsigned** volume so an inverted tetrahedron reads as satisfied, where a gas law's push grows without bound as the space shrinks; the win they do have (holding an authored shape with corners) belongs to the interior *springs*, not the tetrahedra, and `bend` closes most of it anyway. Numbers + the two reopeners in `ARCHITECTURE.md` § *Why there is no tetrahedral solid*. **The `Jolt/Physics/Hair/` module is likewise declined on measurement** (2026-08-06 audit, apparatus reverted): it takes **no force of any kind** (so it cannot be blown, where every Ollin cloth/rope example gusts via `applyForce`), collides **only with `ConvexHullShape`** (silently skipping the meshes and capsules Ollin's figures are), and needs an **authored groom + skinned scalp** where a sketch generates its own geometry. Decisive number: **the already-exposed rod family carries 20,000 simulated strands at 12.45 ms/frame in ONE soft body** (nothing requires a body's rods to form one chain) against Hair's ~2,500 on its shipped CPU backend, so the Metal path it would need (49 shaders cross-compiled to a `Jolt.metallib` build step, Objective-C++ in the C++-only CJolt target, a new draw path for a Jolt-owned buffer) buys a strand count `Rope3D` already reaches. **If hair is ever wanted, the way in is a multi-strand `Rope3D`**, not this module. Reopeners + numbers in `ARCHITECTURE.md` § *Why there is no hair tier*.

- **Computer vision** (`OllinVision` satellite). A `Camera` (or any `FrameSource`) feeds `VisionAnalyzer` (drops frames it can't keep up with; the display frame is never dropped) and composable trackers over Apple's modern Swift `Vision` API. **Eighteen trackers:** `FaceTracker` / `Face` (76-pt landmarks + roll/yaw/pitch), `ContourDetector` (camera → vector `Shape`), `HandTracker` / `Hand` (21 joints), `BodyTracker` / `Body` (2D pose), `RectangleDetector`, `BarcodeScanner`, `TextRecognizer` (OCR), `ObjectTracker` (lock + follow), `TrajectoryTracker` (parabolic arcs, predicted), `FlowTracker` / `MotionField` (dense optical flow), `PersonSegmenter` / `SubjectSegmenter` (matte + cutout `Image`s), `PointSegmenter` (*whatever you point at*, lifted — point-prompted segmentation over a fetched three-part model: `pick(at:in:)` freezes the frame and encodes once (~0.2 s, `.cpuAndGPU` by measurement — the Neural Engine spends ~9 s specializing these graphs at load against under a second on the GPU pair, warm speed equal), `include`/`exclude` refine against the cached encoding in ~10 ms, three candidate readings with the best score winning; the mask's logits map to a soft rim — byte = 128 + logit·64 — so the upsampled edge lands anti-aliased with alpha 50% on the model's own boundary; prompts and frame both ride the model's 1024² square *squashed*, never letterboxed, so a prompt is `fraction × 1024` on each axis), `ImageClassifier`, `SaliencyTracker` (heat-map + regions + point query), `BodyTracker3D` / `Body3D` (17 joints in meters; canvas / model / camera space), `ModelTracker` (*any* Core ML model — classifier labels / detector boxes / image map / semantic-segmentation `ClassMask`, decoded by observation kind), and `ConceptTracker` (open-vocabulary scoring — *typed phrases as knobs*: `concepts`/`labels`/`confidence(of:)` softmax shares + `similarity(of:)` raw cosine + the `imageEmbedding`/`embedding(of:)` unit-vector surface, over a fetched contrastive image-text encoder pair). **ConceptTracker invariants:** scores are softmax over 100×cosine, *relative to the concept set* — one phrase alone always reads 1, so contrasts are the intended use; text embeddings cache per lowercased phrase (one encoder run each, drained in background — **both inits must queue the initial `concepts` into `pendingPhrases`**, the live path published nothing until they did); compute units are pinned `.cpuAndNeuralEngine` — the GPU graph compiler SIGSEGVs specializing this model family's attention blocks (an MPSGraph attention-fusion pass, crash mid-`MLModel.load`), and ANE+CPU runs both encoders correctly; the tokenizer (`PhraseTokenizer`) is Ollin's own byte-pair-encoding implementation, golden-tested token-for-token against the reference (multi-byte UTF-8 + emoji included — merges file line 0 skipped, first 48,894 merges, ids: 256 stand-ins / +`</w>` forms / merges / start 49406 / end 49407, context 77, truncation re-pins the end marker); the fetched pair is the 2024 permissive-license release — **the 2025 follow-up model is research-only and must never be pointed at by the fetch script**; `ModelTracker.compiledModelURL` stages its cache copy and atomically renames — two trackers loading the same model concurrently read a half-copied `.mlmodelc` otherwise (hit live: paired encoders always double-load); **and every tracker's Core ML load goes through the serializing `ModelLoader`, never `MLModel.load` directly** — concurrent loads of the *same* compiled model directory race in the framework's GPU program preparation and some instances come back silently wrong (measured: four concurrent loads of one compiled path, three corrupt masks, nondeterministic; loads serialized with predictions concurrent, all four byte-identical). Each also runs one-shot on a still `Image` via `detect(in:)`; sync contexts (`setup()`, deterministic figures/exports) run those inline via `waitFor(image) { try await …detect(in: $0) }` — the stills ride as *arguments* (not captures) because `Image` isn't Sendable, it parks the caller (never call it from an async context), and the `@Sendable` closure type is what keeps the work off the caller's actor (a plain closure would inherit main-actor isolation and deadlock the parked main thread). **Coordinate mapping is the crux:** `VisionSpace` (+ each result's `bounds(in:)` / `landmarks(_:in:)`) maps Vision's normalized lower-left origin → Ollin's pixel top-left canvas (y-flip + rect-scale + a `mirrored:` selfie flag) — map results into the *same* `camera.fittedRect(in:)` you drew the frame into, and use Apple's per-result `*InImage` helpers (never hand-roll the conversion — it over-spreads landmarks). Feeds draw via `drawFrame(_ feed:in:waiting:)`. **Hardware split:** classical CV (rectangles / barcodes / contours / object-track / trajectory / optical-flow) *and* OCR run on any Mac; the neural models (body pose, segmentation, saliency, classification, 3D pose) need Apple silicon — surfaced via `isAvailable` / `unavailableReason`. **Gotchas (pinned by probe):** trackers bind to `any FrameSource` via the core `FrameSource` seam + a per-source shared analyzer kept alive by the tap closure (formed in a free function — the render-thread rule); point queries on single-channel buffers (`flow(at:)` / `pixel(at:)`) are clampless nearest-neighbor that *trap* past the last pixel — clamp to `(n−1)/n`, and they take top-down fractions so flip y before the lookup; `InstanceMaskObservation.allInstancesMask` holds *labels*, not a soft matte (use `generateMask`); a Core ML model must load from a *stable* compiled path or it re-specializes (~10 s) every launch; `DetectTrajectoriesRequest` only works through timestamped `CMSampleBuffer`s. Model weights download via `Scripts/fetch-models.sh` (never committed; `StyleMirror`'s is user-trained). Examples span `Vision/WebcamFeed` / `FaceTracking` / `FaceAlign` / `ContourTrace` / `HandTracking` / `BodyPose` / `RectangleScan` / `BarcodeReader` / `TextScan` / `ObjectTracking` / `VideoTrace` / `TrajectoryTracking` / `PersonSegmentation` / `SubjectLift` / `PointLift` / `OpticalFlow` / `SceneLabels` / `EyeCatcher` / `BodyPose3D` / `DepthRelief` / `ObjectDetection` / `DigitReader` / `StyleMirror` / `PaintByClass` / `TugOfWords`. `Docs/Vision/Vision.md`.

- **Geometry & color.** `Vector2` / `Vector3`, `Rectangle` (+ the `Rectangle(fitting:in:)` letterbox everything shares, `contains`, `inset(by:)`, the normalized `point(u:v:)`/`uv(of:)` pair — canvas sugar `Sketch.uv(_:_:)`, example `Basic/NormalizedCoordinates`), `Circle`, `Shape` / `Contour` (arc-length walk helpers + even `resampled(spacing:)`, on both), the `Path` curve builder; per-frame transform stack (`translate` / `rotate` / `scale`, scoped via `withState {}`). The generator/technique catalog below is terse invariants + pointers only — **mechanism, history, and the example/snapshot/test pointers live in [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *The geometry and generator catalog* + § *Color: palettes, dithering, and print separations*** (user-facing API in the named `Docs/` pages), and new catalog depth routes there, not here. Three catalog-wide rules: **determinism is a promise** — no rng decision or position-mutating pass ever walks a Swift `Set`/`Dictionary` directly (sorted candidates, fixed iteration orders; the WFC rule, shared by mazes / space colonization / boids / the physics resolver); **fit a figure by scaling its *points*, not `scale()`** (the CTM scales stroke width too; the trochoid/Lévy rule); and **a geometry cost dial is an artwork parameter, never a `RenderQuality` tier** — geometry is data a sketch reads back and the tiers resolve per-GPU, so a tier would change the *piece* (and make it machine-dependent), not its rendering fidelity; `maxVertices` / `levels` / `resolution` / erosion stay explicit numbers, with `@Param` + `--export-sweep` the tuning path (`RenderQuality` stays GPU-sampling-only; reasoning in ARCHITECTURE.md § *Performance and the quality dial*). The catalog: **`Grid`** — typed `columns × rows` over a `Rectangle`; indexed `points`/`cells` elements (indexed cases are a single loop); `.center`/`.spanning`; `padding`/`gutter` over typed `Insets`; element-type design history settled, don't re-litigate. **Shape booleans & offsets** (vendored Clipper2, BSL-1.0) — offset output must be `SimplifyPaths`'d or cascaded insets balloon; **stroke as shape** `stroked(width:join:cap:)` — the input is *not* normalized first (that would erase open paths); **convex hull** `convexHull(of:)` (monotone chain). **SVG import** — `SVG` + `loadSVG` / `drawSVG` / `fitted(in:)`; control points transform **before** flattening; initial fill **black**, initial winding **`.nonZero`** (don't "harmonize" with Ollin's `.evenOdd` default); unresolvable `url(#…)` → mid-gray, never dropped; unsupported containers skip their whole subtree. `Docs/Drawing/SVG.md`. **Fourier epicycles** — `Epicycles` + `drawEpicycles`; `terms:` is a largest-first *prefix*;
