- **Closing a loop: recognizing a place already scanned** (`Sources/Ollin/3D/ScanGraph.swift` + `3D/PoseGraph.swift`, core). `ScanGraph` wraps a `WorldCloud`, corrects every frame the same way, and keeps a **keyframe** every `keyframeDistance` / `keyframeTurn` (pose, reported pose, a cloud thinned to `keyframeDetail`, and the `reach` of what it saw). A new keyframe near an old one (`searchRadius`, `maximumViewAngle`, at least `separation` keyframes back) is fitted against a patch built from that old one plus its `neighborhood`; an accepted match becomes a robust edge in a pose graph, which is straightened, and the fused cloud is rebuilt from the keyframes' new poses. `add(_:correcting:)` returns an `Update` (alignment, keyframe, `Loop`); `Loop` carries keyframe/recognized/overlap/error/**moved**. The solve is least-squares Gauss-Newton with Levenberg damping on the SE(3) manifold, written from Grisetti et al., *A Tutorial on Graph-Based SLAM* (2010), with the box-plus retraction and numerically-differentiated Jacobians from Blanco-Claraco (2010/2021); the sparse normal equations are never assembled and are solved by diagonally-preconditioned conjugate gradients. **Load-bearing:** one pose is **held** (Grisetti's gauge fix; without it the recovery tests fail, verified red by sabotage, while a "the held pose never moved" test cannot fail because two mechanisms enforce it); the retraction is a body-frame turn plus a world-frame move, and the numeric derivative must go through that same retraction; the matching fit runs **twice, wide then narrow** (a single narrow pass mis-converges on an error bigger than its reach and reports a confident wrong answer: at 0.5 scale drift a one-pass match made the scan *worse*, 0.40 → 0.45, where the two-pass one gives 0.37 → 0.08); the matcher sets `minimumStability` because a match against one flat wall records drift as though measured (`aMatchTheGeometryCannotPinDownIsRefused`, verified red by sabotage); `rebuild()` must reset `world.correction` to the last keyframe's straightened pose or the live feed keeps adding frames where the map used to be; every odometry edge weighs the same (weighting each by its own fit's stability was built and measured on two scenes, helped one and hurt the other, and was dropped); `turnScale` defaults to the scan's own median `reach` (fixed too small in a big room, the scan comes out square but turned). Envelope: drift past roughly `matching.range * reach` is refused rather than guessed. Measured on a staged 1.15-lap sweep with the per-frame fit off: worst keyframe error 0.223 m → 0.048 m. **What it buys is a scan that agrees with itself, which is not the same as a scan in the right place:** one closure pulls the two ends of a walk together and shares the difference along the chain, so it earns most of it at the point of return, and in a small room where nearly every frame sees something already fused the per-frame fit has taken most of the drift out before the walk gets back (the Guide figure's staged room is one such: the closed half draws each wall once and holds a quarter fewer points, while its poses score no better against the truth). `Docs/3D/Phone.md` (Recognizing a place already scanned); examples `3D/Depth/ClosedLoopScan` (no hardware, side by side, true walls overlaid) and `3D/Phone/PhoneWorldScan` (**C** cycles reported / lined up / closing); `PoseGraphTests` (9) + `ScanGraphTests` (14), GPU-free; Guide Ch 26.
- **Accessibility: color vision and motion** (`Sources/Ollin/Color/ColorVision.swift` + `Core/Accessibility.swift` + the `.colorVision` `Filter` case). The author-facing half of the accessibility tier (the viewer-facing half is the bullet below): **`ColorVision`** (a `kind` + a `severity`, the `Sim` struct-plus-`Kind` shape; `.normal` / `.protanopia` / `.deuteranopia` / `.tritanopia` + the `protanomaly(_:)` family; `Kind: ParamOption`) drives `Color.simulated(_:)` / `Palette.simulated(_:)` / `Ramp.simulated(_:)` and **`Filter.colorVision(_:)`** for a whole frame, plus the palette judgement `confusions(under:)` / `confusions()` / `isColorblindSafe()` and the published **`Palette.colorblindSafe`** (Okabe-Ito). **`Sketch.prefersReducedMotion`** reads the system setting through the AppKit/UIKit seam. **Load-bearing:** the model is **Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes 2009**, its Table 1 of 33 matrices parsed from the authors' own page **by script, never retyped**, and it belongs in **linear light** (the matrix projects the *spectral power* of the display primaries, so a display-value application is a visibly different color — the common web mistake, pinned against a hand-written reference by `theMatrixIsAppliedInLinearLightNotToDisplayValues`); a severity between tabulated steps interpolates its two neighbors, which is the authors' own advice; the GPU filter needs **no encode/decode** because a layer already holds linear values, and it clamps **only the low end** (a layer may legitimately carry values above 1 for the tone map) where the CPU `Color` call clamps both (a `Color` is a display value). **The judgement is the whole OKLab distance, lightness included, and that is the load-bearing fact for the docs:** red against green under protanopia keeps **85%** of its distance (one is far darker) but only **20%** across the hue plane, and 6% under deuteranomaly, so *two colors that differ only in hue merge, and a lightness difference saves them* (measured twin pair: 0.281 → 0.014 matched for lightness, 0.601 stepped apart). The 0.06 default tolerance is **measured, not guessed** (`colorblindSafe`'s worst pair 0.076, a familiar six-color chart set 0.007). **`prefersReducedMotion` applies nothing by itself** (only a sketch knows which movement carries the piece) and reads **false headless**, so an export is machine-independent. Example `Color/ColorVision`; snapshot `color-vision` (both paths in one frame, so a drift between the `Color` call and the filter shows); `ColorVisionTests` (12) + `ColorblindPaletteTests` (5) + `ColorVisionRenderProbes` (4, Metal-gated, the filter pinned against the `Color` call for every kind). `Docs/Helpers/Accessibility.md`; Guide Ch 2 § *Will everybody see it?* + the ColorVision figure, Ch 3 § *When somebody would rather it stopped*. Ahead in ROADMAP: complex-script text.

- **Describable output** (`Sources/Ollin/Core/SketchDescription.swift` + the accessibility overrides on `OllinMTKView` in `Core/SketchView.swift`). The viewer-facing half: a sketch says what it shows, so a generative piece is not opaque to a screen reader. **`describe(_:)`** is the whole piece in a sentence, **`describe(_:as:in:)`** one named part with an optional canvas `Rectangle`, **`noDescription()`** clears both; they fill the public `Sketch.accessibleDescription` (**`SketchDescription`** = summary + **`DescribedElement`**s carrying `name`/`text`/`region`, `spoken` = "name: text", `lines` = the summary then the parts). Author-provided only — **nothing is derived from the drawn shapes** (a list of what was drawn is not a description of what it means; the vector recorder is the seam if that ever changes, noted in DESIGN-NOTES). **Load-bearing:** parts are keyed by name in **first-named slot order** and a removed part **keeps its slot**, so a part that comes and goes returns to its place rather than the end of the list — the reading order must never move under somebody walking it by hand (the defect that only showed when the example was *rendered*: a sun below the horizon on frame 0 put "the sun" last all day); `==` compares what *reads* (summary + present elements), never the slots; the AX surface is **pull-based** off the sketch (`isAccessibilityElement`/`accessibilityRole`/`accessibilityLabel`/`accessibilityChildren` overrides), role `.image` with no parts and `.group` with them, the child elements **kept and updated in place** (a screen reader follows an element by identity, so rebuilding them each query drops its place), and **`.layoutChanged` posts only when the set of parts changes** (`SketchDescription.shape`, names + whether there is a summary), never on a reword — else a per-frame description interrupts sixty times a second; a region maps canvas→view through `viewRect` (scale + the y-flip, AppKit counting up from the bottom), no region = the whole canvas; **a sketch that says nothing changes nothing** (the view keeps its default AX, and the SVG is byte-identical — `role`/`aria-labelledby`/`<title>`/`<desc>` are emitted only for a non-empty description). Travels with the work: SVG `<title>` + `<desc>` (the standard place, first children, XML-escaped) and the PDF document title; **PNG/GIF/video carry nothing** (no standard slot — stated, not faked), and `VectorRecording` carries the description so SVG and PDF cannot drift. `OllinMTKView` and `makeOllinMTKView` are internal (were file-private) so the tests drive a real view — the wiring *is* the feature. Example `Basic/Describing` (a day over a bay, words written from the same numbers as the picture, the sun dropped from the description when it sets); **no pixel snapshot** (the output is words); `DescriptionTests` (19, five sabotages verified red). `Docs/Helpers/Accessibility.md`; Guide Ch 29 § *Saying what it shows: describable output* + the SayingWhatItShows figure.

- **Math & motion.** `map` / `dist` / `lerp`, the bare shaping scalars `clamp` / `fract` / `step` / `smoothstep` (spelled + argument-ordered to match the shader library so the vocabulary carries into per-pixel code; `Easing.smoothstep` is the same curve as an `Easing`, renamed from `smoothStep`), the loop-phase helpers `loopProgress(over:phase:)` / `pingPong(over:phase:)` (0…1 sawtooth / out-and-back fold of the sketch clock; `phase` is a fraction-of-loop head start), a resolution-relative `scale`, seedable `random` / `noise` (`randomGaussian` / `randomVector` / `ring` / `randomChoice` uniform+weighted / `shuffled` / `curlNoise` / `signedNoise`, a master `seed()`; gotcha: zero-width `random(a, a)` consumes no roll, so knob-scaled jitter rolls `random(-1, 1) * amount`), **looping noise** (`noise`/`signedNoise` `loop:`+`radius:` overloads — one lap tours a closed circle through the field and closes exactly, `loop` is `fract`-wrapped; the 2-coordinate form rides a 4D lattice, canonical 32-gradient `grad4`, its own contrast gain `gain4` calibrated to the 3D feel; circle centered at (0.5, 0.5) off the integer lattice on purpose), **`fbm`/`signedFbm`** (1D/2D/3D + 2D-loop forms, octaves/gain/lacunarity, normalized so `octaves: 1` == `noise`, name matches the shader lib), **the noise-variant family** (`Math/NoiseVariants.swift` + additions in `Noise.swift`; every name mirrored in the shader lib's noise module, all reseeded by the one `noiseSeed`): `simplexNoise`/`signedSimplexNoise` (1D/2D/3D, Gustavson's structure over the shared seeded permutation, contrast gains 81.3/87.7 calibrated to the Perlin field's ~2%-clip feel), `worley(x,y[,z], feature: .nearest/.second/.border, jitter:)` (seeded SplitMix-hashed cells; `.border` is zero on cell walls), `ridgedFbm` (Musgrave's crease fold with octave-weight feedback, reparameterized onto fbm's gain/lacunarity) / `turbulence` / `warpedFbm` (iq's two-level self-displacement; **`warp: 0` == `fbm` exactly**), each with the 2D `loop:` form, deterministic + pinned by `NoiseVariantTests`, `Generator` legs on the *Layered effects* bullet, `Docs/Generators/Noise.md`, examples `Patterns/RidgeLines` + `Shaders/DomainWarp` + `Effects/Cellular`, snapshots `ridge-lines` + `noise-toolkit`; `Double.tau`; easing (the `Easing` curve catalog + `@Eased`, plus the `Timeline<Value: Tweenable>` keyframe sequencer — worked example `Motion/Timeline`), input smoothing (`OneEuroFilter` + `@Smoothed`), and the damped spring (`Math/DampedSpring.swift`: `DampedSpring<Value: Smoothable>` + `@Sprung`, the third `FrameAdvancing` sibling — each step evaluates the exact closed-form damped-oscillator solution across the under/critical/over-damped branches, so any dt is stable and steps compose exactly (frame-rate-independent, pinned by `DampedSpringTests`); perceptual `duration`/`bounce` knobs (bounce 0 = critical, clamped (−1, 1]), velocity carries across retargets, `kick(_:)` throws; example `Motion/Springs`), all frame-rate-independent and auto-advanced per frame. `Docs/Helpers/Math.md`, `Docs/Generators/Random.md`, `Docs/Generators/Noise.md`, `Docs/Helpers/Animation.md`.

- **Canvas, capture & tooling.** `canvasSize` (the `CanvasSize` enum, 1080² default) previewed per `windowMode` (`.auto` / `.fixed` / `.resizable`); a maintained `Examples/` set incl. a `Recreations/` section; headless capture (`OllinApp.image(of:)`, `--export`) + deterministic frame-sequence export (`--export-sequence` + `--skip` warmup); **video & GIF export** (`OllinApp.exportVideo` / `exportGIF`, `--export-video` / `--export-gif` — AVAssetWriter h264/hevc/prores + ImageIO GIF over one fixed-timestep drive; codec / `--bitrate` / `--quality` control, the last gated `#if arch(arm64)`); **perfect-loop export** (`--export-loop` + the declared `Sketch.loopDuration` — renders exactly one period as a seamless GIF or video by extension; **the GIF lap is computed against the centisecond-quantized rate** `exportGIF` will actually play, or the frame count drifts off one period; example `Motion/PerfectLoop`, seam-verified byte-identical one period apart); **vector SVG export** (`--export-svg` — a CPU-only serializer, curved SDF shapes traced via marching squares; the opt-in `--hatch` / `Hatching` fill-to-line-work transform for pen plotters); **vector PDF export** (`--export-pdf` / `OllinApp.pdf` / `exportPDF` — replays the *same* recorded commands (`OllinApp.recordVectorFrame`, the shared front half) through a Core Graphics PDF context, so the SVG and PDF of a frame can't drift; hatching and `--frame` apply, both vector flags may ride one invocation; 1 canvas px = 1 PDF pt, so the **paper `CanvasSize` presets** (`.usLetter` / `.usLegal` / `.a3` / `.a4` / `.a5`, points-sized, portrait like the sheet) export true-to-size pages, and **`.dpi(300)`** scales the pixel grid for print-res raster while the `.page` case carries the point size so the PDF page stays the physical sheet; `PDFExportTests` pins structure + rasterized-pixel probes); **print-separation export** (`--export-separations` / `OllinApp.exportSeparations` — one grayscale master per declared `printInks` ink + a registration-marked overprint preview; the machinery and invariants live in the *Geometry & color* bullet); **reproducibility metadata** — every PNG / SVG / PDF / video export embeds a one-line JSON recipe (`ExportMetadata`: the recorded seed(s), `@Param` values, working-dir git hash + `-dirty`, frame/fps) — PNG `Description` text chunk, SVG comment, PDF Subject, video description item; GIF has no slot; **the video writer defers `startWriting` to the first rendered frame** so the recipe can carry the seed `setup()` applies (writer metadata is immutable once writing starts); seeds are recorded at init from `variation` and re-recorded by `randomSeed`/`noiseSeed` (`Sketch.recordedRandomSeed`/`recordedNoiseSeed`; `ExportMetadataTests`); the whole export/bench flag surface lives in `OllinApp.handleCommandLine(_:makeSketch:)` (`Sketch.main()` routes through it, and `OllinLive` runs it on a *loose watched sketch file* — `swift run OllinLive Sketch.swift --export-gif …` — compiling the file only when a flag matches, so any Guide-style sketch exports without needing a target); the `extend(...)` `SketchExtension` seam (setup / beforeDraw / afterDraw / afterFrame / frameRendered, opt-in `wantsRenderedFrame` / `wantsRenderedTexture`); **live reload** (`swift run OllinLive`, the typed `@Param` inspector controls, the shared `FrameStats`); the **live-coding performance host** (`swift run OllinLiveCoding` — evaluate-on-command over the same engine; see the *Live coding* block under § *Live reload* below, `Docs/Tools/LiveCoding.md`); the **examples gallery** (`swift run OllinExamples`) — a two-sidebar host on the OllinLive window recipe (fixed `HStack` + `.contentSize` + unified gradient toolbar + title-bar accessory toggles, the chrome shared via the core `WindowChrome.swift`): left, the example list as a collapsible tree mirroring the folder groups (`ExampleCatalog.tree`, disclosure state persisted, an Xcode-style bottom filter bar) — arrow keys navigate it because the canvas takes keyboard focus **only on click** (`SketchView(keyboardFocus: .onClick)`, a glass "click the sketch to use the keyboard" hint floated only for sketches whose *source* reads keys, `Example.usesKeyboard`); right, the hidable inspector (⌘/, the shared `MonitorCardView` + `@Param` knobs — so the gallery omits `OllinHUDCommands`/the detached panel). `Examples/3D` is grouped into topic subfolders (`Camera`/`Depth`/`Effects`/`Environments`/`Geometry`/`Lighting`/`Materials`/`Phone`/`Raymarching`), targets renamed to match (`Example-3D-<Group>-<Name>`); render-correctness snapshot tests (`Tests/OllinTests`, re-record via `OLLIN_RECORD_SNAPSHOTS=1`); CI. The AVFoundation link lives in the core for offline export only. `Docs/Output/Export.md`, `Docs/Core/Canvas.md`, `Docs/Helpers/Parameters.md`.
- **Path-traced export.** `--path-traced [N]` (+ `--pt-depth`, default 8) switches the still/sequence/video exports to an offline path tracer over the *same* scene the raster window tunes live — physically soft area shadows, color bleed, mirror-in-mirror reflections, real solid/thin glass with tinted transparent shadows, emissive meshes sampled as lights, base-color textures read at hits, and a thin-lens depth of field (`Camera3D.aperture` / `focusDistance`, read by the traced camera only). Programmatic switch `OllinApp.pathTracedExport` (`PathTracing`); the bare flag's sample count follows `--render-quality` (64/256/512); needs an RT GPU, else a one-line note + the raster render; contact sheets, vector exports, and `--bench` stay raster by design. **Load-bearing:** the trace runs in its **own completed command buffers before the frame's other encoding** (chunked adaptively toward ~1s of GPU work per buffer — watchdog + progress; the still export prints a rewriting sample line, the sequence drivers keep their per-frame line) — only then may the frame's shadow pass re-fill the shared accel/offsets rings the trace used; the scene handoff generalizes the caustic-geo pattern (`buildShadowAccel(pathTraceMats:)` breaks coalesced runs on **any** batch-finish change and uploads one `OllinMaterial` per geometry); **matcap batches are excluded from the traced scene and keep rastering** (the unlit emissive-look finish — an area light's glowing prop must not swallow the light's own next-event rays); the traced layer composites **once, at the first solid mesh batch** (premultiplied over, so silhouette edges blend and draw order against 2D holds) with the fragment **writing the primary pinhole depth**, so wireframe/grid/matcap and the untraced 3D kinds (points, strands, raymarched fields, instanced meshes/fields) still raster and occlude correctly. Integrator conventions are **raster parity**: punctual lights keep the un-normalized legacy Lambert for standard/toon/gooch and the full microfacet terms for the physically-based finish; area lights are physical with `color` as radiance (a **tube radiates by its silhouette ribbon, length × diameter**, never its full skin — the full-skin form overshoots by ~π); the environment samples the same equirect at the same normalization × intensity as the skybox/IBL; **no environment → the flat ambient is the miss field**, which makes an ambient-only scene an exact furnace (`PathTraceTests` pins the mean within 3/255). Variance controls, each from the published technique: **environment importance tables** (512×256 latitude-weighted luminance CDFs + solid-angle pdf grid, cached per equirect texture; the environment strategy reads the table's own filtered mip so one lamp texel can't out-spike its cell), the **power heuristic** through one shared lobe-pdf function on both sides (bias otherwise), **filtered importance sampling** on lobe-side environment reads (mip from the sampled pdf's solid angle — mirrors stay sharp), **path-space regularization** (next-event evaluation at *indirect* vertices floors roughness at 0.25 — the single-pixel-glitter fix, since a near-mirror NEE has no pdf division; the continuation stays exact so mirror chains stay sharp), and Russian roulette from depth 3. **Deterministic by construction**: sampling is a pure function of (pixel, sample index, bounce) and accumulation order is fixed, so the same command renders the same bytes (pinned). **Glass** (`transmission > 0`, physically-based): a stochastic weight-free mix by the transmission fraction routes that share of paths to a microfacet dielectric — VNDF micro-normal, exact dielectric Fresnel, TIR reflects, a thin pane (thickness 0) passes straight through with no bend, a solid bends by Snell and pays Beer-Lambert `attenuationColor^(d/attenuationDistance)` per interior segment (tint applied on the crossing *in* only, never the exit) — with estimator weight the outgoing Smith G1 alone (the VNDF pdf cancels the rest), so a polished clear sphere is a furnace-exact lossless dielectric (pinned within 4/255). **A glass vertex runs no next-event pass and marks `prevNEE = false`; the next miss or emissive hit then takes *full* weight** — the MIS bookkeeping rule; breaking it under- or double-weights everything seen through glass. **Transparent shadows:** NEE visibility runs a closest-hit walk that passes through transmissive geometry (albedo × transmission per thin sheet / solid entry + Beer inside, a straight line — the bent, focused kind stays the live `caustics()` feature's), gated by a scene-level any-transmission flag so glass-free frames keep the cheap any-hit ray. **Textures & surface maps:** the accel run also breaks on map identity across all five slots (gate-aware: a texture only counts while its finish gate — `normalScale`/`mrGate`/`occlusionStrength`/`emissive.w` — is up); a per-geometry **bindless five-slot resource-ID table** (`OllinPTTexEntry`: base, normal, metallic-roughness, occlusion, emissive; `useResources` residency; the white stand-in in an unbound slot is the base slot's multiply identity, and the gated slots are never read unbound) is sampled at the hit's interpolated uv at the **ray-cone mip** (pixel footprint at the eye + spread × path distance, over the triangle's own texel density; per-map resolution folded in per slot). The maps compose exactly as the raster surface-mapped fragment: the **normal map** bends through the interpolated vertex-tangent frame (bitangent = sign · cross(N, T), bend on the raw frame, flip to the viewed side after) while **`OllinPTHit.Ng` keeps the geometric normal for the ray machinery** (epsilon offsets, the below-horizon break — the raster's detail-vs-position split; unmapped scenes are untouched since Ng == N there); the **metallic-roughness** channels multiply the *finish-table* factors (which carry the drawing-state × mesh-material product — the vertex w slots carry only the drawing state, so a gated hit must read the table); the **occlusion ramp dims the environment share only** — the env NEE term at the vertex and, via a carried `aoPrev`, the paired lobe-side miss pickup, both halves scaling together so the MIS pairing stays consistent; direct lights, emissive hits, and surface-to-surface bounce light stay undimmed (the trace occludes those with real geometry); the **emissive map** multiplies the factor at hits and at mesh-light NEE sample points, while **selection, pdf, and the reverse MIS weight all stay factor-based** (map texels never exceed 1, so the factor pdf's support covers the glow). **Triplanar** base color + normal map project at hits through the raster's own `ollin_triplanar_surface` (level-0 reads, matching the raster's mipless textures). **Emissive-mesh sampling:** a CPU power CDF over the glowing geometries' triangles (luminance × area, two-sided); because selection is power-proportional and the point uniform, the area pdf at *any* sampled point is luminance / total power — no per-triangle lookup — which is what makes the reverse power-heuristic weight at a BSDF-found emissive hit computable from the hit alone (full weight at depth 0). Envelope: height (parallax), the detail pair, and decals stay raster-side (a traced hit reads the flat surface at its plain uv), layered lobes trace as their metallic-roughness base, thin-pane pass-through ignores frosting (roughness shapes its reflection only), and the shadow walk's tint skips the per-interface Fresnel. `PathTraceTests` (furnace ×3 incl. glass, parity, texture parity, transparent + tinted shadow, the emissive floor pinned to the analytic small-emitter irradiance ±18% with a low-spp noise bound, determinism ×3, off-is-off, snapshot `path-traced-3d`; the maps probes: normal-map halves apart + raster parity, mr map byte-equal to its factors, occlusion black/white furnace exact, emissive map head-on to the analytic byte and through the light sampler to the scaled floor irradiance, triplanar trace-vs-raster parity — the flat-face probes stage on `Mesh.plane`, since **`Mesh.box` carries no uvs and a uv-less mesh silently degrades every uv-gated map**); example `3D/Effects/PathTraced`; `Docs/Output/PathTraced.md`.

- **Live recording (performance capture)** (`Sources/Ollin/Export/SessionRecorder.swift` + `Core/CaptureAudioSource.swift`). Records a live run in real time — frames as rendered, the sketch's sound as played — into one movie: `startRecording(to:audio:)` / `stopRecording()` / `isRecording` sugar over the public `SessionRecorder` extension (`audio:` `.sketch` default / `.microphone` / `.sketchAndMicrophone` / `.none`; `codec:` reuses `VideoCodec`, h264 default; default file `~/Movies/Ollin/<Sketch> <stamp>.mov`); hosts wire ⌘⇧R (OllinLiveCoding stage chip + saved toast; OllinLive menu) and OllinLive takes `--record` (film from the first frame). Video rides the **CGImage frame-grab hook** (`wantsRenderedFrame`, armed only while recording — the full-fidelity off-screen re-render; the GPU-texture hook skips 3D/shadows/effects, so don't switch to it without closing that gap) into AVAssetWriter's **real-time mode** with host-clock PTS (a slow frame lasts longer; a frame the encoder can't take is **dropped, never awaited**, and counted). Audio: the core `CaptureAudioSource` seam (Mirror-discovered off the sketch's stored properties like `ExportAudioSource`; Synth/AudioPlayer/Tone conform — **a node allows one tap per bus**, so capture rides `installAnalyzerTap`'s fan-out via `CaptureTapRelay`, never a second tap) feeds per-source `AudioCaptureSink` lanes mixed on the writer queue every 100 ms, 150 ms behind the clock, with the offline `tanh` knee. **Load-bearing:** a lane places buffers by host-time stamp with a running counter — the counter wins under 50 ms jitter, the stamp wins past it — so **a device gap becomes silence, never a slide** (the A/V drift killer on long takes); the audio track is declared whenever mode ≠ `.none`, so a late-entering instrument lands mid-file in sync; **a recording survives a live-reload swap** (`reload(to:)` carries `sketch.sessionRecorder` to the new instance, whose `setup` hook rescans its instruments; hosts must address the *current* sketch via `SketchSession.currentSketch` — `core.sketch` deliberately stays the first mount, and a recorder wired to a stale instance films nothing); a canvas-size change **finishes the file cleanly** (a movie's frames are all one size); quit, Control-C, and SIGTERM finalize via `stopAndWait()` (an unfinished writer is an unplayable file; the recorder installs its own DispatchSource signal handlers once, kept for the process's life); `.extended` refuses (HDR stays offline), `.wide` tags P3; a `VideoPlayer` soundtrack doesn't reach a take yet (its tap carries track time, not host time); headless (`isRenderingHeadless`) refuses with a pointer at `--export-video`. Verified live: a 20 s take at a solid 60 fps with sound in every 5 s window **across a mid-take hot reload**, finalized by SIGTERM. `AudioCaptureSinkTests` (6, the timeline placement) + `SessionRecorderWriterTests` (2, a real writer, no audio hardware — CI-safe on purpose); example `Export/Record`. `Docs/Output/Recording.md`; Guide Ch 29 § *Keeping the take*.

- **Per-frame profiler** (`Sources/Ollin/Core/FrameProfile.swift` + `Renderer/FrameCapture.swift`). The "why is my sketch slow" tier over the shipped `FrameStats`/extension-seam machinery: a public `FrameProfile` rides `FrameInfo` and `FrameStats`, carrying the four-way time split (`cpuDrawMS` / `cpuEncodeMS` / `gpuMS` / `waitMS`), the submitted work (`drawCalls` / `passes` / `computeDispatches` / `batches`), and the geometry each path drew (triangle / fringe / SDF instance / field quad / image / glyph / mesh / point splat / particle / clip). **Load-bearing:** the counts are taken **in the encode loop beside each `drawPrimitives`**, never derived from `drawer.batches`, so a batch the renderer skipped (an image whose texture failed to build) is not counted, and `FrameProfileTests` pins that difference; **every render encoder in the renderer goes through the one `countedEncoder(_:_:caller:)` choke point** (a `#function`-defaulted label, so a new shadow / probe / filter pass counts itself and can name itself), which is why the pass number stays honest as passes are added; **the wait is timed apart from the encode** (the frame-time readout's own lesson: timing across the triple-buffer semaphore measures vsync and says nothing), so `waitMS` is reported as headroom rather than folded into CPU cost; **GPU time comes from the command buffer's own `gpuStartTime`/`gpuEndTime` in the completed handler**, which runs off the main actor, so it rides an `OSAllocatedUnfairLock` and **reads the timestamps into a local before the lock's closure** (`MTLCommandBuffer` is not `Sendable`) and lands one or two frames late, hidden by the smoothing `SketchView` applies to all three measured times; headless `renderedFrame` resets the counts too, so an export and a test read the same profile. UI: a **cost row on the shared `MonitorCardView`** (so all three hosts and the detached panel get it at once) in the Photos-info-window idiom the card already follows: **two bars on one scale (the frame period), deliberately not stacked** (the CPU builds frame N while the GPU draws N-1, so the two overlap rather than add), over three icon-led counts with the per-path breakdown in their tooltips; the strip's **CPU cell now reads `profile.cpuMS`** (draw + encode) rather than `frameTimeMS` (draw alone), or one name would carry two numbers. **GPU frame capture**: `Sketch.captureGPUFrame(to:)` and `View ▸ Capture GPU Frame` (⌘⇧G, a one-shot `OllinHUD.captureFrameKey` the runner takes and clears) wrap **the frame that asked** (the request is taken between `performDraw` and `render`) in an `MTLCaptureManager` `.gpuTraceDocument`, and print the frame's pass list beside the path; **Metal refuses to capture unless the process was launched with `MTL_CAPTURE_ENABLED=1`**, so the refusal prints the exact line to run rather than failing silently. `FrameProfileTests` (9, incl. the one-time first-frame setup passes, which are real and stay counted); no pixel snapshot (the output is numbers). `Docs/Tools/Profiling.md`; Guide Ch 29 § *When it gets slow: the cost row*.

- **Installation mode** (`Sources/Ollin/Core/Installation.swift` + `Live/InstallationHost.swift` + the clock in `Core/SketchView.swift` / `Core/Sketch.swift`). The unattended-run tier: `open var installation: Installation` declares a piece left running for days, `.on` filling the screen (native full screen, so the menu bar and Dock go with it and the window keeps the keyboard — a borderless one cannot become key without a subclass), hiding the pointer, and holding the display awake; `--installation` / `--no-installation` put any sketch either way with no edit. **Load-bearing:** the **sketch clock is the sum of its own frame steps**, each capped at `SketchRunner.longestFrameStep` (0.25 s), never `now - startTime` — a gap in the frames (a display asleep overnight, a drag, a stall) is then a pause rather than one eight-hour `deltaTime` that throws every integrator into the far distance; a capped step also never feeds the fps readout (a stall is not a frame rate). The **GPU clock restarts** (`Sketch.shaderClock`, the one `Float(time)` choke point in `performDraw`): a 32-bit clock in seconds cannot hold a week (**measured: at 604,800 s, `Float(t + 1/60) == Float(t)`, so shader-driven motion stops dead; at 86,400 s the ULP is already half a frame step**), so `.automatic` wraps at a **whole number of `loopDuration` laps** (invisible by construction, capped near 1000 s), while a sketch with no declared loop keeps its clock running rather than take a silent jump; `installation` off is identity, so nothing else changes. `frameCount` reaches the GPU with `UInt32(truncatingIfNeeded:)` (a plain init traps after ~2.3 years at 60 fps). Power is **both** a held `ProcessInfo.beginActivity([.idleDisplaySleepDisabled, .idleSystemSleepDisabled])` **and** a 30 s repeat of `IOPMAssertionDeclareUserActivity` — the assertion alone stops display sleep but **not the screen saver**, which arms off the input-idle timer that declaring activity resets (verified live in `pmset -g assertions`: `PreventUserIdleSystemSleep` + `PreventUserIdleDisplaySleep` + `UserIsActive`). A screen change arrives as a **burst** (a display waking reports many arrivals in a second), so `didChangeScreenParametersNotification` is **debounced 0.5 s**, then the window is put back on a screen that still exists and `SketchRunner.displayChanged(to:)` retimes `preferredFramesPerSecond` (read once at view build otherwise). The log **flushes on the spot** (stdout to a file is buffered, and a killed run then loses the whole trace). Applies on the standalone `OllinApp.run` path only — the live host and the gallery own their windows — and an export has no window for any of it; the shader-clock restart does ride the sketch into an export, landing on a whole lap there too. A **loose single-file sketch reaches that path through `OllinRun`** (`Sources/OllinRun/`): `ollin <file> --installation` compiles the file once through `SketchLoader` and hands the sketch to `OllinApp.run`, so the window, the supervisor, the checkpoint and the warp are the shipped ones and nothing downstream knows the sketch was loose; the script routes on the flag (`--calibrate` too), and the live host, which cannot honour one, says so once per session when a sketch it is hosting declares an installation. It **reloads nothing**, by design: a wall runs the code it was started with, and the live host is where a piece is worked on. **Load-bearing:** the checkpoint is filed under the **unqualified** type name (what `String(describing:)` gives), so the fresh per-compile module a loose load carries (`OllinRuntimeSketch_<token>`, different every time) never reaches the file name and a piece resumes across launches instead of silently starting over every morning; the compile runs **before** `OllinApp.run` so a broken file fails once with the compiler's own message rather than in each supervised child until the watch gives up. `OllinRun --selftest` pins the name across two compiles (printing both modules, so the check cannot pass for the wrong reason) and the declaration surviving one. Verified live on a loose file: full screen from the flag alone, a checkpoint every 2 s, `kill -9` on the child bringing the piece back resumed with its wall intact, and the watch stopping with no orphan left. **Checkpoint and restore**: `Installation(checkpoint: .every(seconds:))` writes the run down on a cadence, `@Saved` (a `Codable` property wrapper found by the same mirror walk as `@Param`) marks what to keep, and a relaunch puts back the seed, the clock, every `@Param`, and every saved property. **Load-bearing:** the restore is **two-phase** — seed and knobs *before* `setup()` (so a sketch builds from the numbers it built from last time), state *after* (that is where `setup()` fills its properties in); **each property carries its own JSON**, so a renamed one finds nothing, a retyped one fails alone, and neither costs the properties behind it (pinned by a property declared *after* the failing one, verified red by sabotage); the file is **atomic** (a power cut cannot leave half of one), **JSON a person can read** (nested as real values, not escaped strings inside strings), keyed on the sketch **type name** + a format version, and **nothing here ever stops a piece from starting** — every mismatch is logged and skipped. `--fresh` ignores a file without deleting it; `saveCheckpoint()` / `forgetCheckpoint()` are the manual pair. Saves ride the **sketch clock** (a paused piece does not come back owing a pile), plus one on `applicationWillTerminate` and one from a `DispatchSourceSignal` on SIGTERM/SIGINT (`signal(_, SIG_IGN)` first, or the default action kills the process before the source runs) — verified live: 10 tiles at the last cadence save, 14 in the file after a `kill -TERM`. A save is **never written before the run has read the file it replaces** (`didSetup`): every save asked for from outside the frame loop (a schedule closing for the night, a quit, a stop signal) can arrive before the first frame, which is where the restore happens, and writing then puts an empty run over a piece that has been growing for days (found live, by launching a piece inside its own dark hours). **Restoring is off even under `.on`** (it changes what a piece does on launch), and the live host and gallery never checkpoint at all (restoring old state on every reload is the opposite of iterating). GPU-held state is **out of reach by construction** (accumulation, feedback, sim fields, compute buffers are framework textures) and the docs say so. `Vector2`/`Vector3`/`Color`/`Rectangle`/`Insets` gained `Codable` for this. **Restart on failure** (`Live/Supervisor.swift`): `Installation(restarts: .onFailure)` turns the process into a **supervisor that owns no window** (the branch sits in `OllinApp.run` *before* the window, so the process owning the window is the replaceable one) which runs the piece as a child and starts another when a run ends badly. **Load-bearing:** the child is marked by an **environment variable** (`OLLIN_SUPERVISED`), never an argument — the sketch's own flag surface stays untouched, and the round trip through the env the supervisor actually builds is what pins that a child never supervises in turn; a stall is found by a **heartbeat written from the main run loop** (`Heartbeat`, a `Timer` in `.common`), never from the frame loop, because a `noLoop()` still and a piece dark for the night both legitimately draw no frames while the run loop keeps turning, and a main thread stuck in a frame is exactly what stops it; **a stalled child that exits tidily still counts as a failure** (it can catch the SIGTERM, save, and `exit(0)`, which by status alone is indistinguishable from somebody quitting the piece — read that way, the wall stays dark; sabotage-verified red); the first heartbeat gets a **120 s startup grace** (a piece has not answered at all until its first frame, and killing a heavy `setup()` there makes such a piece unable to start at all; sabotage-verified red); the stop is **SIGTERM then SIGKILL after 5 s** (a hung main thread never services the signal, and `CheckpointSignals` has already set `SIG_IGN` on it); `RestartPolicy` counts a run shorter than 30 s as a **strike** with a growing wait (1/5/15/30/60 s) and **gives up after five**, one real run clearing the record, so a nightly crash recovers for ever while a piece broken at launch does not spin all night; the supervisor **forwards SIGTERM/SIGINT to the child** (a signal sent to it by name alone would orphan the piece) from a `DispatchSourceSignal` built **non-isolated**. **Off even under `.on`** (a piece that crashes while you work on it should stay crashed), `--no-installation` takes it off with everything else, and it exists only on the standalone path. Verified live: `kill -9` and `kill -STOP` on the child each brought the piece back within a second or two, resumed from its checkpoint, with no orphan after the supervisor itself was stopped. **Behavior by time of day** (`Core/Schedule.swift`): `Installation(schedule:)` is a list of `Period`s (`ExpressibleByArrayLiteral`, so the call site never names the type), each a start `Time` (`ExpressibleByIntegerLiteral`, `.at(9, 30)` to the minute) + a name + `shows`, with `.open(from:to:)` the sugar for one showing period and one dark one; a sketch reads `scheduledPeriod` / `scheduledProgress` **anywhere, at a desk as much as on a wall** (the read is off the *declared* value, so only the darkening needs the installation). **Load-bearing:** the current period is **the last one that has started**, falling back to the *last of the day* when now is before the first — the small hours belong to last night, and read instead as "the first that has not started yet" a piece would come on at midnight in an empty building; that same rule is what makes a night crossing midnight one period rather than two. `Time.wrappedMinutes` treats **a time to itself as zero, not a whole day** (progress at a start must be 0), so the one-period case restores the day length explicitly, and `Time` itself **wraps into the day** (`.at(24)` is midnight, `.at(9, 90)` is 10:30) because every reading compares minutes since midnight and a time outside the day would simply never come round. The host pushes the state to the runner on **every reading, not only on a change**, and holds the runner **directly** (`InstallationHost.attach`) rather than through `OllinActiveSketch.runner`, which a piece only registers with on its first *frame* and a piece opening outside its hours has none — the defect in the first build, caught live: the log said dark while the frames kept advancing. Going dark **pauses the frames** (`SketchRunner.setShowing`: `isHidden` + `isPaused`, and a still sketch keeps its own pause across it), so the clock stops with them and the morning resumes where the night stopped, writes the state on the way down, and **releases the power assertions** so the panel can rest. Verified live with a boundary a minute out: frames advanced, froze flat across the dark period (a screenshot of a black window), and advanced again when it opened. **Fitting the wall** (`Core/Projection.swift` + `Core/ProjectionCalibration.swift` + `Live/ProjectionCalibrator.swift` + `Geometry/Homography.swift` + the projected present twins in `ShaderEffects.metal`): `Installation(projection:)` corner-pins the presented frame onto what it is thrown at (`shows` the part of the canvas this machine carries, `corners` where that lands, `blend` how far the edge fades reach, `gamma` the projector's own answer to the standard curve), with ⌘K raising four draggable handles over the running piece and the numbers kept per display. **Load-bearing:** the installation window **always** presents through the projected pass — fit corners reproduce the old SwiftUI letterbox exactly, so ⌘K has corners to drag on *any* piece and no `if` can swap view identity (and the MTKView with it) mid-run; the geometry then renders at the **canvas's own proportions** (`MetalRenderer.pictureSize`, reusing the render-size seam the temporal upscaler opened) because the view now fills the display and a drawable-shaped target would carry oval dots and strokes of two widths. The **fade is applied to light, not to a code value** (after the tone map, before the encode), which is what makes two beams weighted `w` and `1 - w` sum to exactly one coat; `gamma` 2.2 is the standard curve and yields exponent 1, no correction (**sabotage-verified**: weighting as a code value reads 0.87 coats mid-band). `blend` widths are **canvas fractions, the same units as `shows`**, so both machines are told the same number about the same band and their two fades are the same function of the same wall. The warp rides **only the two paths that present into a drawable** (`encodePresent(projected:)`), so an export, a frame grab and a Syphon feed stay unwarped (pinned by a test that diffs an export against itself with a warp set). The two present fragments are **new functions, not a branch in the shipped pair** (a grown fragment re-contracts under fast math, the rule the wide-gamut twin already carries). `fwidth` is read in **uniform control flow** — a lane that returned early has no neighbors to read — so the behind-the-horizon guard multiplies the answer rather than returning. Corners are filed under the **display's own UUID** (`CGDisplayCreateUUIDFromDisplayID`, which survives a replug where the display number does not) because a keystone belongs to the room and not to the work, so every piece shown there opens square; nothing about reading that file may stop a piece from starting (the checkpoint's rule). A drag that folds the quad has **no map**, so the handles read from the corners themselves and the last good placement stays up: reading them back through the map throws all four across the display, and dropping the placement makes the picture vanish under the hand. The pointer takes the warp backwards, so a piece being lined up still reads `mouseX` in its own canvas, and a **described part's frame goes through it forwards** (the box around its four mapped corners, since a screen reader takes a rectangle) so an accessibility element still points at the piece of wall it is talking about. `--calibrate` opens with the handles up, and ⌘K rides a **local key monitor** (a full-screen window has no menu bar to hang a command on) that passes every other key through untouched. `InstallationTests` (12, the clock restart verified red by sabotage) + `CheckpointTests` (11, the per-property isolation verified red by sabotage) + `SupervisorTests` (15) + `ScheduleTests` (13) + `ProjectionTests` (22, both pixel claims verified red by sabotage: a flipped axis and a fade in the wrong space); no pixel snapshot (the warp is screen-only, so it is presented into an off-screen display texture through the same `encodePresent` instead). **Where the canvas is on the desk** (`Sketch.canvasOnScreen` / `screenFrame`, pushed each frame by the runner): the rectangle this canvas covers in screen points, and the screen it covers it on, so several windows of one sketch can look into one world (and, ahead, several displays can). **Load-bearing:** it is measured **the way the canvas is** (origin at the top-left of the *primary* screen, y down), because a sketch mixing a y-up desk with a y-down canvas draws its world upside down; `NSScreen.screens.first` anchors that origin, never `.main`, which is the screen with the key window and is a different screen on two displays. It reports the **view's** rectangle rather than the window's, so a host with a title bar or a sidebar says where the drawing is and not where its furniture is. It is read **every frame** (a window being dragged is current in the frames drawn during the drag), and is `nil` with no window, which is what lets a piece fall back to being its own world in an export. `PlacementTests` (3, the turn-over verified red by sabotage). **Several displays from one machine** (`Core/Displays.swift` + `Live/DisplayWall.swift`): `Installation(displays:)` (`.one` / `.spanning` / `.mirroring` / `.parts([Projection])`) puts one canvas on every display the machine drives, as a window and a drawable per display fed from **one canvas render**, presented once per display **in the same command buffer**, so every beam of a wall carries the same frame. **Load-bearing:** the placement **travels with the display** (`encodePresent(placement:)`) and is never read off the renderer's own, or every beam carries the drawing display's part (pinned on pixels, sabotage-verified red); `.spanning` derives each part from **where the displays actually are** (`WallPlan`, pure arithmetic over desk rectangles, so a wall of six is checked with none of them plugged in), dividing the machine's own `projection.shows` rather than the whole canvas (four beams over two machines is then two declarations of two), and the **gap between two monitors carries canvas nobody sees**, which is what the arrangement looks like from in front of it; **exactly one part is the primary one** and the piece's own window is moved to it, or a run whose display got no declaration would draw frames with nowhere to put them (sabotage-verified red); the canvas is drawn at the size the **wall** asks for (`MetalRenderer.wallDemand`, the largest drawable size over its own `shows`), capped at `SketchRunner.largestPicture` (16384) and the cap said out loud, since a wall quietly drawn at half the resolution somebody paid for is noticed at the opening; a wall window **takes the keyboard only while the handles are up** (`OllinDisplayWindow.canBecomeKey`, so a key-reading piece never goes deaf on the display it is drawn in), joins **all spaces** (a swipe must not take the piece off the wall), and **never counts as a real window** for the quit-on-last-close rule; ⌘K raises the handles on **every** display at once (the key monitor moved to `InstallationHost`, one per app) while the corner keys go to the window last clicked, each display still filed under its own UUID. **`--displays one|spanning|mirroring`** puts any piece on a wall, and **`--rehearse [n]`** lays that wall out as `n` windows on one desk (it implies an installation, keeps the pointer and the title bars, and reads a `.one` declaration as `.spanning`, since asking for a rehearsal is asking to see a wall): the parts go **side by side with a gap, never overlapped** (two beams sharing a band add their light, two windows only hide each other), and a rehearsed display is **filed under nothing**, so nothing dragged there can reach the room's calibration. A display plugged in or unplugged rebuilds the wall on the debounce the window move already waits out; a rehearsal ignores it, its windows standing for displays that are not here. `DisplayWallTests` (16) + `ProjectionTests.everyDisplayCarriesItsOwnPart` (the per-display placement, sabotage-verified red). Examples `Installation/Unattended` + `Installation/Resuming` + `Installation/Watched` + `Installation/Hours` + `Installation/Fitted` + `Installation/ManyDisplays` (a long canvas spread over every display, and rehearsable on one desk) + `Installation/ManyWindows` (one world through as many windows as are opened, kept in step with no messages at all: every window reads the time of day, so the world is the same function everywhere); `Docs/Output/Installation.md` + `Docs/Core/Sketch.md`; Guide Ch 29 § *Leaving it running* + § *Several windows, one world* + § *Several displays, one machine*.

- **Fabrication export** (`Sources/Ollin/3D/MeshExport.swift` + `MeshPrintCheck.swift` + `ZipWriter.swift`). The write half of the `Mesh` story, the 3D counterpart of the plotter path: **`mesh.write(to:)`** / `data(as:)` / the `Sketch` sugar `saveMesh(_:to:)` emit **`MeshFileFormat`** `.stl` (binary) / `.obj` / `.threeMF`, sized by a declared **`ModelUnit`** (3MF records it, STL/OBJ have nowhere to put it) with `normalized(scale:)` the documented sizing step, plus **`printCheck()`** → **`MeshPrintCheck`** (closed / consistently oriented / inside-out / boundary + non-manifold edge counts / degenerates / size / volume / `problems` in plain words). **Load-bearing:** every format shares **one `FabricationMesh` preparation** (weld → drop degenerates → settle winding → turn z-up), so an STL and a 3MF of one mesh describe the same solid rather than each format's own idea of it; **welding is a precondition, not a nicety** (Ollin's generators are flat-shaded, so an unwelded mesh is **100% boundary edges** and a slicer sees loose triangles, the same discovery the soft-body tier made, pinned by the raw-vs-prepared counterfactual); **winding follows the normals, not the authored order**, by **area-weighted agreement over the whole surface** flipping every triangle together or none (the subdivision-surface rule: `Mesh.extrude` winds inward while shading outward), falling back to **signed volume** only when a mesh carries no normals; the z-up turn is the **rotation** `(x, -z, y)` (a quarter turn about x, determinant +1) so winding and normals ride along untouched; the **STL header must not begin with `solid`** or a sniffing reader takes the binary file for the text form; **OBJ is geometry-only** (a merged corner has no one normal, and writing an averaged one would round off the very edges the merge just joined); **`printCheck()` describes the prepared mesh, never the raw arrays** (on those every generator reads as broken, which is the whole reason the counts differ from `Mesh.triangleCount`); a failing mesh is **written with a note, never refused** (an open surface is a fine thing to draw). 3MF is an OPC package written by **`ZipWriter`** (deflate-when-it-pays else stored, own CRC-32, **fixed 1980 timestamps so output is byte-reproducible**), the write counterpart of `USDZipArchive` and verified against both it and the system `unzip`; the spec's manifold + distinct-corner + counter-clockwise rules are exactly what the shared preparation leaves true. **Deliberate v1 cuts:** no vertex colors / uvs / materials (the 3MF materials extension is unwritten, so no multi-material or full-color print), no ASCII STL, and **no CLI flag** (a sketch draws many meshes, so there is no single "the mesh" for a flag to name). Example `3D/Geometry/Fabrication` (a tunable torus knot whose `sealed` knob opens the tube's ends: the shape barely changes and the report turns red); `MeshExportTests` (20, the weld / winding / z-up treatments each verified red by sabotage, STL and OBJ read back through the shipped loaders, 3MF parsed through the shipped ZIP reader + `XMLDocument`); no pixel snapshot (the output is files, not frames). Guide Ch 29 § *Something you can hold: a 3D print* + the closed-versus-open figure; `Docs/Output/Fabrication.md`.

- **Spatial export** (`Sources/Ollin/3D/SceneExport.swift` + `SceneWriterUSD.swift` + `Sources/Ollin/Export/SpatialExport.swift`). The write half of the USD story, per-*scene* where fabrication is per-*mesh*: **`scene.write(to:)`** / `data(as:metersPerUnit:)` / the `Sketch` sugar `saveScene(_:to:)` emit **`SceneFileFormat`** `.usdz` (package) / `.usda` (text layer), and **`OllinApp.spatialScene(of:frame:)`** / `exportSpatial` / **`--export-usdz <path>`** (`--frame`, `--meters-per-unit`) drive a sketch headlessly and collect the frame's 3D draw calls. **The architecture is one writer with two ways in:** the frame recorder's *output is an ordinary `Scene`* (the same value `loadScene` hands back, so a recorded frame can be read, edited, and redrawn), which is what makes it structurally impossible for the frame path and the file path to drift. **Ollin writes the USD itself** (Model I/O is out of the USD path entirely, and both halves of a writer were already here): a `.usda` text layer plus `ZipWriter`, which gained `stored:`/`alignment:` (defaulted, so the 3MF path is byte-identical) for the spec's zero-compression, 64-byte-aligned, layer-first package. **Every mapping is the stated inverse of the reader's** and the tests hold the two against each other, because a writer that drifts from its reader is wrong in a way nothing else notices: colors sRGB→linear, `focal = aperture / 2tan(fov/2)`, ortho `verticalAperture = height × 10`, spot cone as a **half-angle in degrees**, texture `v → 1 − v`, and a USD matrix's four rows are the simd matrix's four **columns**. **Load-bearing, each a real defect the system validator caught:** a Mesh needs **`subdivisionScheme = "none"`** or every schema-honoring viewer shows a smoothed, shrunken cage; a prim that binds a material must **`prepend apiSchemas = ["MaterialBindingAPI"]`**; a primvar reader's **`varname` is a `string`, not a `token`**. A preview surface has one diffuse slot, so whichever of the mesh's base color and its per-vertex colors is present carries the **composed** product (vertex colors go out as `displayColor` fed to diffuse through a `UsdPrimvarReader_float3`; a texture's tint rides `UsdUVTexture.inputs:scale`) and the other steps aside with a note. **`MeshMaterial` grew the format-expressible finish** (`metallic`/`roughness`/`opacity`/`ior`/`clearcoat`/`clearcoatRoughness`, spec defaults, carried-not-rendered) so a `Scene` can hold what USD can say; a non-PBR finish maps `shininess → roughness` through the **shipped area-light curve** `(2/(s+2))^(1/4)`, and `Drawer.activeLights` was extracted from `makeLighting` so the renderer and the export can't disagree about which lights a frame had. **Nothing is dropped quietly:** 2D drawing, point clouds / GPU particles / raymarched fields (skipped at their own funnels, `isosurface`/`particleSurface` named as the way through), stylized finishes, animation and skinning, and the environment (deliberate, and usually right: a viewer lights a model with its own surroundings, which in AR is the actual room) each note once. Textures package as PNG entries in a `.usdz` at an **anchored relative** path and are noted-and-skipped for a bare `.usda` (one text file has nowhere to put an image). Output is **byte-reproducible**. Example `3D/Geometry/SpatialExport`; `SceneExportTests` (29: round trips through the shipped reader for geometry / materials / textures / cameras / every light kind / transforms, the written-color-is-linear and half-angle checks that a matching pair of mistakes would slip past, package layout, determinism, the 3MF-untouched guard, and **`usdchecker --arkit`** over a package carrying every light kind, a texture, per-vertex colors, and a PBR material, tool-gated like the `usdcat` cross-check); no pixel snapshot (the output is files). Guide Ch 29 § *Something you can walk around: USDZ* + the drawn-versus-reopened figure; `Docs/Output/Spatial.md`.

- **Spatial video** (`Sources/Ollin/Export/SpatialVideoExport.swift` + `Sources/Ollin/3D/Stereo.swift`). The moving counterpart of the USDZ model: **`--export-spatial <path.mov>`** / `OllinApp.exportSpatialVideo` renders a stereo pair per frame and muxes it as **MV-HEVC spatial video**, the format Apple's platforms play with real depth. A sketch declares **`stereoGeometry`** (`StereoGeometry(interocular:convergence:)`, `.automatic`), either number derivable and either overridable per export (`--interocular`, `--convergence`, `--meters-per-unit`, plus the video export's `--frames`/`--seconds`/`--fps`/`--skip`/`--bitrate`/`--quality`); `Camera3D.stereoPair(_:aspect:)` / `stereoEye(_:interocular:convergence:)` / `StereoGeometry.resolved(for:aspect:)` are the public geometry. **Load-bearing:** the pair comes out of **one `performDraw()` rendered twice** with `Drawer.aimStereoEye(_:previous:)` swapping the camera between renders, never two draws (a second draw rolls the rng and steps every sim again, and the artificial-life tier is documented as not frame-exact even at one seed, so the eyes would see different worlds); the repeat render is free because `statefulEncodeIsRepeat` already exists for the live frame grab, and `previous:` moves with the eye or motion blur reads the eye separation itself as a sideways lurch. The convergence lean is **one matrix entry, `[2][0]`, correct for all three projections by two separate routes** (perspective/intrinsic: clip w is the view distance, so a constant NDC shift collapses into it, the asymmetric frustum a parallel rig is defined by, the `jittered` identity; orthographic: no such w, so it needs a depth-proportional shear, which works out to the same value), and `stereoLateral` defaults to 0 so an ordinary camera's projection is **untouched by construction**. The eyes step along the camera's own right axis and stay **parallel** (toeing in leaves unfusable vertical parallax at the corners). The derived spacing is a **stated criterion**: 1% of the frame width at the convergence plane, which for a camera with a vanishing point is also 1% at infinity, the cinema comfort figure; the frame width is read off the built projection's `[0][0]` so an intrinsic lens measures the same way. **Five metadata keys are what make the system call it spatial** (`HasLeft`/`HasRightStereoEyeView`, `HorizontalFieldOfView` in millidegrees, `StereoCameraBaseline` in micrometres, `HorizontalDisparityAdjustment`, which is 0 because convergence is already in the pictures); drop one and `AVAssetPlaybackAssistant` reports `.stereoMultiviewVideo` but **not** `.spatialVideo`, so that near neighbor is not the test. All three of `MVHEVCVideoLayerIDs`/`MVHEVCViewIDs`/`MVHEVCLeftAndRightViewIDs` are written: naming the middle without the last **hangs** the encoder rather than refusing. Buffers must be **IOSurface-backed**, from the receiver's own pool; the write path uses the macOS 26 `TaggedPixelBufferGroupReceiver.appendImmediately`, not the adaptor already deprecated at the deployment floor; settings are built at **frame 0** because the lens and the spacing are only known once `draw()` has set the camera; and the video input is **`expectsMediaDataInRealTime = true`** (the ordinary video export does not need it) because a file-paced *multi-layer* input stops accepting frames at the end of the first ~1 s chunk and never recovers (measured at frame 36 of a 30 fps export: status still `.writing`, error nil, readiness false forever), the same exemption the soundtrack input already takes. Envelope, stated not faked: an accumulating sketch (one persistent surface) and a 2D sketch read flat with a one-time note, and anything the sketch flattened during `draw()` (`project()`, `depth(at:)`, a billboard) lands on the screen plane in both eyes. Sound rides along via the shipped soundtrack path. Example `3D/Geometry/SpatialVideo`; `SpatialVideoTests` (11: the geometry against counterfactual twins, the system's own `.spatialVideo` reading, the metadata numbers, and a wider-eyes-disagree-more probe over both decoded layers; the convergence-lean sabotage is red in three); no pixel snapshot (the output is a file). Mechanism: [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) § *Spatial video*. Guide Ch 29 § *Something you can look into: spatial video*; `Docs/Output/Spatial.md#spatial-video`.

- **Variations & seed exploration.** A sketch is a *generator*, so its seed is first-class: `Sketch.init` rolls **`variation`** (a five-digit `Int`, 1…99_999) and seeds **both** `random` and `noise` from it, so an unseeded run still varies yet always names the number that brings it back (every export's recipe now carries a `seed`). **Load-bearing:** `seed(_:)` *sets* `variation` (it's the one host-navigable seed); `randomSeed`/`noiseSeed` are **partial reseeds that leave `variation` alone** (so the recipe still splits them). Two unseeded exports of a randomness-free sketch differ **only** in the recorded seed (`SVGExportTests` pins body-equality; snapshot pixels unaffected, none re-recorded). Live navigation is **`SketchRunner.restart(variation:)`**, an in-place restart on the *same instance* (reseed → `frameCount = 0` → `resetAccumulation()` → re-run `setup()` next frame), so `@Param` values survive; it must call **`sketch.loop()`**, not just unpause the view — a still `noLoop()` sketch's fresh `noLoop()` is only a *change* (and only re-pauses) if the running state was restored first. The shared `VariationCardView` (core `Live/`) drives it from all four inspector surfaces via `OllinActiveSketch.runner`, and `SketchSession.recordSeed` carries a navigated seed across hot reload the way tuned knobs do (applied in `syncParams` **before** `setup()`, so a sketch that pins its own seed still wins; pinned by `OllinLiveCoding --sessiontest`). Export side: **`--seed N` reseeds before `setup()` on every export path** (one `make()` wrapper in `handleCommandLine`), and **`--export-grid`** / `OllinApp.contactSheet(of:seeds:)` / `exportContactSheet` tile one frame per seed into a labeled proof sheet, with **`--export-sweep`** / `contactSheet(of:sweeping:values:seed:)` the same sheet along one named `@Param` instead (values apply via the hosts' `ParamStored` restore path so a tile matches the dragged knob; **every tile pins one seed** — given, or rolled once and recorded — so the knob is the only thing changing; unknown name → nil + the sketch's actual parameter list on stderr; both sheets share one private tiling core; **the framing is deliberate, don't re-blend it**: seeds = a piece's *identity* (the Variations story, matching the generative-art seed culture — @eaviles keeps `Docs/Core/Variations.md` seed-pure) while the sweep is a *tuning* tool, documented on the Parameters/Export pages) — a **fresh sketch per tile** (no state leak) over **one reused renderer** (`OllinApp.renderImage(of:frame:fps:renderer:)`, factored out of `image(of:)`), `resetAccumulation()` between tiles, tile width floored at 64px, the sheet's own recipe carrying the seed list (`ExportMetadata.sheetRecipe`). Example `Randomness/Variations` (a whole composition per seed — the decisions live in `setup()`); `VariationTests`. `Docs/Core/Variations.md`, `Docs/Output/Export.md`.
- **Record & replay.** A run written down as a **`Take`** (`Sources/Ollin/Core/Take.swift`, versioned Codable JSON: the `variation` seed, per-frame clock samples exactly as the display drove them — live jitter included, every input event and `@Param` change frame-stamped, plus the starting knob values) and played back exactly, to the byte. **Load-bearing:** both halves live on two choke points — every driver (live window, all export loops, benchmark) funnels through `Sketch.advance`, where the player overrides the caller's clock after applying that frame's events and knob changes and the recorder writes down whichever clock applies; and all input enters `Sketch` through its internal handlers, each of which taps the recorder and **gates external calls off while a player is attached**, with the player re-ingesting through the same paths so the hooks (`mousePressed()` et al.) fire again on replay. **The stamp contract:** an event or change stamped `k` was received after frame `k` drew and applies at the advance where pre-increment `frameCount == k` (`frames[k]` is that advance's clock) — recording and playback must share the pre-increment stamp or replays shear by a frame. `Take.install(on:)` must land **before `setup()`** (seed and starting knobs first, so setup builds from them); `--seed` beside `--replay` re-seeds *after* install on purpose (same gestures, different variation; pinned). Windowed: `--record-take <file>` (standalone + OllinLive — the recorder attaches in the runner's first-frame block ahead of `setup()`, autosaves on a **doubling cadence** since a growing take re-encodes whole, value-copy written off-main; quit writes the final file, and a reload or seed-restart ends/restarts the take) and `--replay <file>` (live input gated, keyboard becomes the transport: space pause/start-over, arrows step, shift-arrows jump 30, Home/End; a backward scrub rewinds to 0 and re-simulates, `stepReplayFrame` mirroring `renderImage`'s stateful-GPU stepping — accumulate/feedback frames render offscreen, everything else `stepCompute`s). Headless: `--replay` composes with **every** export flag through the one `make()` wrapper plus the advance override (video-shaped exports default their length to the take's). Determinism scope: time, inputs, knobs, `random`/`noise` replay; live feeds, OSC/MIDI, and `Date()` reads do not (documented). Verified byte-identical through the CLI (replay-export twice, hashes equal; non-replay differs) and `TakeTests` (7: exact trace + hook replay, input gate, knob landing on its frame, past-end advance, seed-override, file round-trip + version refusal, GPU byte-identity; player sabotage verified red). `Docs/Core/Replay.md`.
